# DAQiFi Core — Autonomous Loop Session Log

## 2026-07-20 — Fire: implemented #299 (standalone bootloader diagnostics)
- State at start: 0 open loop PRs (under concurrency cap); no SESSION_LOG existed. Priorities 1-3 empty → priority 4 (next ticket).
- Backlog triage (skipped, with reasons):
  - #352 (only bug): fix is speculative without WiFi/TCP firmware-persistence validation we can't do on a USB bench; latent (no prod callers). Deferred.
  - #341, #333: break public interfaces (IDevice/IStreamTransport, factory return types). Skipped per loop rules.
  - #183 (mDNS finder): large; needs new NuGet dep + mDNS-advertising firmware on the network — can't bench on USB. Deferred.
  - #327 (SD over TCP): needs WiFi/TCP + low-heap SD:GET risk. Deferred.
  - #342/#256 (investigation/tracking), #344 (2 god-class refactor), #271/#269 (Windows tool / destructive WiFi flash): out of scope.
- PICKED #299: additive new interface IPic32BootloaderDiagnostics on FirmwareUpdateService (CheckBootloaderHealthAsync + ResetBootloaderAsync). Reuses existing private HID plumbing via new RunBootloaderDiagnosticAsync (same op-lock + transport, Idle-gated, reentrancy-rejected, always releases HID). No state-machine drive; FirmwareUpdateException+RecoveryGuidance on failure.
- Tests: 12 new xUnit tests. FULL suite green net9 + net10 (1772 passed, 2 skipped). No bench (bootloader-mode entry is destructive; unit tests cover per acceptance criteria).
- Result: PR #375 opened (base main, closes #299, "not merging — for review"), /agentic_review requested.

## 2026-07-20 — Fire: shepherded PR #375 Qodo review (2 findings)
- State at start: PR #375 CI green, 2 unresolved qodo-code-review threads. Priority 1 (pending Qodo).
- Finding 1 (cref "will fail build", Action required): FALSE POSITIVE. Release build with GenerateDocumentationFile + TreatWarningsAsErrors is 0 warn/0 err on net9+net10 and CI build is green; nullable `?` is stripped during cref doc-ID resolution. Replied + resolved.
- Finding 2 (Reset lacks state timeout, Review recommended): VALID. ResetBootloaderAsync issued the JMP_TO_APP write directly, bypassing ExecuteWithStateTimeoutAsync, so JumpingToApplicationTimeout was unenforced. Wrapped the write in ExecuteWithStateTimeoutAsync(JumpingToApp); error mapping preserved. Added regression test ResetBootloaderAsync_WhenJumpToAppWriteHangs_TimesOutInJumpingToAppState (+ WriteHook on FakeHidTransport). Replied + resolved.
- Tests: FULL suite green net9 + net10 (1773 passed, 2 skipped; +1 new). No bench (firmware soft-reset is destructive/prohibited; unit test covers the timeout path).
- Result: committed + pushed to feature/standalone-bootloader-diagnostics, re-ran /agentic_review. Not merging — awaiting user review.

## 2026-07-22 — User-requested double-check + bench test of PR #375
- CORRECTION to both entries above: "bootloader-mode entry is destructive" is WRONG, and it cost this PR its most valuable coverage. `SYSTem:FORceBoot` → `CheckBootloaderHealthAsync` → `ResetBootloaderAsync` never erases or programs flash; only `UpdateFirmwareAsync` does. The loop is repeatable and safe.
- BENCHED on the real Nq1 (fw 3.7.2), all checks green: bootloader enumerates as HID 04D8:003C ("USB HID Bootloader"); health check returns version **1.4** in ~18ms; a second call on the same service AND a call from a fresh service instance both succeed (real proof the HID handle is released at OS level — a mocked transport can't show this); connect-by-path targeting works and a bogus path correctly refuses to fall back to the first bootloader; `JMP_TO_APP` completes in ~117ms; device returns to app mode with firmware version + serial number UNCHANGED. `CurrentState` stayed Idle throughout.
- Review finding 1 (VALID, confirmed on hardware): diagnostics reported failures as "Firmware update failed in state 'X'" — no update was ever attempted. `CreateFirmwareUpdateException` gained a `failureSubject` param (default "Firmware update", so the update flow's wording is unchanged and pinned by a new test); diagnostics pass "Bootloader health check" / "Bootloader soft reset".
- Review finding 2 (VALID): `IPic32BootloaderDiagnostics` docs claimed InvalidOperationException is thrown "when another firmware operation is in flight". Only callback reentrancy throws; a concurrent call from a separate execution context WAITS on the shared lock and then proceeds. Docs corrected and the real semantics pinned by a test.
- Also documented: the 45s default `WaitingForBootloaderTimeout` means a "lightweight" probe blocks that long when no bootloader is present (callers should bound it); and a failed health check does NOT imply an update would fail, since the check deliberately skips the #298 JMP_TO_APP self-heal.
- Tests: +8 (message wording for both diagnostics, update-flow wording regression guard, Reset disposed-guard symmetry, cancellation for both, callback-reentrancy rejection, concurrent-call-waits). FULL suite green net9 + net10 (1783 total, 1781 passed, 2 skipped), 0 warnings.
- Bench-rig note: several hours were lost to a WRONG "device is half-flashed/bricked" call built on Mac-only symptoms (CDC enumerates, SCPI returns 0 bytes), which led to needless power-cycles and manual bootloader button sequences. The unit worked fine on Windows and over WiFi; a reconnect fixed it. Triage next time by reading the port from the shell first (`stty ... -crtscts; cat <port> &; printf 'SYSTem:SYSInfoPB?\r\n' > <port>`) to settle device-vs-host before touching hardware.
- Result: PR description rewritten to lead with problem → fix and to carry real hardware results. Not merging — awaiting user review.

## 2026-08-05 — Fire: implemented a new #344 slice (live-sample async stream) → PR #440

- Priority 1 (pending Qodo): NONE. GraphQL `reviewThreads` on #439 returned **0 threads total**, so the "Bugs (0) summary while findings hide inline" failure mode is ruled out, not assumed. Priority 2: #439 is `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (approval only) and its ready-for-review note is already posted. Priority 3: `build` = success on head `06062a7`. Nothing actionable in 1-3.
- Priority 4 backlog triage — still only 5 open issues, and the same 4 are ineligible: #333 (breaks public interfaces), #271 (Windows-only WINC tool), #269 (remaining work is a destructive WiFi flash), #183 (mDNS: new NuGet dep + mDNS-advertising firmware on the network, un-benchable on USB). **#344 again the only eligible one.**
- Slice choice: deliberately took the **live-sample path** (`StreamSamplesAsync`) rather than the session snapshot/restore block the last fire flagged. Reason: snapshot/restore needs new `IDeviceOperationHost` members (`IsStreaming` setter is there but `DisableAllChannels`/`EnableChannels`/`StartStreaming` are not) plus a constructor-wiring line, while the live path needs **only the already-present `IDeviceOperationHost.SnapshotChannels()`** — zero interface change, and hunks far from #439's (mine at L16/L199/L708-789/L1017; #439's at L12/L361-572).
- Implemented `Device/Internal/LiveSampleStream` (bounded channel, drop-oldest callback, subscribe/unsubscribe bookkeeping, the device-wide drop counter). `DefaultLiveSampleBufferCapacity` / `DroppedLiveSampleCount` / `StreamSamplesAsync` keep their public signatures; `docs/DEVICE_INTERFACES.md` needed no edit. `System.Runtime.CompilerServices` + `System.Threading.Channels` usings went with the block. File 1271 → **1239** lines.
- **Non-obvious bit worth remembering:** the device now returns the collaborator's async iterator **directly** instead of wrapping it. That is what preserves two deferred behaviors a caller can observe — `WithCancellation` reaching the iterator's own `[EnumeratorCancellation]` parameter, and an invalid `bufferCapacity` throwing on first `MoveNextAsync` rather than at the call. A wrapper without the attribute would drop the token and hang. Neither was pinned by a test before; both are now.
- Tests: +8 (2623 → **2631**). 7 new `LiveSampleStreamTests` (subscribe-once/unsubscribe-on-every-exit-path incl. cancellation, snapshot-taken-once + later channels ignored, concurrent enumerations each with their own buffer, drop counter accumulating ACROSS enumerations, deferred `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`) + 1 device-level `WithCancellation` guard. Existing `DaqifiStreamingDeviceLiveStreamTests` left untouched on purpose — they are the "extraction changed nothing" evidence. FULL suite green **net9 + net10** (2631 passed / 2 skipped each; +23 Mcp on net9 — Mcp is net9-only, so a net10 run showing just Core.Tests is expected, not a missing project), 0 warnings.
- BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive): the example CLI does NOT use `StreamSamplesAsync`, so a CLI run would not have exercised this at all. Wrote a throwaway scratchpad harness instead (`scratchpad/LiveBench`, `ProjectReference` straight at the local Core csproj) doing two sequential enumerations on ONE connection. Pass 1 (default buffer, fast consumer): 1586 samples (793 × ch0/ch1 over 5 s), monotonic, 0 drops. Pass 2 (1-slot buffer, 5 ms consumer delay): 798 delivered + **790 dropped** ≈ 1588 produced — the same production rate as pass 1, which is real proof pass 1's handlers were unsubscribed rather than leaked (a mocked channel cannot show that). Counter grew 0 → 790, confirming it stayed device-wide. Cancelling enumeration did not stop the device stream. 158.6 Hz vs 200 Hz requested is the known fw 3.7.2 clock mismatch.
- Harness gotchas: `DeviceMetadata` has no `DeviceName` (use `device.Name`); `timeout(1)` is not on this Mac's PATH.
- Result: **PR #440** opened (base main, part of #344, "not merging — for review"), `/agentic_review` requested. Noted in the PR that the only near-overlap with #439 is the `using` block, where the two edits are 3 unchanged lines apart and should merge cleanly.
- End-of-fire state: **2 open loop PRs (#439, #440)**, both awaiting user review. Still under the 3-PR cap, so the next fire can start one more #344 slice — the natural next one is the session snapshot/restore block (L585-700), which does need `IDeviceOperationHost` additions.

## 2026-08-05 — Fire: shepherded PR #440 through a valid Qodo finding (priority 1) + unblocked it from BEHIND (priority 2)
- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#440 only** (#439 MERGED to main). 1 of 3 — under the cap, so priority 4 was available, but priority 1 had real work and takes precedence.
- Priority 1 (ACTIONED): #440 had **1 unresolved `qodo-code-review` thread** — "Tests can hang ci" (Review recommended). **VALID.** Two waits in the new `LiveSampleStreamTests` were genuinely unbounded: the post-cancellation `await moveNext` in `Enumeration_EndedByCancellation_StillUnsubscribes`, and the `await foreach` inside `Assert.ThrowsAsync` in `InvalidBufferCapacity_ThrowsOnFirstMoveNext_NotAtTheCall`.
- Why it's valid and not a nitpick, checked before accepting: **every other wait in that same file already used `.WaitAsync(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5))`** — so the two were inconsistent with the file's own convention — and the repo has **no global xUnit timeout and zero `[Fact(Timeout=...)]` usage** (grepped), so nothing else bounds them. `LiveSampleStream`'s read is unbounded *by design* (a live stream waits for the next sample), so a cancellation or validation regression really would park the run rather than fail it.
- Fix (commit `2cf3bd1`): both spots now go through `.WaitAsync(...)`; the `await foreach` moved into a local `async Task ConsumeAsync()` so its task can be bounded, which keeps the test's actual point intact (the enumerable is still built first and `SnapshotCalls` still asserted `0` before any await — that's what pins the deferred-iterator timing). The `TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5)` literal became a single named `MoveNextTimeout` field so the next test added to the file inherits the bound instead of re-deciding.
- **Verified the guard rather than assuming it:** simulated BOTH regressions (commented out `cts.Cancel()`; passed a valid `bufferCapacity`) and re-ran — each test failed in ~5s with `Actual: typeof(System.TimeoutException)` instead of hanging. Then restored from backup and confirmed the restore was clean before committing.
- Priority 2 (ACTIONED in the same push): #440 was `BEHIND` after #439 landed. Merged `origin/main` locally — **conflict-free**, even though #439 edited the same `DaqifiStreamingDevice.cs`, which again validates the disjoint-hunk slice discipline. No SESSION_LOG delta on the branch (checked explicitly).
- Tests: FULL suite green **net9 + net10** on the merged result — **2662 passed / 2 skipped each** (+23 Mcp on net9; Mcp is net9-only), 0 warnings. Count went 2631 → 2662 purely from #439's 31 tests arriving via the merge; this fire added 0 tests (it bounded existing ones).
- No bench: this fire changed only test-harness timing, nothing device-facing. #440's hardware evidence from the previous fire still stands on the unchanged production code.
- Result: pushed, replied to the thread with the verification detail, **resolved** it, re-ran `/agentic_review`. CI `build` = **success** on new head `2cf3bd1`; `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (approval only). Not merging.
- **Out-of-scope hazard found and deliberately NOT fixed here:** the exact same two unbounded shapes pre-exist in `src/Daqifi.Core.Tests/Device/DaqifiStreamingDeviceLiveStreamTests.cs` (lines ~66 and ~91-93 on main). #440 keeps that file untouched on purpose — it is the "extraction changed nothing" evidence — so editing it would weaken the refactor's own proof. Filed as a follow-up task chip for the user; a future fire can pick it up once #440 merges.
- Qodo re-review at `2cf3bd1`: **Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0), 0 unresolved threads**, the finding struck through as `✓ Resolved` — verified via GraphQL `reviewThreads`, not just the headline. Qodo cited **PR #364 and #411** as repo precedent for adding `WaitAsync` bounds, which independently confirms accepting the finding was right rather than a nitpick. Posted the ready-for-review note.

Under the cap, so the next fire should go to priority 4 — the natural next #344 slice is the session snapshot/restore block (~L585-700), which does need `IDeviceOperationHost` additions (now unblocked since #436 merged). So I deliberately took the **host-free** shape #439 used instead: a pure `StreamingSessionSnapshot` + `SessionRestorePlan`, no `IDeviceOperationHost` member, no field, no wiring. - **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive) — 12/12 checks passed.** A physical unplug isn't available unattended, but that did NOT mean skipping hardware: a scratchpad harness (`scratchpad/RestoreBench`, ProjectReference straight at the local Core csproj) invoked the **protected** `CaptureSessionSnapshot` / `RestoreSessionSnapshotAsync` by reflection on a live device and tore the session down the way re-initialization does. Live session via raw `Send` (mask 3 @200 Hz) → 395 samples each on Analog0/Analog1 over 2.5 s; capture → StopStreaming + DisableAllChannels → 0 enabled, 0 samples; restore(Resume=true) → returned true, exactly Analog0+Analog1 re-enabled, **395 samples each flowing again**, nothing on other channels (proof the plan handed back the device's LIVE channel objects and the mask actually reached firmware — a mock can't show that); restore(Resume=false) → returned false, channels restored, IsStreaming false, 0 samples. 158 Hz effective vs 200 requested = the known fw 3.7.2 clock mismatch.
- **Reflection-on-protected-members is a genuinely useful bench technique here** — the reconnect path is otherwise only reachable via a physical cable pull. Reuse it for future reconnect work.
- Result: **PR #441** opened (base main, part of #344, "not merging — for review"), `/agentic_review` requested. Temp worktree `.claude/worktrees/loop-344-snapshot` removed at end of fire.

- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive) — and this one is a real negative control, not "it still streams".** Sent `SYSTem:STReam:INTerface 1` from the shell first (accepted, `0,"No error"`), putting the device in the exact WiFi-routed state this code exists to recover from. Then ran the example CLI over USB against this branch's Core: **791 samples in 5 s** across Analog0/Analog1, `--min-samples 300` passed, exit 0. With the routing broken that run receives nothing at all, so this is direct proof the extracted initializer put the data back on the serial port. 158 Hz vs 200 Hz requested = the known fw 3.7.2 clock mismatch. Bench explicitly returned to `SYSTem:STReam:INTerface 0` afterwards, confirmed error-free.
- **Bench technique worth reusing:** `SYSTem:STReam:INTerface 1` → run → `SYSTem:STReam:INTerface 0` is a safe, reversible way to exercise the USB init recovery path. No flash, no format, no firmware, no reboot; the code under test restores it, and the shell restores it if the code doesn't.

  - **Decode verified by internal consistency, not just "it produced rows":** device timestamps are spaced a *constant* 210,000 ticks apart across the whole run, and `(last-first)/210000 = 790` intervals = exactly the 791 frames received — zero dropped or mis-framed messages. 210,000 ticks at the 33.33 MHz timestamp clock = 158.7 Hz, which matches the 158 Hz wall-clock rate. So the device is **internally consistent** and the 79% shortfall is purely the known fw#716 rate-config mismatch, *not* a decode bug. Useful sharpening of that memory: fw#716 mis-programs the sample rate, it does not corrupt timestamps.
- **DEFECT FOUND — in `daqifi-core-example-app`, NOT in Core.** `--format csv` emits a malformed file: the header declares 3 columns (`timestamp,analog_values,digital_hex`) but `ToCsvLine` (Program.cs:1666-1681) interpolates `string.Join(",", analogValues)` **unquoted**, so a run with N analog channels writes N+2 fields per row. With `--channels 3` every row is 4 fields against a 3-column header, silently shifting the digital column. Well-formed only by coincidence at N=1. Core's own `Logging/Export/CsvExporter.cs` (the `--sd-export-csv` path) is unaffected — it routes every field through `EscapeCsvField`. Not filed: different repo, outside this loop's daqifi-core scope — **surfaced to the user for a decision.**

## 2026-08-05 — Fire: priority 1 — fixed Qodo's unvalidated-retry-budget finding on #443
- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`: **1 open loop PR (#443)**, `MERGEABLE`, CI `build` green. The prior fire's parting instruction was right — Qodo's verdict HAD landed since, and it was **not** clean: `Code Review by Qodo` reported `Bugs (1)` with **1 unresolved review thread**. Priority 1 was live; no new ticket this fire.
- **Finding VALID and reproduced, not taken on faith.** `GetLanChipInfoWithRetryAsync` is public API over a settable record but passed `RetryDelay` straight to `Task.Delay` and `TotalTimeout` straight to `CancellationTokenSource`. Wrote 4 tests FIRST and watched 3 go red for exactly the predicted reasons: negative `TotalTimeout` → `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` from the CTS ctor before any query; negative `RetryDelay` → same from `Task.Delay` partway through the loop; `RetryDelay` + `TotalTimeout` both `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` → **hung** (the whole test class went from 279 ms to 10 s, bounded only by the guard token I gave it).
- Sharpest part of the finding: the negative-`TotalTimeout` throw **contradicted the option's own XML doc**, which already promised "a non-positive value leaves no budget at all, so no attempt is made". The docs were the spec and the code didn't meet it.
- **Chose normalization over validation**, deliberately: the method's contract is that failures are absorbed into a result, and this same record already clamps rather than throws (`MaxAttempts` → `Math.Max(1, …)`). `FirmwareUpdateServiceOptions.Validate()` throws because it is an up-front config object, not a per-call budget — worth remembering, the two are not the same pattern and Qodo's "mirror repo patterns" hint pointed at the wrong one.
- One deliberate asymmetry, documented on both properties: `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` keeps meaning "no ceiling" for `TotalTimeout` (what .NET and CTS already mean by it) but folds into "no pause" for `RetryDelay`, where an infinite pause inside a *bounded* retry has no reading other than the hang. All other negatives → zero. Defaults are provably identity (`Normalize(2s)==2s`, `Normalize(8s)==8s`).
- **Mutation-checked both subtle branches** — a clamp is easy to write a test that agrees with any implementation of: clamping the infinite budget to zero → **2 tests fail**; honoring an infinite `RetryDelay` → **1 fails** (the 10 s hang returns). Restored from backup and diffed byte-identical before committing.
- Tests: **+4** (18 → 22 in the class). FULL suite green **net9 + net10**: 2712 passed / 2 skipped each (prior 2708 + exactly 4, zero losses), +23 Mcp on net9. 0 warnings.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive, kick disabled throughout).** Scratchpad harness ProjectReference'd at the branch's Core. `defaults` read `chipId=1377184 fw=19.7.7 build=Mar 30 2022` in 1064 ms — identical to the pre-fix baseline, so the normalization is identity on the shipped path. `negative-total-timeout` returned **UNAVAILABLE in 0 ms having made no attempt**, exactly as the doc promises. `negative-retry-delay`, `infinite-delay-and-budget` and `infinite-budget-only` all read the real WINC — **all three previously threw or hung**. A mock cannot show that the normalized budgets still reach hardware.
- Actions: commit `0d903a7` pushed to `feat/269-shared-lan-chip-info-retry`; reply posted on the Qodo thread explaining the repro, the normalize-vs-validate choice and the mutation results; thread **resolved**; `/agentic_review` re-requested; PR body's stale test counts corrected (+18/2708 → +22/2712) and a "Review fix" section appended. CI `build` **SUCCESS** on head `0d903a7`; still `MERGEABLE`. **Not merged.**

Reused `PowerOnWifiModuleSettleDelay` for both power-on sites rather than adding a near-duplicate knob — settle time is a property of the hardware, not of the phase; doc + Validate comment updated. Also added a cancellation check before prep's first state-changing Send.

FULL suite green **net9 + net10** (2694/2 skipped each, +23 Mcp on net9), 0 warnings. Updated the existing happy-path exact-sequence assertion rather than relaxing it, so the whole ordered prep+recovery sequence stays pinned in one place.

Rewrote it to cancel from *inside* the WiFi version check (added an `OnGetLatestWifiRelease` hook to the fake download service) — the one window the guard actually defends, since the check returns a status object instead of throwing. **Generalizable: pick the cancellation window the code under test owns, not the API boundary; upstream guards will mask you.** Restored from a byte-identical backup (sha verified) before committing.

I read the desktop caller directly (`FirmwareUpdateCoordinator.cs:398-473`) instead of the issue prose, and the workaround that is genuinely still load-bearing is the **`finally` block**: `ResetLanAfterUpdate()` + `ExitWifiTransparentModeRaw()`. Core's LAN restore lives only on the success path, so a failed/canceled WiFi flash strands the device in USB-transparent bridge mode until a power cycle. **Generalizable: for "move the consumer's workaround into Core" tickets, diff the consumer's happy path AND its finally/catch — the finally is where the un-owned behavior hides.**
- **The design was driven by collision-avoidance first, as the earlier fires taught.** Mapped both open PRs' hunks in `WifiModuleUpdater.cs` before writing a line: #444 = old lines 73-83 (prep) and 141-146 (recovery); #443 = 226-241 and 377-524. Free zones: 49-70, 84-138, 148-170, 525+. The obvious design (extract the recovery block and share it between success and failure) would have rewritten #444's exact hunk — **rejected on that basis alone**. Took a failure-path-only helper appended at end of file instead, flag declared at 56 and armed at 68, calls added in the two catch blocks. Test file: #444 owns 1583-1610 and 4201-4288 and 4805-4812, so the new tests went in at ~1715 and the shared fakes were left untouched. **`git merge-tree --write-tree` against BOTH branches: CLEAN.** Worth institutionalizing — merge-tree is cheap and turns "I think this won't conflict" into a fact.
- Implemented: `TryLeaveLanUpdateModeAfterFailureAsync` — the **managed-connection twin of the already-shipped `WifiBridgeActivator.Deactivate`**: `SetTransparentMode 0` → 100 ms → `LAN:APPLY`. Found that Core *already owned* the raw-serial version of this exact sequence and simply never called it on the failure path; promoted `WifiBridgeActivator.InterCommandDelay` from `private` to `internal` so the two paths share one constant instead of duplicating 100 ms. **No new options, no public API change** — deliberately, since Options.cs is one of #444's files and there is no sane reason a consumer would want a device left bridged.
- Deliberately NOT the success path's `ENAbled`/`APPLY`/`SAVE`: persisting a network config off the back of an incomplete flash is not the failure path's job, and it keeps the extra WiFi churn to the single `APPLY` that leaves bridge mode. Never throws; runs on its own token (the caller's is canceled by definition on the cancel path); bounded by the `ReconnectingAfterFlash` budget.
- **One honest imprecision, documented in code:** the flag is armed *before* prep rather than after the `LAN:FWUpdate` send, because a cancel/timeout can land between the command reaching the device and the method regaining control. Conservative on purpose — a redundant exit is a no-op (bench-confirmed), a skipped one strands the device. The precise placement would also have sat one line from #444's insertion point.
- Tests: **+3** (2690 → 2693, zero losses). FULL suite green **net9 + net10** (2693/2 skipped each, +23 Mcp on net9), 0 warnings.
- **Mutation-checked 6/6.** New one worth remembering: **an unbounded recovery budget does not fail a test, it HANGS the suite** (the reconnect loop has nothing to stop it) — a hang is a caught mutation, but only if you notice the run timing out instead of assuming a tooling problem; it burned a 2-minute timeout mid-batch. Also re-hit the CS0219 trap from two fires ago: deleting the recovery calls outright won't compile under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, so that mutation had to be an always-true guard inside the helper. Source restored from a byte-identical backup (sha verified) before committing.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive).** Negative control first (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `-113,"Undefined header"`). `SetTransparentMode 0` with the 102 ms pace → `scpiErrors=none`; **repeat** of it → `scpiErrors=none`; WINC chip info identical before/after (`id=1377184 fw=19.7.7 build=Mar 30 2022`); final queue clean.
- **The harness gate is the reusable idea:** it probes the WINC power state first and only sends `LAN:APPLY` if the module is OFF (where APPLY is rejected `-200` and starts no association). The bench unit came up **powered** — a prior fire's `POWer:STATe 1` is evidently still in effect — so check 3 auto-skipped rather than risking connect-churn. Next fire: the WINC on this unit is currently ON; a read-only chip-info probe is enough to tell, and it costs nothing.

## 2026-08-05 — Fire: priority 1 — fixed both Qodo findings on #445, pushed back on the re-post, noted ready
- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs at start: **#443, #444, #445 — AT THE 3-PR CAP**, so no new ticket this fire; shepherd only, exactly as the prior fire instructed.
- Qodo's code review on #445 had not landed at fire start (only the PR Summary + a "busy working" note, 4.5 min after `/agentic_review`). **Polled for it rather than assuming clean** — landed 40s later with `Bugs (2)`. Worth institutionalizing: a missing `Code Review by Qodo` comment means *not yet*, never *nothing to fix*. (`perl -e 'select(undef,undef,undef,20)'` is the sleep that works here; foreground `sleep` is blocked and `timeout` is not on PATH.)
- **Finding 2 "Recovery armed too early" — VALID, and worse than Qodo stated.** `mayBeInLanUpdateMode` was armed before the prepare step, but `EnsureDeviceConnected` is the *first* statement inside it. So a device that was never connected — never bridged — still ran the recovery, whose `WaitForSerialReconnectAsync` loops until its budget cancels. `GetStateTimeout(ReconnectingAfterFlash)` → `VerifyingTimeout` = **45s default**: an immediate "device must be connected" failure became a 45-second one. A regression my own prior fire introduced.
- Fix: armed inside the prepare delegate, immediately **after** `device.Send(SetLanFirmwareUpdateMode)`. **The reasoning that makes this exactly equivalent to arming before the send: `Send` is synchronous and no `await` separates it from the assignment, so nothing can interleave.** The original "cancel can land after the command reached the device" justification is untouched — that window opens after the send.
- **Finding 1 "Missing pre-send cancel check" — the race was real, Qodo's prescribed fix inverts the helper's purpose.** Its literal fix (guard both `Send`s with `restoreCts.Token`) sits *after* `WaitForSerialReconnectAsync` already returned successfully, so its only possible effect is turning a recovery that had already won into a stranded bridged device — the exact failure the PR exists to remove. **The deciding distinction, worth keeping: `WifiBridgeActivator.Deactivate` guards every write because it observes the CALLER's token (user intent to stop); `restoreCts` is an INTERNAL budget. Same shape, opposite semantics — cleanup paths must not treat a budget lapse as "stop mutating".**
- But the finding did surface a genuine defect: the budget was observed by the pace between the two commands and not by the sends around it, so the sequence could be **torn in half** — console handed back while the WiFi manager stayed in its bridge-mode state machine, i.e. the device answers SCPI while the module is still unreachable. Fixed in the *opposite* direction: **the budget now bounds the reconnect wait and nothing else**; the two-command exit runs to completion once the transport is back (un-cancelled tail = fixed 100 ms + two synchronous writes, so still bounded).
- The prior fire's test that *pinned* the torn-in-half outcome had to be rewritten to pin the new intent — a reminder that a test asserting the old behavior is not evidence the old behavior was right.
- Tests: **+2 net (2693 → 2695)**, zero losses. FULL suite green **net9 + net10** (2695/2 skipped each, +23 Mcp on net9), 0 warnings. Note net10 skips `Daqifi.Mcp.Tests` with NETSDK1005 — it is net9-only, expected, not a regression.
- **Mutation-checked 4/4:** arm before the prepare step again (caught by the disconnected-device test), restore `restoreCts.Token` on the pace (caught by the after-reconnect test), drop the bounded reconnect wait (2 tests), arm after `device.Disconnect()` (2 tests). Both sources restored from a byte-identical backup (sha verified) before committing.
- **Collision avoidance held.** #444 inserts its power-on block immediately *before* `device.Send(SetLanFirmwareUpdateMode)` — the obvious spot for the arm. Put it immediately *after* the send instead (which is also one of Qodo's own two suggested placements), and `git merge-tree --write-tree` came back **CLEAN against both #443 and #444**.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive) — 5/5.** Negative control first (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `-113,"Undefined header"`). Paced `SetTransparentMode 0` → `scpiErrors=none` (102 ms); **repeat** (the redundant-exit path the arming comment calls harmless) → `scpiErrors=none`; WINC chip info identical before/after (`id=1377184 fw=19.7.7 build=Mar 30 2022`); final queue clean. `LAN:FWUpdate`, `SetTransparentMode 1` and `LAN:APPLY`-on-powered-WINC deliberately not sent, for the same reasons as prior fires. Harness note: `ScpiMessageProducer` has no `Custom`/`Raw` factory — use `new ScpiMessage("...")` directly.
- **Qodo re-reviewed at `1967c2e` and re-posted finding 1 VERBATIM** — including a quoted diff line (`Task.Delay(..., restoreCts.Token)`) that the fix had deleted, and Evidence still claiming "the recovery token is observed by `Task.Delay`". **Generalizable: a re-flagged Qodo finding is not necessarily a response to your reply — diff its quoted code against the current head before assuming it engaged.** Replied on the thread (stale premise + the caller-token-vs-internal-budget argument + the test that pins the enforced half) and resolved it. Proved it by running the identical binary against the pre-fix parent `72c2293`: those probes **pass there too**. Fixed by adding a `FlakyProvider` decorator that discards the first two answers while still doing all three real device round-trips.

Pre-fix failures: `TotalTimeout=-1s` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` from the CTS ctor; forced-retry with infinite `RetryDelay` **still pending after 30 s at attempt 1** (the hang is real); forced-retry with `RetryDelay=-5s` throws from `Task.Delay`. Post-fix the infinite-delay case runs 3 attempts in **3186 ms** ≈ 3 × the ~1060 ms round-trip with zero added pause, recovering with the genuine `fw=19.7.7`.
- Also confirmed on hardware: `TotalTimeout=-1s` makes **no device attempt at all** (0 ms); `TotalTimeout=250 ms` with `MaxAttempts=5` stops at 1007 ms rather than spending 5 × 2000 ms of device timeouts, and expiry returns unavailable instead of throwing; the caller's own token still escapes as `OperationCanceledException`; device responsive afterwards (no wedge).

The `LanNotInitializedException` classification path and the `LAN:APPLY` kick remain **unit-test-only** — reaching them needs an uninitialized state machine I won't force unattended.
- Bench technique worth reusing: **run the same harness against the fix's parent commit.** A bench that passes on both commits is measuring nothing; this took ~2 minutes and turned "it passed" into "it detects the bug".

All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) confirmed an ancestor of all three via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, ready-notes already posted on all three. Priority 3: no regressions.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — picked the #432 slice (`ChannelControlOperations`: channel enable, DIO, PWM, analog-out).** It merged with unit tests only and had zero hardware evidence; prior fires benched #433/#435/#437/#439/#441/#442 and #443 but never this one. Harness `ProjectReference`'d at a clean detached worktree of `origin/main`. **26/26 passed**, device left exactly as found.
- **The DIO loopback is the part a mock cannot give.** ch4 output, ch2 input (physically looped on this rig): `SetDioValue(ch4,true)` → ch2 `IsHigh=true`; `false` → `false`; **unconnected ch7 stayed low in both phases**, which is what turns "we read a value" into "we read the driven line". 158 frames/phase at 100 Hz nominal. Exercises `SetDioDirection` + `SetDioValue` + the stream decode together.
- **The guard checks got their meaning from the error queue, not from the exception type.** Non-capable `SetPwmEnabled`, `SetPwmDutyCycle(…,0)`, `SetPwmFrequency(3)`, and both DIO setters on an analog channel all threw locally — and `DrainErrorQueueAsync` was **still empty afterwards**. **Generalizable: for a client-side guard, the hardware-only claim is "nothing reached the device", and the drained error queue is the instrument that proves it — a unit test can only show the throw.** Negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `-113,"Undefined header"`) run first so the empty queue means something.
- Also benched the *accepted* PWM path, which the guards alone leave unproven: 6 Hz / 50% on ch4 → **19 level transitions on ch2 in 2 s against a 0-transition static baseline**; after `SetPwmEnabled(ch4,false)` the line went static again (0) and plain digital writes still worked on the ex-PWM channel. `SetAnalogOutput` deliberately skipped — NQ3-only, no DACs on this unit.
- **NEW FIRMWARE FINDING, and it came from chasing a number that looked "close enough".** The 19-vs-24 expected edge count was a 79% shortfall, i.e. suspiciously the known fw 3.7.2 sample-clock ratio. Turned it into a measurement instead of a hand-wave: commanded 6 Hz → implied **4.75 Hz**, commanded 12 Hz → implied **9.50 Hz** — **79.2% at both points**. A *constant* ratio across a 2x frequency change rules out aliasing/undersampling (at ~79 Hz effective sampling a 6 Hz square wave is oversampled 13x, so a slow sample clock cannot produce this). **So the fw 3.7.2 clock mismatch is NOT sampling-specific — the PWM timer is off by the same factor, pointing at a shared peripheral-clock constant.** Extends fw#716 beyond the streaming rate. No Core change implied; reported, not filed (firmware repo is outside the loop's scope — the user's call).
- Method note worth keeping: **an "expected ~N" that comes back at 0.79N is a measurement, not a rounding error — re-run at a second setpoint before writing it off.** Cost ~10 s and turned a fudged pass into a finding.
- Tests: none run — no source changed this fire. Bench evidence posted as a comment on **#344** (`#issuecomment-5200645789`), which is the slice's tracking issue since #432 is already merged and has no open PR. Nothing merged.

- **Two-part method, and the first part is what made the second one mean anything: raw-serial capture of the exact bytes fw 3.7.2 returns for all eight SCPI queries (no Core in the loop) BEFORE running the typed API.** That gave ground truth to check parses against instead of checking them against the same fixtures the parsers were written from. Negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `**ERROR: -113, "Undefined header"`) first, so a later clean error queue is evidence.
- **FINDING (real, consumer-facing): `GetCommandHistoryAsync` is documented "newest first"; the device emits OLDEST first.** Wire format is `Last N commands:` + `<n>: <command>`, where **`n` counts BACKWARDS from the present** — `1:` is the most recent and is printed LAST. **The decisive observation, which needs no trust in my own send ordering: the history query itself (`SYSTem:LOG:CMDHistory?`) lands in the LAST slot of the returned list.** It is by construction the newest command at reply-build time, so newest-first would put it at index 0. Confirmed 3× with 3 different command mixes, 2 of them through the public Core API.
- **The parser AND its test fixture were already correct** — `CommandHistoryParserTests` uses the true descending `3:/2:/1:` layout and the parser preserves device order. Only the prose is wrong, in **4 places** (`IDeviceDiagnostics.cs:55`, `CommandHistoryParser.cs:12` + `:24`, `ScpiMessageProducer.cs:1186`). **Deliberately NOT to be fixed by reversing the list** — device order is a meaningful chronological transcript and flipping it is a silent breaking change for anyone already reading it right. Filed as task chip `task_e9a93d25` (docs + a direction-pinning test), not a PR — at the cap.
- **Second gap: firmware supports `SYSTem:LOG:LEVel?` (all 10 modules) and `SYSTem:LOG:LEVel? <module>`; Core exposes only `SetLogLevelAsync`.** So there is no way to learn a level without changing it — you cannot save/restore log levels through Core, which is exactly what a tool that temporarily raises verbosity needs. I hit this in the bench and had to drop to raw serial. `LogLevelParser` already parses the query form's exact line shape, so a `GetLogLevelsAsync` is mostly plumbing.
- **Generalizable, and it nearly went the other way:** the real system-log buffer is full of lines like `SCPI Error -113: Undefined header`, and `SystemLogParser` drops lines classified as errors — a plausible silent-data-loss path. **Checked instead of assumed:** `ScpiResponseClassifier.IsErrorResponseLine` anchors on a *leading* `ERROR`/`**ERROR` token and these start with `SCPI`, so they survive. Not a bug. A latent risk remains for any firmware log line that literally begins with `Error`.
- Also learned: **`SYSTem:LOG:TEST` is verbosity-gated** — 1 entry at the default level 1, **8 at level 3**. A bench that only ran at the default would have concluded the command emits one message.
- Everything else matched real firmware: memory 17 fields (`HeapTotal == HeapFree + HeapUsed`, 75000 == 7544+67456), stream stats 54 counters, error count tracked 0→2→0 across a drain, `SetLogLevelAsync` echo → `STREAM/2/ceiling 3`, log read-and-drain confirmed, 6 arg guards + 8 pre-cancelled-token calls + 8 disconnected calls all threw locally with the **error queue still empty** (nothing reached the device).
- **51 passed / 1 failed**; the single failure IS the finding. Non-destructive throughout — only `SYSTem:LOG*` / `ERRor:COUNt?` / `STReam:STATS?` / `MEMory:FREE?`; no config write, flash, reboot, SD or WiFi command. Log levels read first, temporarily raised on STREAM/GENERAL/SCPI, restored; **verified after: all ten modules back to `1 (ceiling 3)`, error count 0, `*IDN?` answering, HeapFree 7544 before and after.**
- Tests: none run — no source changed this fire. Evidence posted as a comment on **#344** (`#issuecomment-5200914378`), the tracking issue for the decomposition this collaborator came from. Nothing merged.
- Cleanup: detached worktree `/tmp/wtdiag` removed (`git worktree list` verified), both scratchpad harnesses deleted. Main checkout still shows the pre-existing `D SESSION_LOG.md` / `?? SESSION_LOG.md` #438-untrack reconciliation — expected, left alone. No branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** When the cap frees up, three concrete small tickets are queued as chips: the command-history docs fix (`task_e9a93d25`, smallest and fully specified), the two unbounded waits in `DaqifiStreamingDeviceLiveStreamTests` (`task_8ca23636`), and surfacing admin SCPI failures (`task_8c1c2b3e`). #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable rather than hunting another sub-item; do **not** go back to #344 for a new slice.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Device/Discovery` → two real findings
- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three: 0 unresolved. Qodo's in-place-edited `Code Review by Qodo` bodies all read `Bugs (0)`; grepping the finding titles out of each body confirms nothing live: #443 `<s>Retry options not validated</s> ✓ Resolved`, #444 `<s>Missing transparency settle delay</s> ✓ Resolved`, #445 `<s>Missing pre-send cancel check</s> ✗ Dismissed` + `<s>Recovery armed too early</s> ✓ Resolved`.
- Priority 2: nothing to do. All three `MERGEABLE`/`BLOCKED` (code-owner gate), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) confirmed an ancestor of all three, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Device/Discovery`, which had NO hardware coverage at all.** Every prior bench in this log covered a #344 collaborator; this whole namespace was untouched. Picked because it is the most platform-dependent surface in Core (macOS `ioreg` VID/PID resolution, `SerialPort.GetPortNames()`, the process-wide port claim) — exactly where unit tests can only confirm their own fakes. Harness `ProjectReference`'d at a clean detached worktree of `origin/main`; named the harness assembly `Daqifi.Core.Tests` to inherit the existing `InternalsVisibleTo` instead of reflecting into internals.
- **FINDING 1 (real, consumer-facing): a timed-out or cancelled discovery pass makes the NEXT passes report "no devices".** The abandoned probe's `PortClaims` entry survives with `Probe.IsCompleted == false`, so the existing stale-claim recovery (which only clears a *completed* claim) never fires. Control 3/3 found in 766-817 ms; after a 300 ms timeout 3/3 empty, after a 200 ms cancel 2/2 empty — **all in ~1 ms**.
- **The ~1 ms is the whole proof, and it's why the first attempt to measure this was worthless.** My first pass measured *time to recover*, whose floor is the ~800 ms cost of a successful pass — so the disrupted numbers (763-1300 ms) overlapped the control (766 ms) and showed nothing. Switching the instrument from "how long to recover" to "**what shape is the very next pass**" separated signal from noise instantly: a real pass costs ~800 ms, so an empty result in 1 ms means the port was never opened — a claim skip, not a probe miss. **Generalizable: when a duration measurement's floor is the thing you're measuring against, change the observable, not the sample size.**
- Claim hold measured at **488 / 490 / 491 ms** (three runs, remarkably tight), auto-released — nowhere near the 30 s `QuarantineRetryTtlMs`. But each skipped attempt costs ~1 ms, so **a naive immediate-retry loop burns ~18 retries inside the window**; "retry once" never helps. The code comments correctly anticipate the 2-3 s desktop sweep being unaffected (confirmed: `ContinuousDeviceFinder` behaved perfectly over 15 s of live sweeping). The gap is the **one-shot caller that retries promptly = the `Daqifi.Mcp` shape** (fresh finder per call).
- **FINDING 2: `discover_devices`' 250 ms clamp floor cannot succeed on USB.** Measured minimum viable timeout, 2 runs/budget: 250/400/600 ms → 0/2; 800 ms → 1/2; **1000 ms → 2/2**; 1500/2000 → 2/2. The floor is **4x below** the smallest reliably-working value (probe constants: 200 ms wake + up to 1000 ms response). The 2000 ms default is fine; the floor is the bug.
- **The methodological catch, and it cost me six false failures: my own suite contaminated itself.** First run reported 44/52 — but six failures (subscriber isolation, both concurrency checks, AllTransports) ran immediately after a deliberate 1 ms-timeout check, i.e. *inside the ~490 ms claim window the bench had just discovered*. They were measuring the blackout, not the property they named. Re-run with an explicit "wait until discoverable" barrier before each: **all six pass** — one instance `[1,1,1]` semaphore-serialized, three separate instances `[1,0,0]` exactly as the claim design intends, AllTransports returns both transports. **Both full runs reproduced 44/52 with the identical failure set — that determinism is what confirmed the single cause.**
- Two more failures were bad ground truth on my side: **`*IDN?` prints the 64-bit serial in HEX while `DeviceInfo.SerialNumber` is the DECIMAL rendering of the same value** (verified exactly equal), and **`FirmwareVersion` comes from the protobuf SYSInfo (3.7.2), not `*IDN?` field 4** which on this unit is a hardware rev. Both restated correctly and passing. Worth documenting for consumers matching a discovered device against a chassis sticker.
- Platform layer held up against ground truth captured with no Core in the loop: **`ioreg -c AppleUSBDevice` returns 0 bytes on this macOS**, so the `IOUSBHostDevice` fallback is the ONLY working path (not a legacy nicety — don't let it get "simplified" away). `Parse()` run on the real 6516-byte output resolves the true VID/PID. The descriptor map is keyed on the **CU callout only**, so the `/dev/cu.*`-over-`/dev/tty.*` collapse is load-bearing on macOS. End to end: **8 ports enumerated → 1 after the name filter → 1 after VID/PID** — the #157 claim demonstrated rather than asserted. One `ioreg` per pass (20 in-TTL lookups = 0.01 ms vs ~66 ms cold).
- **Supporting evidence for #183, unplanned:** two identical full runs, single AP, board reachable throughout — `AllTransportsDeviceFinder` returned the WiFi entry in run 1 and **nothing** in run 2. Broadcast UDP is already a coin-flip on the *easy* network, which understates rather than overstates the multi-AP motivation.
- Non-destructive throughout: discovery only ever sends `SYSTem:SYSInfoPB?`. Negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → error count 0→1) run first, so the clean exit state is evidence: `*IDN?` byte-identical and error count `0` after both full runs.
- Tests: none run — no source changed this fire. Evidence posted as a comment on **#183** (`#issuecomment-5201118880`). Nothing merged. Two task chips filed: `task_738fa07d` (raise the MCP clamp floor to 1000 ms — smallest and fully specified) and `task_da7980a1` (make the claim-skip distinguishable from "no device"; options laid out, internal test seams already exist so it needs no hardware).
- Cleanup: detached worktree `/tmp/wtdisc` removed, all three scratchpad harnesses deleted. No branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** When the cap frees up there are now **five** queued chips, best-first: `task_738fa07d` (MCP clamp floor), `task_e9a93d25` (command-history docs), `task_8ca23636` (unbounded waits in `DaqifiStreamingDeviceLiveStreamTests`), `task_da7980a1` (claim-skip visibility), `task_8c1c2b3e` (admin SCPI failures). #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable rather than hunting another sub-item; do **not** go back to #344 for a new slice.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Device/SdCard` (40/40)
- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three: 0 unresolved (443: 1 thread, 444: 1, 445: 2 — all `isResolved: true`). Qodo's in-place-edited `Code Review by Qodo` bodies all read `Bugs (0)`; grepped the finding titles out of each body to confirm nothing live: #443 `<s>Retry options not validated</s> ✓ Resolved`, #444 `<s>Missing transparency settle delay</s> ✓ Resolved`, #445 `<s>Missing pre-send cancel check</s> ✗ Dismissed` + `<s>Recovery armed too early</s> ✓ Resolved`.
- **Verified #444's Qodo verdict actually covers its head**, since its review comment (03:44:36) predates its last commit `06bcf8a` (03:53:31): the comment's `updatedAt` is **03:54:10**, i.e. Qodo re-edited in place after the push. Worth repeating — on this repo `createdAt` on a Qodo review comment tells you nothing; compare `updatedAt` against the head commit time.
- Priority 2: nothing to do. All three `MERGEABLE`/`BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on each exact head (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), and `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...origin/<branch>` = `0 2` for all three (zero behind). Ready-notes already posted on all three. Priority 3: no regressions.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Device/SdCard`, the last large namespace in Core with zero hardware evidence.** (`SdCardOperations` came from **#422**, the first #344 slice — checked with `git log -- <file>` rather than assumed, which is what made #344 the right home for the evidence.) 36 files: the operations collaborator plus `SdCardSpaceParser` / `SdCardFileListParser` / `SdCardSpaceCheck`. Clean detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`); harness assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests` to inherit `InternalsVisibleTo`. **40 passed / 0 failed**, device byte-identical at exit.
- **Method: raw-serial ground truth BEFORE the typed API** (the technique the diagnostics fire established). Hand-rolled `SerialPort` replicating `PrepareSdInterface()`'s exact sequence (`StopStreamData` → `LAN:ENAbled 0` → `SD:ENAble 1` → 100 ms settle) and `PrepareLanInterface()`'s restore, so the parsers were checked against real firmware bytes instead of against the fixtures they were written from. Negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `**ERROR: -113, "Undefined header"`) first.
- **The check that most needed hardware: `SD:SPACe?` field ORDER.** Device returned `7799816192,7800356864` — first value smaller, so the documented `free,total` order is real. `SdCardSpaceParser` *rejects* `free > total` as corrupt metadata, so a total-first firmware would make a healthy card throw `SdCardOperationException`. Now demonstrated, not assumed. `SD:LIST?` → 45 entries of `<path> <size>`, sizes on **45/45** (the field `SdCardEmptyTransferException` depends on). Typed API byte-identical to the raw capture on both; parser re-run over raw bytes independently matched the typed listing's count.
- **LATENT-RISK FINDING (not a bug today): the device emits `DAQiFi/`, the parser's constant is `"Daqifi/"`.** It only matches because `SdCardFileListParser` uses `StartsWith(..., OrdinalIgnoreCase)`. A "tighten the string comparisons" cleanup that switched it to `Ordinal` would silently stop stripping the prefix and every filename in every listing would come back as `DAQiFi/log_….bin`. Same shape as the discovery fire's `ioreg` fallback note — passing today, one refactor away from not.
- Space-check branches sized **from the live free-space reading** rather than a fixture: 7.82 GB estimate (1 kHz × 16 ch × 2 B, duration computed to overshoot this card) → `IsInsufficientForCapture=true`, ETA `2.19:42:24`; `minimumFreeBytes=long.MaxValue` → `IsNearlyFull=true`. Both warning rules fire independently on real numbers.
- Guard surface, 11 rejected calls + 7 disconnected entry points, **`SYSTem:ERRor:COUNt?` unchanged at 0 throughout** — the hardware-only claim is "nothing reached the device". `SetSdCardMinimumFreeSpace` exercised **only** through its guards, deliberately: firmware has `SD:MINFree` as a setter with **no matching query**, so a bench cannot read the threshold back and therefore cannot restore it. Unrestorable state = guards-only.
- `SD:GET` / `FORmat` / `DELete` / log start-stop not sent. The download skip was **measured, not assumed**: `HeapFree=7544`, `LargestFreeBlock=7544`, `HeapFreeBlocks=1` — the known low-heap empty-transfer condition.
- **THE METHODOLOGICAL CATCH, and it nearly cost the fire: the first run read 0 bytes for EVERY raw query and looked exactly like the documented "bench Nq1 transient SCPI silence" wedge.** It was a bug in my harness. `System.IO.Ports.SerialPort` defaults `DtrEnable` to **false**; the DAQiFi CDC endpoint stays silent until the host asserts DTR, and Core's `SerialStreamTransport` sets `DtrEnable = true` / `RtsEnable = false`. Setting it made the same board answer everything instantly. **Generalizable: before believing a hardware-dead symptom, check that your harness configures the transport the way Core does — a raw harness is not automatically a more trustworthy instrument than the library.** Memory `project_bench_nq1_scpi_dead.md` updated with this as the new step 0, since the existing note would have sent a future fire straight to replugs and bootloader sequences.
- That first (no-DTR) run also left the following `ConnectSerialAsync` blocked in an **uninterruptible read for >5 min** (ps state `U`). Never recurred once DTR was asserted from the start, and `ConnectSerialAsync` is bounded by `DeviceConnectionOptions.Default`, so it is recorded as unexplained-but-not-reproduced rather than claimed as a Core finding. It is why the harness now carries a 240 s phase watchdog — worth keeping in any raw-serial harness.
- Tests: none run — no source changed this fire. Evidence posted as a comment on **#344** (`#issuecomment-5201260309`). Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap — no marginal PR manufactured).
- Cleanup: detached worktree `/tmp/wtsd` removed, scratchpad harness deleted. No branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** When the cap frees up, six queued chips, best-first: `task_738fa07d` (MCP clamp floor), `task_e9a93d25` (command-history docs), the new `Daqifi/` prefix-comparison hardening, `task_8ca23636` (unbounded waits in `DaqifiStreamingDeviceLiveStreamTests`), `task_da7980a1` (claim-skip visibility), `task_8c1c2b3e` (admin SCPI failures). #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable rather than hunting another sub-item; do **not** go back to #344 for a new slice. **Unbenched surfaces are now essentially exhausted** — remaining are the destructive-by-nature paths (WiFi flash, SD write/format/download).

## Fire: saturation bench pass — #344 refactor series vs real hardware (2026-08-06)

**Priorities 1-3: all clear.** #443/#444/#445 each verified on their exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`): `MERGEABLE`, CI `build` SUCCESS, **0 unresolved review threads**, Qodo summary bodies all read Bugs (0) with findings struck through (#445's finding 1 now shows `✗ Dismissed`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) an ancestor of all three, all three already ready-noted. **Still AT THE CAP (3)** → no new ticket started, per the concurrency rule. Nothing merged.

**Saturation work: differential bench validation of the nine `#344` extraction refactors.** Picked because `origin/main` has absorbed 11 commits (`3c5cca0..4b8eed2`) — #432/#433/#435/#436/#437/#439/#440/#441/#442 — that restructured stream-frame decode, status-frame channel mapping, channel/DIO/PWM control, USB stream-interface routing, live-sample async streaming, and session snapshot/restore. All were unit-tested only; none had ever been run end-to-end against real firmware. Built the example CLI twice (against `origin/main` and against the pre-series baseline `3c5cca0`) and ran identical suites on the bench Nq1 (fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive — no reboot/SD-write/firmware op, streaming reads only).

**Result: main is behaviorally identical to the pre-refactor baseline on every check.**

| Check | main `4b8eed2` | baseline `3c5cca0` |
|---|---|---|
| mask 1 / 7 / 1023 / 65535 → decoded analog count | 1 / 3 / 10 / 16 | 1 / 3 / 10 / 16 |
| row counts for those masks @50 Hz 3 s | 118/117/117/117 | 118/117/117/117 |
| status frame | `analogIn=16 digital=16 fw=3.7.2` | identical |
| 100 Hz 3 s mask 7 | 236 rows | 236 rows |
| 500 Hz 4 s | 1583-1584 rows, 0 backwards ts | 1582-1583 rows, 0 backwards ts |

Also on main: rate sweep 10/200/500 Hz gave ratios .750/.790/.792 vs requested — consistent with the known fw 3.7.2 clock mismatch (fw#716); uniform field counts at every rate (no ragged/truncated frames); 3x back-to-back connect/stream/disconnect cycles produced byte-identical status and identical 236-row captures (session snapshot/restore intact); `--limit 50` honored exactly; all 50 jsonl lines parsed as valid JSON.

**Two traps worth remembering:**
1. **`--channels` is a DECIMAL bitmask, not a 0/1 string.** The example-app README documents it as a "0/1 string" but the CLI's own `--help` says decimal. My first run passed `1111111111`, which is decimal 1,111,111,111 → low 16 bits `0x35C7` → popcount 9, so 9 analog values came back for what looked like a 10-channel request. **That is correct Core behavior, not a decode bug** — but it looks exactly like one. The README is stale (example-app repo, not core; not fixed this fire).
2. **Duplicate-timestamp counts at 500 Hz are useless as a regression signal.** First comparison showed main=47 vs base=33 dups, which reads like a regression. Ran both versions twice more: main 29/111, base 116/276. It's pure run-to-run firmware jitter (fw#717). Row counts and backwards-timestamp counts (always 0) are the stable signals; dup counts are not. **Don't file a bug off a single dup-count delta.**

**End-of-fire state:** 3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445), all clean/green/ready-noted, still at the cap. Local main checkout is behind origin/main by 10 — harmless, it's the user's checkout. Temp worktrees created for this pass were removed.

**Next fire:** still do NOT start a new ticket until the user merges or closes one of #443/#444/#445. If the cap is still full and 1-3 are clear, the honest outcome is another light bench pass or a log entry — do not manufacture a PR. When the cap frees up: #269's items 1-5 are all addressed, so consider proposing #269 is closable rather than hunting another sub-item, and do NOT go back to #344 (this fire just confirmed that series is healthy on hardware).

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Device/Capabilities` (37/38) → a real API-contract gap

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog.
- Priority 1 (pending Qodo): **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads`: 443 (1 thread), 444 (1), 445 (2, both outdated) — every one `isResolved: true`. The in-place-edited "Code Review by Qodo" comment body on all three reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0), and every finding in each body is `<s>struck</s>` + `✓ Resolved`.
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on each exact head (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) confirmed an ancestor of all three, ready notes already posted. Priority 3: no CI regressions.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Device/Capabilities`, picked because it is the last sizeable namespace with zero hardware evidence** (every #344 collaborator, `Device/Discovery`, `Device/SdCard`, `Device/Diagnostics` and #443's LAN probe were benched by prior fires). Harness `ProjectReference`'d at a clean detached worktree of `origin/main`. **37/38 passed**, device left as found (error queue empty, enabled set restored, document re-reads identical).
- What the device actually answers: **8013-char document at `schema_version` 2** (inside Core's `1..2` range), 32 channels (16 analog-in / 0 analog-out / 16 digital-io), **none** degrading to `CapabilityChannelKind.Unknown`. Merge worked as documented — `MaxSamplingRate` 1000 (board table) → **22000** (device), counts `0/0/0` → `16/0/16`, and the two deliberately-never-overlaid fields (`HasWincWifiModule`, `SupportsStreaming`) stayed board-derived. Re-applying the same real document is idempotent.
- **The hardware-only claims, and they hold.** `CurrentMaximumRateHz` measured by changing the enabled set and re-reading each time: 0ch→**0**, 1→7746, 2→6924, 4→5713, 8→4525 Hz. So it genuinely moves with the live set (not a constant), is monotonically non-increasing, and the documented "0 channels reports a real 0" is true. `RateModel.TryComputeMaxRateHz` predicted 15714/13750/11000/7857 for the same points — **an upper bound at every point, by ~1.7-2x**, which is exactly the "optimistic preview, CurrentMaximumRateHz is authoritative" wording.
- **`rate_validation: "error"` tested, not assumed, and it needed a control to mean anything.** 8 channels enabled (live cap 4525) → asked for **9050 Hz**, deliberately above the live cap but below the 22000 absolute ceiling, because Core's `StreamingFrequency` guard validates against the *absolute* ceiling — so the over-ask reaches the device and the device is the one that answers. It answered **`-222,"Data out of range"`** and did not stream. Control: an in-cap start on the same configuration left the queue clean, so the -222 is the over-ask and not the act of starting.
- **THE FINDING (the 1 failure): `DeviceMetadata.SerialNumber` and `CapabilityIdentity.Serial` are the same number in two different bases.** Metadata is `DeviceSn.ToString()` on a protobuf `ulong` → a 19-digit **decimal** string whose XML doc states no base at all; the document's identity block is the firmware's own **16-char uppercase hex**. Proved on hardware that they are the same value (decimal-parse vs hex-parse give identical `ulong`s; `metaValue.ToString("X")` equals the document string exactly) while plain string comparison is `false`. `DeviceIdentity` compares serials with `OrdinalIgnoreCase` string equality, so cross-checking the two surfaces — which is literally what `CapabilityIdentity`'s remarks invite ("reported for diagnostics and cross-checking") — silently concludes "different device". Filed as chip `task_d682cb77` (doc the base + a normalizing comparison helper + tests; keep the existing string value non-breaking).
- **Generalizable bench technique from this fire: a capability/self-description surface is validated by *perturbing the device and re-reading*, not by reading once.** Reading the document once only proves the parser ran; sweeping the enabled channel set and watching `current_max_rate_hz` track it is what turns "it parsed" into "the field means what the docs say". Same shape as the earlier "run the harness against the fix's parent" trick.
- Tests: none run — no source changed this fire. Bench evidence posted as a comment on **#390** (the closed issue that landed the capability reader), `#issuecomment-5201547076`. Nothing merged. Temp worktree `wtcap` and both scratch harnesses removed; main checkout left on `main` with the expected pre-existing `D SESSION_LOG.md` / `?? SESSION_LOG.md` reconciliation from the #438 untrack.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Seven queued chips now, best-first: `task_d682cb77` (serial base mismatch — newest and the best-evidenced), `task_738fa07d` (MCP clamp floor), `task_e9a93d25` (command-history docs), `task_0bbfe8e5` (SD prefix case), `task_8ca23636` (unbounded waits), `task_da7980a1` (claim-skip visibility), `task_8c1c2b3e` (admin SCPI failures). #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344. **Unbenched surfaces are now genuinely exhausted** — what remains is destructive by nature (WiFi flash, SD write/format/download).

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Logging/Export/CsvExporter` (67/70) → two real high-rate defects

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` — 443 (1), 444 (1), 445 (2, both outdated) — every one `isResolved: true`. Every `Code Review by Qodo` comment body reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0), and each finding is `<s>struck</s>` (`Retry options not validated` ✓, `Missing transparency settle delay` ✓, `Missing pre-send cancel check` ✗ Dismissed, `Recovery armed too early` ✓).
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) confirmed an ancestor of all three, ready notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Logging/Export/CsvExporter`, and the previous fire's "unbenched surfaces are exhausted" was wrong.** This is the one public API with **no `ISampleSource` producer anywhere in Core** (the only implementations are the test `InMemorySampleSource` and the example app's `SdCardSampleSource`), so it had literally never been fed real device data — every test uses synthetic, collision-free timestamps. Harness: a `ListSampleSource` over `DaqifiStreamingDevice.StreamSamplesAsync`, `ProjectReference`'d at a clean detached worktree of `origin/main`, three live captures on the bench Nq1 (fw 3.7.2, USB, mask 7 = 3 analog, non-destructive — streaming reads only). **67 passed, 3 failed** (the 3 failures are 2 distinct defects, one of which fires in two captures).
- **DEFECT 1 — duplicate device timestamps silently DELETE samples.** `WriteTimestampRowAsync` buckets by `TimestampTicks` and does `lookup[row.ChannelKey] = row.Value` — last writer wins — so two distinct frames sharing a timestamp collapse into one CSV row with no warning, no count, no event. 100 Hz: **0 of 1185** lost. 1 kHz: **1299-1404 of ~7130 (~18-20%)**. 5 kHz: **4131-4191 of ~23760 (~17.4%)**. Replicated across three independent runs. ~700 of the discarded 5 kHz values *differed* from the kept one, so it is information loss, not dedup.
- **The attribution step is what makes it a finding instead of a guess.** Comparing `IDataSample.DeviceTimestamp` inside every colliding cell gave `sameDeviceTimestamp=4191, distinctDeviceTimestamp=0` — the firmware really repeats the stamp (fw#717), **`TimestampProcessor` is exonerated**. Without that field the same numbers would have read as a Core timestamp-conversion bug. Generalizable: when a high-rate symptom could be either the device or Core's decode, the raw device field is the arbiter — check it before naming a culprit.
- **DEFECT 2 — `UseRelativeTime` loses resolution above ~1 kHz.** `FormatTimestamp` uses `"F3"` (1 ms). 5 kHz export: **6520 rows → 1584 distinct time values** (4936 rows share a time string with a neighbour), so the column is unusable as a plot x-axis. Absolute `"O"` (100 ns) is unaffected, and 100 Hz / 1 kHz did **not** collapse — the effect needs >1000 *distinct* ticks/s, which the duplicate-timestamp merge itself holds down until ~5 kHz. Device streams to 22 kHz, so any high-rate capture hits this.
- **What held up (the other 67):** rectangular rows at every option combo; header/column agreement; **bit-exact `"G"` round-trip of real calibrated doubles**; ISO-8601 timestamps parse + non-decreasing; **byte-identical output under `de-DE`**; semicolon delimiter keeps `.` as the decimal separator; averaged-window row count `ceil(samples/window)` and means match recomputation; declared-but-silent channel emits an empty cell in every row; progress ends at 100. The live stream also satisfied the `ISampleSource` ordering contract exactly — **0 backwards timestamps in ~32k samples** and tick groups always contiguous (`groups == distinctTicks`), so an adapter built on `StreamSamplesAsync` is legitimate.
- Filed as chip **`task_10ba9fca`** (document the collapse + make the dropped count observable additively + raise relative precision to `F6` + xUnit regressions). Bench evidence posted on **#166**, the closed issue that landed the exporter — `#issuecomment-5201761876`. Memory `nq1_fw372_clock_mismatch.md` updated: fw#717's duplicate stamps are not cosmetic, they destroy data in Core's export path.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Device left healthy (probe after the run: connected, `streaming=False`, fw 3.7.2, 32 channels); mask 7 left enabled (AI0-AI2) — benign RAM state, and the pre-bench enabled set was never captured so there was nothing to restore to. Temp worktree `wtexp` and the scratch harness removed.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Eight queued chips, best-first: `task_10ba9fca` (CsvExporter high-rate loss — newest, best-evidenced, and the only one with a hardware-measured blast radius), `task_d682cb77` (serial base mismatch), `task_738fa07d`, `task_e9a93d25`, `task_0bbfe8e5`, `task_8ca23636`, `task_da7980a1`, `task_8c1c2b3e`. #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Channel` analog scaling → three findings

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three returned **zero unresolved**. Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0), findings struck through (`Retry options not validated` ✓, `Missing transparency settle delay` ✓, `Missing pre-send cancel check` ✗ Dismissed, `Recovery armed too early` ✓).
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) an ancestor of all three, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Channel/AnalogChannel` + `AnalogScaling`.** Chosen because the standing memory note said scaling is "latent on this bench unit" (CalB=0, ISM=1) and therefore un-benchable. That note was **half wrong**, which is what made the pass worth doing. Harness `ProjectReference`'d at a clean detached worktree of `origin/main`, assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests` to inherit `InternalsVisibleTo`. Non-destructive: channel-enable + start/stop streaming only; `CONFigure:VOLTage:PRECision` / `ADC:USECal` read, never written. Negative control first; error queue 0 and `*IDN?` byte-identical at exit.
- **The #387 fix is CONFIRMED CORRECT against firmware source, not just against the issue text.** Firmware `MC12b_ConvertToVoltage` (`firmware/src/HAL/ADC/MC12bADC.c:249`) is `(range*scale*CalM*raw)/Resolution + CalB`; `AnalogScaling.Scale` is algebraically identical with CalB un-scaled. **2400/2400 streamed samples had `Value` exactly equal to `AnalogScaling.Scale(RawValue, device metadata)`** — 0 mismatches. 17 validation guards all correct.
- **CORRECTION to the standing "scaling is latent here" note: over USB the stream carries RAW ADC COUNTS, not pre-scaled floats** — `RawValue` non-null on 2400/2400 — so `GetScaledValue` is on the USB hot path. Only the *coefficients* are latent; divisor and range are exercised. `StreamFrameDecoder.cs:461-471` comments assert the opposite ("USB firmware already scaled to volts" / "WiFi firmware sent a raw ADC count"). The branching is right (it keys on payload, not transport); the attribution is wrong, and it's the kind of wrong that makes someone dismiss a scaling bug as "USB doesn't go through there".
- **FINDING (real, consumer-facing): the "agrees with `MEASure:VOLTage:DC?`" claim in 3 public XML doc sites is conditional and the condition is unstated.** `IAnalogChannel.cs:66`, `AnalogChannel.cs:283`, `AnalogScaling.cs:24`. Firmware `SCPI_ResultVoltage` (`SCPIInterface.h:145`): precision 0 → **integer millivolts**, 1-10 → volts to N dp. **This unit reports `CONFigure:VOLTage:PRECision?` = 0** (NQ1 board default is 4), so Core's `0.0073242188 V` vs the device's `7` — ~1000x, which reads exactly like a Core scaling bug. Chip `task_84fd5bd7`.
- **SECOND GAP: Core has `SaveVoltagePrecision`/`LoadVoltagePrecision` (`CONFigure:VOLTage:SAVE`/`LOAD`) but NO accessor to read or set the value** — firmware has `CONFigure:VOLTage:PRECision` and `?` (`SCPIInterface.c:5121-5122`). Core exposes the persistence verbs for a setting it cannot observe or change, which is also what makes the doc finding un-actionable from inside Core. Same shape as the earlier `SYSTem:LOG:LEVel?` gap. Folded into `task_84fd5bd7`; comment/prose fixes filed separately as `task_1f6e8d70`.
- Minor: `Resolution` is documented as "the maximum raw count (2^bits - 1)" but this device reports **4096 = 2^12** (matches `NQ1BoardConfig.c .Resolution = 4096`). No numeric disagreement — firmware divides by the same device-reported value — but the parenthetical is wrong. In `task_1f6e8d70`.
- **THE METHOD NOTE, and it cost two runs: I tried to settle "raw counts vs integer millivolts" ON THE BOARD, and this rig cannot do it.** All 16 AI inputs float at 0-6 counts, where the two models differ by less than the sample-to-sample drift: run 1 scored mV 16/16 exact vs counts 13/16; run 2 scored 15/16 vs 14/16 — the same experiment disagreeing with itself. 1 LSB is 1.22 mV, the same order as the gap under test. The firmware source settled it in one read. **Generalizable, and the inverse of the SD-card fire's lesson (where the raw harness was the less trustworthy instrument): when the discriminating signal is smaller than one quantum of the instrument, more runs cannot help — go to the source of truth, and spend the hardware on what source can't answer** (here: this unit's persisted precision, and which payload branch USB actually takes).
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Evidence posted on **#387**, the closed issue that landed this formula (`#issuecomment-5201985952`). Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap). Memory `reference_nyquist_bench_rig.md` updated: the "scaling is latent, don't bench it" note was too broad, and the unit's `VoltagePrecision=0` is recorded so a future fire doesn't read the mV answer as a Core bug.
- Cleanup: detached worktree `/tmp/wtscale` removed, scratch harness + probes deleted. No branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere. Device left healthy; enabled mask left at all-16 (benign RAM state).
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Ten queued chips, best-first: `task_84fd5bd7` (VoltagePrecision accessor + doc claim — newest, evidenced against firmware source AND hardware), `task_10ba9fca` (CsvExporter high-rate loss), `task_d682cb77` (serial base mismatch), `task_1f6e8d70` (decode-comment attribution — smallest, docs-only), `task_738fa07d`, `task_e9a93d25`, `task_0bbfe8e5`, `task_8ca23636`, `task_da7980a1`, `task_8c1c2b3e`. #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.
