Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0), findings struck through (`Retry options not validated` ✓, `Missing transparency settle delay` ✓, `Missing pre-send cancel check` ✗ Dismissed, `Recovery armed too early` ✓).
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) confirmed an ancestor of all three via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Communication/Producers/ScpiMessageProducer`, the SCPI command-string factory.** Picked because its unit tests assert each generated string equals a hardcoded literal — the purest case of a test checking an assumption against itself — and it had zero hardware evidence. Prior fires covered every #344 collaborator, Discovery, SdCard, Diagnostics, Capabilities, CsvExporter and Channel/AnalogScaling.
- **METHOD — two independent falsifiers, and the static one was worth as much as the live one.** (1) Reflection over every public static member returning `IOutboundMessage<string>`, invoked and `GetBytes()` dumped, so the inventory is byte-exact and complete: **86 members / 78 distinct headers**, no member left unresolved. (2) Firmware ground truth from the **exact matching tag** — `git show v3.7.2:firmware/src/services/SCPI/SCPIInterface.c` in the local firmware checkout — **267 libscpi patterns**. Negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `-113,"Undefined header"`) before anything else.
- **STATIC RESULT: all 78 Core headers exist verbatim in the fw v3.7.2 table, zero unmatched.** Core builds no phantom command. (Note the `SYSTem:COMMUnicate:LAN:FWUpdate` capital-U inconsistency is harmless — libscpi matching is case-insensitive.)
- **LIVE RESULT: 44/50 accepted with a clean error queue; all 6 rejections are device state, not Core defects.** `SD:LIST?` and `SD:BENCHmark?` → `-200` from low heap (`HeapFree=7544/75000`, `LargestFreeBlock=7544`, `HeapFreeBlocks=1`) while the card itself is fine (`SD:SPACe?` 7.8 GB free) — fw#703 exactly as the standing memory note predicts. `CONFigure:ADC:USECal 1` → `-200` **but bank 0 accepted clean**, so the format is right and this unit simply has no user cal bank. `SOURce:VOLTage:LEVel 0,1.5` and `CONFigure:DAC:UPDATE` → `-200` because the board reports **0 analog-output channels**. Quoted-string and two-parameter arg forms all accepted (`SYSTem:LOG:LEVel STREAM,2` → `STREAM: 2 (ceiling 3)`, DIO/PWM pairs, `chanCALM? 0`, no-op write-backs of `SD:MAXSize`/`SD:MINFree`, `StartStreamData 50`/`StopStreamData`).
- **TRAP — one "failure" was my own harness and I nearly filed it.** `CONFigure:CAPabilities:JSON?` looked like an error because the 8 KB response was still draining when the error-queue probe fired, so trailing JSON was read back as the error string. Re-run with a full quiescence wait: **8014 bytes, valid JSON, queue empty**. **Generalizable: on any large response the error-queue probe must wait for the line to go quiet, or it reports the payload as the error.** Same family as the CsvExporter fire's "check the raw device field before naming a culprit" — verify the instrument before believing the reading.
- **THE FINDING (real, consumer-facing): Core's DIO/PWM/analog-out control surface is WRITE-ONLY.** 189 of 267 fw commands are unexposed; 9 are the direct readback partner of a setter Core already ships — `DIO:PORt:DIRection?` / `ENAble?` / `STATe?`, `ENAble:VOLTage:DC?`, `PWM:CHannel:DUTY?` / `ENable?` / `FREQuency?`, `SOURce:VOLTage:LEVel?`, `SYSTem:STORage:SD:MINFree?`. A consumer cannot confirm or restore anything it sets. Same shape as the known `CONFigure:VOLTage:PRECision` gap. **The proof it bites: this bench pass had to drop to raw serial to verify its own restoration.** Also unexposed and plausibly wanted: `MEASure:VOLTage:DC?`, `SYSTem:BAT:LEVel?`, `LAN:CONNected?`, `LAN:MAC?`. Filed as chip `task_050f3b71` (with the minor `SetDioPortState` non-InvariantCulture double folded in — integral today via `value ? 1 : 0`, so latent only).
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Bench evidence posted on **#345** (`#issuecomment-5202143732`). Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap).
- Cleanup: detached worktree `/tmp/wtscpi` removed, emitter project `/tmp/scpiemit` and scratch drivers deleted. No branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere. Device left healthy — error queue 0, `*IDN?` byte-identical to the pre-run read, stream format / interface / cal bank all restored to captured originals. Benign RAM residue: `LOG:LEVel STREAM`=2 and PWM ch4 freq/duty set with PWM **disabled** — neither has a Core readback to restore through, which is the finding itself.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Eleven queued chips, best-first: `task_050f3b71` (SCPI readback gap — newest, evidenced against firmware source AND hardware), `task_84fd5bd7` (VoltagePrecision accessor), `task_10ba9fca` (CsvExporter high-rate loss), `task_d682cb77` (serial base mismatch), `task_1f6e8d70` (decode-comment attribution), `task_738fa07d`, `task_e9a93d25`, `task_0bbfe8e5`, `task_8ca23636`, `task_da7980a1`, `task_8c1c2b3e`. #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Communication/Consumers` → two real findings

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three: **0 unresolved**. Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads `Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0)`; grepped the finding titles out of each body — #445's only live-looking entry is `<s>Recovery armed too early</s> ✓ Resolved`, nothing unresolved anywhere.
- Priority 2: nothing to do. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...origin/<branch>` = `0 2` for all three (zero behind `4b8eed2`), ready-notes already posted on all three. Priority 3: no regressions.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Communication/Consumers`, the last namespace in Core with zero hardware evidence.** Every prior bench in this log covered a `Device/*` collaborator. Picked because it is the raw byte-stream framing layer: `ProtobufMessageParser`, `LineBasedMessageParser`, `CompositeMessageParser`, `StreamMessageConsumer` — the surface where unit tests can only confirm the synthetic bytes they were written from. Clean detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`); harness added to the existing `Daqifi.Core.Tests` project so it inherits `InternalsVisibleTo`. **35 checks: 22 passed, 13 failed.** No source changed, so no repo suite run and no PR — reported only.
- **Method: capture the wire first, then replay it through the real accumulate/parse/trim loop.** Hand-rolled `SerialPort` capture of every shape (negative control `SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `**ERROR: -113, "Undefined header"` first), then replayed the exact bytes through the parsers using the same `Add byte → ParseMessages → RemoveRange(0, consumedBytes)` sequence `StreamMessageConsumer.cs:435-439, 630-638` runs. **Checking the harness against the consumer's real loop before trusting a single number is what makes the split-point sweep meaningful.**
- Ground truth worth keeping: the `SYSTem:SYSInfoPB?` reply is `C8 04` varint + **584**-byte body + **a trailing ASCII `0D 0A`** — the device terminates its *binary* reply with CRLF. Stream frames are 10 bytes (1 ch @10 Hz, **0 % null and 0 % printable**) and ~11 bytes (4 ch @100 Hz, 16.6 % null / 8.3 % printable). Whole-buffer parses of both stream captures consume 100 % of bytes with zero resyncs and strictly increasing timestamps — **the steady-state hot path is sound**.
- **FINDING 1 (latent, real): `ProtobufMessageParser.FindNextParseableFrameStart` eats genuine partial frames.** Its justifying comment says "a genuine partial frame runs past the end of the buffer, so nothing parseable can follow it" — false. The scan starts *inside* the partial frame's own body, and real protobuf bodies contain runs that decode as a complete valid `DaqifiOutMessage`. Two-read sweep over **every** boundary of the real 588-byte frame: **133 of 587 split points (22.7 %) lose the reply outright**, 393 spurious frames emitted instead; never a silent stall. Sharpest single number: first read = 326 bytes → 1 spurious message and **326 bytes reported consumed**, i.e. the prefix plus the first 324 body bytes of the real frame, which the caller then trims.
- **Why it is latent, and the step that established that:** classified every spurious frame with `ProtobufProtocolHandler.DetectMessageType` — **all 393 are `Unknown`**, which `HandleAsync` drops and `SerialDeviceFinder` filters out. So no fabricated data reaches consumers; the damage is the *real* reply going missing. And **20/20 live `SYSInfoPB?` requests through `StreamMessageConsumer` returned the real frame** — on this USB host the 588 bytes always arrive in one read. Exposure is any transport that splits reads (`TcpStreamTransport`/WiFi, slower host, larger status frame). **Generalizable: "the parser emits garbage" and "a consumer acts on garbage" are different claims — run the emitted objects through the production type filter before assigning severity.**
- Live symptom that *does* reproduce on USB: text reply preceding the frame → **10/10 requests emitted `Unknown → Unknown → Status`**, two spurious frames ahead of the real one, so a consumer acting on the first `MessageReceived` gets an all-zero message. Offline equivalent: one-shot → 3 msgs (2 spurious then real); chunked at 64 → 9 msgs, **none** the real frame.
- **HYPOTHESIS I KILLED (worth recording):** I expected the device's own trailing CRLF to be the leading noise that arms the faulty scan on the next reply. Tested it directly — reply-at-a-time delivery of two back-to-back captures gives **2 messages, both real**, and CRLF-stripped gives the same. The CRLF is harmless; the trigger is purely read-boundary splits. **A plausible mechanism story is not evidence — the two-line test that falsifies it costs less than the paragraph explaining it.**
- **FINDING 2 (public-API, not live): `CompositeMessageParser` routes the real binary status frame to the text parser.** `IsLikelyTextCommand` ends with `fullText.EndsWith("\r\n")`, and the device's *binary* reply ends with CRLF — so a buffer that is 24.0 % printable and **40.5 % null** is classified `LikelyText`, `LineBasedMessageParser` swallows all 588 bytes as one "line", and the frame is destroyed. Nothing in the repo constructs `CompositeMessageParser` (live paths use the concrete parsers directly), so it is an exported-API defect, not a live one.
- Evidence posted as a comment on **#268** (`#issuecomment-5202412946`) — the issue whose fix (PR #272, `ed605b8`) introduced the scan, traced with `git log -S` rather than assumed. Two chips filed: `task_b73cf0ca` (fix the scan without regressing #268's echo-noise recovery) and `task_859fecee` (composite routing). Nothing merged, no branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- Bench hygiene: non-destructive throughout — queries plus short protobuf streams; no reboot/format/firmware/SD/WiFi command. Device verified after: `*IDN?` answering, error queue drained to `0,"No error"` (the 2 entries were my own negative controls), `HeapFree=7544` **identical to the pre-run read**, stream stopped, stream format `0`, interface `0`. Benign RAM-only residue: analog enable mask left at `1`.
- Harness trap for next time: a `Drain(quietMs)` helper with no hard ceiling **never returns while a stream is live** — the first capture run wedged mid-stream and had to be killed, which left the port claimed by a zombie `dotnet` host (`lsof /dev/cu.usbmodem*` to find it). Every drain loop needs both a quiet-time and a max-duration bound, and every capture program should send `SYSTem:StopStreamData` on entry.

- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Communication/Transport` (21 files, 4375 lines), the largest namespace in Core with zero hardware evidence.** Prior fires covered every #344 collaborator plus Discovery / SdCard / Diagnostics / Capabilities / CsvExporter / AnalogScaling / ScpiMessageProducer / Consumers; this one was never touched. Clean detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`), harness assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests` to inherit `InternalsVisibleTo`. **58 passed / 1 failed**, device byte-identical at exit. Scope chosen deliberately: serial + the retry/watchdog scaffolding, **skipping HID entirely** (that is the destructive firmware-flash path) and touching TCP only on loopback and TEST-NET-1 (`192.0.2.1`), so no WiFi churn.
- **THE FINDING (doc accuracy, not a code defect): the rationale behind #424's classification is measurably false.** Both `SerialPortConnectFailure` and `SerialPortConnectException` justify evidence-based classification by asserting the platform exceptions are indistinguishable — *"an IOException whose message and HResult are the same in both cases, so neither the type, the text, nor the errno can tell them apart"*, and *"the same (bogus, non-errno) HResult"*. Measured on the exact cited configuration (macOS 26.5, .NET 9, System.IO.Ports 10.0.10): the **outer** `UnauthorizedAccessException` is identical as documented, but the **inner** differs — missing port → `IOException "No such file or directory" HResult=0x2` (**ENOENT**), busy port → `IOException "Resource busy" HResult=0x10` (**EBUSY**). Those are real errnos, not bogus.
- **Why the conclusion still stands, for a stronger reason than the one written down — and this is the part worth keeping.** The inner errno is present but *unreliable*: in a minimal process the **first failed open reproducibly reports `Unknown error: 203` (`0xCB`)**, which is not a macOS errno at all (ELAST ≈ 106). Three runs × 25 iterations: anomaly always at index 0, 1/25 every run. **Warm-up does not clear it** — a preceding successful open, with real read/write traffic, or closed first, all still give 203 (9/9 across those variants) — yet the full harness's own first failed open reports ENOENT. So some earlier process state flips it and **I did not isolate which, and said so** rather than shipping a mechanism story. Consequence that makes it worth filing: a consumer who read that remark, checked it once, and "fixed" Core to classify from the inner errno would be right in their test process and **wrong on the first connect failure the app ever makes** — the one a user hits after a replug renumbers the port. `Classify` never reads the errno, so Core is correct in every case measured. Filed as chip `task_a5656d24` (docs-only).
- **Method note that paid off twice:** the compiler itself proved one claim — `sce is not UnauthorizedAccessException` failed to compile (CS8121), which is stronger evidence for the documented API break than any runtime assertion; rewrote it as `typeof(...).IsInstanceOfType`. And the S1 raw-exception ground truth was captured with a hand-rolled `SerialPort` **before** running anything through Core, so the classification was checked against real platform bytes rather than against the fixtures it was written from.
- Everything else verified live: **#399's operational timeouts read back off the OPEN handle** (`ReadTimeout=500`, `WriteTimeout=2000`); `transport.Stream` round-tripped a real `*IDN?` byte-identical to the raw baseline; second `ConnectAsync` a true no-op (0 extra events); `TransportNotConnectedException` / `ObjectDisposedException` on the right sides of disconnect and dispose; handle genuinely released (raw reopen succeeds). Watchdog: 6 polls on a healthy port → no false drop, 1 miss no-op, 2 misses report the loss, teardown releases the handle, I/O escalation at exactly 5 faults with one `ReportIoSuccess` resetting it, intentional disconnect never a loss. Retry: exhausted → classified `NotFound` with backoff; pre-cancelled token never opens; cancel during a 3 s backoff returned in **403 ms**; and the best one — **a busy port released mid-retry connects on a later attempt** (3 failed, then success), which turns the `InUse` "retrying can succeed" doc promise into evidence.
- **`enableDtr: true` is load-bearing, proven by negative control:** DTR asserted → `*IDN?` answers (35 bytes); `enableDtr: false` → **0 bytes in 1.5 s**; DTR-true again → answers. Polled via `BytesToRead` only, **never a blocking read** — that is exactly the trap that wedged a prior fire's harness for >5 min.
- `TcpStreamTransport`: unreachable host → substituted `TimeoutException` honoring the budget (813 ms for 800 ms); a *refused* connect → `SocketException` in 23 ms, correctly **not** relabelled a timeout.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Bench evidence posted on **#424** (`#issuecomment-5202541516`), the issue whose fix this validates. Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap), no branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- Cleanup: detached worktree `/tmp/wttransport` removed, harness deleted, port confirmed free (`lsof`). Device healthy — `*IDN?` byte-identical to baseline, error queue `0,"No error"` (the only entries were my own negative controls). No RAM residue: the bench opened and closed ports, it never set device state.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Note the user's main checkout is **10 commits behind `origin/main`** (harmless, but it is why `git status` there shows a staged `D SESSION_LOG.md` — #438 merged and origin/main ignores the file at `.gitignore:133`).
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining** (for the next true-saturation fire): `Device/Network` (read-only paths only — LAN writes are churn-risky), `Device/Protocol/ProtobufProtocolHandler`, `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (`MacOsHidPlatform`/`HidLibraryTransport`, deliberately skipped here as the destructive flash path), and `Logging` outside `Export`.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Communication/Producers/MessageProducer<T>` → two real findings

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three: **0 unresolved**. Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads `Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0)` and every finding title is struck through (`Retry options not validated` ✓, `Missing transparency settle delay` ✓, `Missing pre-send cancel check` ✗ Dismissed), each posted against the exact head SHA.
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Communication/Producers/MessageProducer<T>`, the outbound queue/writer-thread pipeline.** Picked because it is the **write half of the transport** and the read half (`Consumers`, #268) and connection half (`Transport`, #424) already have hardware evidence — this closes the triangle. Every existing unit test writes into a `MemoryStream` or a fake that throws on demand, so nothing in the suite had ever seen a write that *blocks*, which is where all the interesting behaviour lives. Note `ScpiMessageProducer` (the command-string factory) was benched earlier; this is the unrelated queue/thread class. Detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`), standalone console harness. **63 checks: 51 passed / 12 failed**, and the 12 failures are exactly two defects. No source changed → no repo suite run, no PR.
- **Ground truth before any Core code was in the path, and it validated a load-bearing assumption:** a real macOS `SerialStream` write timeout raises **`System.TimeoutException`** (256 KB at `WriteTimeout=1ms` → threw in 36 ms). That is the exact type `ProcessMessages` special-cases to *avoid* reporting a health fault, so #382's classification is correct on this platform rather than correct-by-assumption. Negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` → `-113`) first, as always.
- **`IsIdle` (#342) is not a theoretical concern — it dominates.** Sampling `(QueuedMessageCount, IsIdle)` in a tight loop across 20 sends of a 7-byte SCPI command caught the "queue empty but the write has not finished" state in **335 153 of 350 600 samples (95.6 %)**. `QueuedMessageCount == 0` is the wrong barrier by a wide margin on real hardware; `IsIdle == true` implied the command had reached the device 10/10.
- **FINDING 1 (real, public API): `StopSafely` reports success for a message that never left the host.** The wait loop watches the *queue*, but a message is dequeued before it is written; then `_producerThread?.Join(1000)` (MessageProducer.cs:145) **ignores the caller's `timeoutMs` and discards its result**, and `return true` runs regardless. Parking the writer inside a 256 KB write (really ~2005 ms against `WriteTimeout=2000`): `StopSafely(500)` / `(1000)` / `(10000)` each returned **true at ~1009 ms with `IsIdle == false`**. **Three independent proofs of non-delivery, not one** — the return value contradicts `IsIdle`; `SendFailed(TimeoutException, IsTimeout=True)` fires *after* the claimed success for that same message; and the device never answers the `*IDN?` appended to that payload. Second, separable bug in the same three lines: `StopSafely(10000)` gave up at 1008 ms and `StopSafely(500)` was held for **twice** its budget (`SerialDeviceFinder.cs:587` is a live 500 ms caller). The sibling `StreamMessageConsumer.StopSafely` already does it right (`Join(timeoutMs)`, returns `stopped`, nulls the field only `if (stopped)`) — the reader side got the fix, the writer is still on the original #33 code. Chip `task_236c726e`.
- **FINDING 2 (latent, public API): a restart after a timed-out stop leaves TWO writer threads on one stream.** `Stop()`/`StopSafely()` null `_producerThread` even when the join timed out and `Start()` has no stale-thread guard, so the parked thread re-reads `_isRunning` — true again — and keeps draining alongside the new one. **Method worth reusing: `GetBytes()` runs on whichever writer thread dequeued the message, so a message that records `Environment.CurrentManagedThreadId` is an exact census of live writer threads.** Steady state `[4]` → after the restart `[9,4]`; two runs, `[9,4]` and `[16,14]`; the producer's log prints "background loop exited cleanly" **twice**. This is precisely what `StreamMessageConsumer.Start()` refuses to do — the `_startLock` + `ConsumerThreadNotExitedException` guard PR #384 added for #383. Chip `task_363af64c`.
- **HYPOTHESIS I KILLED, and it lowered the severity I filed at.** I expected two writers on one stream to corrupt the wire, so I raced both threads with 32 KB payloads whose trailing `*IDN?` only survives an unsplit write. Both threads provably took part (census `[16,14]` across the six bulk messages) and the wire was **intact: 6/6 queries answered, 0 parse errors, error queue clean**; 40 small commands after the restart came back 40/40. `SerialStream.Write` serializes enough on macOS that I could not produce corruption. Filed as a broken invariant with **no reproduced live symptom**, and the chip explicitly tells the implementer not to claim corruption — same discipline as the `CompositeMessageParser` call.
- **Everything else passed and is worth keeping as evidence #408/#382/#342 hold up live:** `Send()` never throws for a dead stream; **5/5 `SendFailed`** (`ObjectDisposedException`, `IsTimeout=False`) with the producer still draining and still `IsRunning`; 5 faults reported to the sink for 5 failures; over the real `SerialStreamTransport` pairing, **8/8 `SendFailed` after an intentional disconnect with 0 connection-loss events** (write faults are not escalated into a spurious drop); 120-deep burst → **120/120, zero loss**; strict `*IDN?`/`SYSTem:ERRor?` alternation preserved as the device saw it; `Stop()` really drops pending work (1 of 60); `Send` before `Start` / after `Dispose` throw the documented types; `Dispose` idempotent.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Evidence posted on **#408** (`#issuecomment-5202760048`), the issue whose fix this both validates and extends. Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap), no branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- Bench hygiene: non-destructive throughout — queries plus inert newline payloads, no reboot/format/firmware/SD/WiFi command. Device verified after every section and at exit: `*IDN?` byte-identical to the pre-run baseline, error queue `0,"No error"` (the only entries all run were my own negative controls), no stream started, **no device state written at all** — this bench only opened ports and sent queries. Detached worktree `/tmp/wtprod` and harness `/tmp/prodbench` removed; port confirmed free via `lsof`.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Sixteen queued chips; newest-and-best-evidenced first: `task_236c726e` (StopSafely false success — hardware-proven three ways), `task_363af64c` (stale writer thread), then `task_b73cf0ca`, `task_050f3b71`, `task_10ba9fca`, `task_859fecee`, `task_84fd5bd7`, `task_d682cb77`, `task_a5656d24`, `task_1f6e8d70`, `task_738fa07d`, `task_e9a93d25`, `task_0bbfe8e5`, `task_8ca23636`, `task_da7980a1`, `task_8c1c2b3e`.
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining** (for the next true-saturation fire): `Device/Network` (read-only paths only — LAN writes are churn-risky), `Device/Protocol/ProtobufProtocolHandler`, `Device/Internal/StreamFrameGate`, `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (deliberately skipped — destructive flash path), and `Logging` outside `Export`.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Device/Protocol` + `StreamFrameGate` → the 42 MHz figure is itself wrong

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three: **0 unresolved** (rechecked at end of fire, still 0). Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads `Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0)`; grepped the finding titles out of each body — all struck through (`Retry options not validated` ✓ Resolved, `Missing transparency settle delay` ✓ Resolved, `Missing pre-send cancel check` ✗ Dismissed, `Recovery armed too early` ✓ Resolved).
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE`, `build` **pass** on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) confirmed an ancestor of all three, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Device/Protocol/ProtobufProtocolHandler` + `Device/Internal/StreamFrameGate`.** Both were on the prior fire's unbenched list. Picked together because they are the two decision points in the same decode path, and `DetectMessageType` is the routing brain for *every* inbound protobuf message (`DaqifiDevice.cs:3502` wires it to the status/stream handlers; `SerialDeviceFinder.cs:490` uses it for identity). Detached worktree at `origin/main`, harness assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests` to inherit `InternalsVisibleTo` for the internal gate. Three rounds; **round 1 28/28 green**, rounds 2-3 designed around what round 1 surfaced.
- **THE FINDING: the device reports `TimestampFreq = 42 MHz` but its `MsgTimeStamp` counter really advances at 33.333 MHz (= 100/3 MHz).** 30 s capture at 100 Hz: tick span 999,180,000 over 29.976 s wall → 33.333 MHz; labelled/real = **0.7936**, and 33.333/42 = 0.79365. Holds to four decimals at 50 / 100 / 200 Hz (0.7937 / 0.7936 / 0.7936).
- **The two competing explanations were both killed, which is what makes it a clock claim rather than a guess.** Dropped frames: tick-delta histogram over 2379 deltas gave **zero** doubled deltas, worst jitter ±7 ticks out of 420,000 — the counter is contiguous, the device is not skipping. Host lag: a 3 s drain after `StopStreaming` recovered no backlog, so 2380 frames is the true total and not a truncated one (100 Hz × 30 s would be 3000).
- **Why it matters, and this is the part worth keeping: it contradicts a sentence in #426.** That issue says *"This is not daqifi-nyquist-firmware#716 ... Opposite sign, different cause."* Same cause — one was measured in device time, the other in wall time. One root cause (a sample timer programmed against a clock 1.26× faster than the one it runs on) produces both symptoms. **And #426's fix removed a partial cancellation:** with the old 50 MHz default a 20 Hz log read 42.4 ms/interval (visibly wrong); with the device-reported 42 MHz it reads **50.0 ms — nominally perfect** — while the samples were physically acquired 63.0 ms apart. The error is now invisible without an external clock. Confirmed through Core's own public API, not just my arithmetic on `MsgTimeStamp`: 2379 samples via `IChannel.SampleReceived` spanned 23.780 s of derived timestamps for a capture that really took 29.976 s (0.7933) — a **20.7% time-base error in any export**.
- **Caveats I stated in the writeup rather than papering over:** one bench unit, one firmware version; the counter was measured against the *host* clock with no firmware source read, so "33.33 MHz" is measured behavior, not a proven PBCLK misconfiguration. And **no Core defect** — Core faithfully uses the figure the device reports; the fix is a sanity check or documented correction, not a change to the conversion.
- **Classifier is correct on real hardware: 359/359, plus 2380/2380 stream frames.** Every payload-carrying frame → `Stream`, the status message → `Status`, zero `Unknown`; replaying the whole corpus through a fully-wired handler routed identically to `DetectMessageType`; `CanHandle` accepted all 359. **The Status-before-Error ordering is load-bearing and only hardware shows it:** real status messages carry `DeviceStatus = 3` *and* a nonzero `MsgTimeStamp`, so they match the `Error` predicate and would misclassify if `IsStatusMessage` did not run first — no unit test covers that because the fixtures were written from the code.
- **`StreamFrameGate` (#425/#533) never fired.** 6 stop/start cycles, gaps 25 ms → 1200 ms: **0** `StaleLeftoverFrame` discards. Positive control that the wire is live rather than dead: `PartialAnalogFrame` (#351) fired on **6/6** restarts. So the leftover-frame guard is dormant on 3.7.2 and, importantly, ate no genuine frames. Measured restart turnaround is ≥568 ms of device time, far outside the 50 ms window at 50 Hz.
- **Two smaller real findings.** (1) The USB/WiFi attribution in the analog-payload comments is **backwards**: `IsStreamMessage` and `StreamFrameDecoder.DecodeAnalog` both claim USB sends pre-scaled floats and WiFi sends raw ints, but over USB on 3.7.2 it was **2380/2380 `AnalogInData` (int), 0 `AnalogInDataFloat`** — the "WiFi" calibration branch is the live USB path. Behavior right, attribution wrong, and the comment would tell someone `DataSample.Raw` is null on USB when it is always populated. (2) `ProtobufMessageType.SdCard` is **unreachable** — no path in `DetectMessageType` returns it, so the public `sdCardMessageHandler` ctor parameter can never fire (0 invocations over 359 real messages, and none by inspection).
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Evidence posted on **#426** (`#issuecomment-5202931844`), the issue whose reasoning it corrects. Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap), no branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- Bench hygiene: non-destructive throughout — queries and streaming only; **no** reboot / format / firmware / SD / WiFi command, and no device state written beyond channel enables and streaming rate. Verified at exit: `*IDN?` byte-identical to the pre-run baseline, error queue `0,"No error"` twice (the only entry all run was my own `SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND?` negative control). Detached worktree `/tmp/wtproto` and harness `/tmp/protobench` removed.
- Chips: filed `task_2363da94` (TimestampProcessor's ~1.19 doc claim understates the error and implies the device-reported figure is exact), `task_cb7e2ae8` (backwards USB/WiFi comments, both sites), `task_acdea257` (unreachable SdCard branch). **Dismissed `task_1f6e8d70`** as superseded — a prior fire had filed the same comment-attribution finding without hardware evidence.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.**
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining** (for the next true-saturation fire): `Device/Network` (read-only paths only — LAN writes are churn-risky), `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (deliberately skipped — destructive flash path), `Logging` outside `Export`, and `Device/Internal/ChannelControlOperations` (the #432 collaborator was benched, but not this extracted file directly).

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog (still 5 open issues: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` on all three: **0 unresolved** (443: 1 thread, 444: 1, 445: 2 — all `isResolved: true`; rechecked at end of fire, still 0). Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads `Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0)` with every finding title struck through (`Retry options not validated` ✓ Resolved, `Missing transparency settle delay` ✓, `Missing pre-send cancel check` ✗ Dismissed).
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` **SUCCESS** on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e` — unchanged), ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Device/ScpiResponseClassifier` (242 lines, 20+ call sites across SdCard / Diagnostics / Capabilities / LanChipInfo / DaqifiDevice / DaqifiStreamingDevice).** Picked because every existing test in `ScpiResponseClassifierTests` asserts against **invented** strings — the surface had zero hardware evidence, and it is the brain behind "did the device complain?" for the whole library. Clean detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`), harness assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests` to inherit `InternalsVisibleTo`. Ground truth captured with a raw `SerialPort` **before** any Core code was in the path.
- **Real format on fw 3.7.2 is `**ERROR: -113, "Undefined header"` — note the space after the comma**, which the invented test strings (`**ERROR: -200,"Execution error"`) do not have. Classifier handles it: 9/9 error lines correctly classified with the right extracted code. Errors are reported **twice** — inline as `**ERROR:` *and* pushed onto the IEEE 488.2 queue, one entry popped per `SYSTem:ERRor?`.
- **THE FINDING: one command can return MULTIPLE error lines with DIFFERENT codes, so `LastOrDefault(IsScpiErrorLine)` + `code == X` is unsound.** A bare `:::` reproducibly yields `**ERROR: -101, "Invalid character"` then `**ERROR: -113, "Undefined header"` (3/3, byte-identical). Ran verbatim copies of the two **code-keyed** sites over real device bytes: `SD:LIST?` + `:::` → `[-200,-101,-113]` flips `LanChipInfoOperations.cs:50`'s `LanNotInitialized` verdict to **False** (3/3); `NOTACOMMAND` + `SD:LIST?` → `[-113,-200]` flips `GetSdCardStorageAsync`'s `FeatureNotSupported` to **False** (3/3). Single-error controls both **True**, so the sites are correct only for the one-error case.
- **Why it matters beyond the classifier:** this is the exact mechanism #203 exists to drive, and **PR #443's `GetLanChipInfoWithRetryAsync` (part of #269) is built entirely on `catch (LanNotInitializedException)`** to decide whether to kick `LAN:APPLY` — if the exception doesn't fire, the probe spins out its whole total-time budget without ever kicking and reports `WasLanNotInitialized = false`. Pre-existing on main, **not** introduced by #443.
- **Honest severity, stated rather than papered over: latent, not observed end-to-end.** I could not force `GETChipInfo?` itself to emit -200 plus a trailing error — the WINC on this unit is initialized and answers real JSON (`{"ChipId":1377184,"FwVersion":"19.7.7",...}`), so the -200 branch never fired live. What is proven is that the *premise* (one command ⇒ at most one error line) is false. `ExecuteTextCommandAsync`'s stale-line boundary is no help: these are lines the device emits for the **current** command.
- **A FALSE FINDING I KILLED, and it nearly went out the door.** The first raw capture had `SD:LIST?` answering `**ERROR: -200` while the card was plainly present (`SD:SPACe?` → 7.8 GB) and the device log read `SD:LIST? - SD card not enabled` — this looked exactly like Core misreporting a present card. Checked instead of assumed: `PrepareSdInterface()` sends `SYSTem:STORage:SD:ENAble 1` before **every** SD exchange, so Core's own path never sees it. It was my harness skipping the prepare phase. Sending `ENAble 1` + settle myself then returned the full listing.
- **Everything else green, and worth keeping as evidence:** `SYSTem:ERRor?` queue replies classify at **every** code (`0,"No error"`, `-113,...`, `-101,...` all match `IsSystemErrorReplyLine`) — load-bearing for **#396**, where the **non-zero** codes are the case that matters. A **real 45-entry SD directory listing**: 0/45 misread as terminator, 0/45 as error lines, terminator matched at exactly index 45 (the last line) — the documented `"%s %u"` claim holds on real data. Interleaved error+result is a **live** path, not defensive: `SD:BENCHmark?` returned `**ERROR: -200` *and* `0,0,0`, handled correctly by `ThrowIfSdCardListError`'s `hasContentLine`. The **bare-`ERROR` branch is dead on 3.7.2** — of 80 captured lines, 9 start `**ERROR`, **0** start bare `ERROR`. Firmware log lines (`SCPI Error -113: ...`, `WiFi STA Disconnected - Error Code: 0, ...`) correctly classify as non-error, so `SystemLogParser` loses nothing.
- **Method note:** `FirstOrDefault` vs `LastOrDefault` genuinely disagree in this codebase — `DaqifiDevice`'s init path (4 commands in one exchange) uses `FirstOrDefault` and is **not** code-keyed, which is fine and should be left alone; the two code-keyed sites use `LastOrDefault`. Do not "unify" them blindly.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**. Evidence posted on **#203** (`#issuecomment-5203090089`), the issue whose fix introduced the -200 detection (PR #324). Chip filed: `task_0ac96481` (scan all error lines for the code instead of testing only the last; also flags that the existing unit tests use a no-space error format the firmware does not emit). Nothing merged, no PR opened (correct at the cap), no branch touched, so no `SESSION_LOG.md` delta anywhere.
- Bench hygiene: non-destructive throughout — queries, intentionally-invalid headers, and the SD `ENAble` mode flag. **No** reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware / WiFi reconfiguration; LAN deliberately never disabled (churn risk) and the SD listing was obtained without it. Verified at exit: `*IDN?` byte-identical to the pre-run baseline, error queue `0,"No error"` (every entry all run was my own negative control), `SD:ENAble` restored to `0` **and re-verified** (`SD:LIST?` → -200 again). Detached worktree `/tmp/wtscpi` removed, harness deleted, port confirmed free.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Nineteen queued chips.
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining** (for the next true-saturation fire): `Device/Network` (read-only paths only — LAN writes are churn-risky), `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (deliberately skipped — destructive flash path), `Device/DeviceErrorThrottle` + `DeviceErrorEventArgs` (the error *reporting* pipeline; needs a decode that throws per frame, so it may only be reachable synthetically), `Device/TimestampGapDetector`, and `Device/DaqifiDeviceRegistry` / `DaqifiDeviceFactory`.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `TimestampGapDetector` → the default threshold IS the one-lost-sample boundary

- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` — 443 (1), 444 (1), 445 (2, both outdated) — all `isResolved: true`. Every `Code Review by Qodo` body reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0).
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) an ancestor of all three, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged. Priority 4 skipped — **at the 3-PR cap.**
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `Device/TimestampGapDetector`.** Picked because it is wired live into the decode path (`DaqifiStreamingDevice.cs:1122` → `GapDetected`) yet every test in the suite feeds synthetic deltas, and it was on the previous fire's unbenched list. Detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`), standalone console harness assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests`. Three rounds, 10/50/200/1000 Hz, mask 7.
- **METHOD that made it work: capture the per-frame `msg_time_stamp` via `MessageReceived` (same gated population `DecodeStreamFrame` sees, post-#425), then replay offline through a fresh `TimestampProcessor` + `TimestampGapDetector` wired exactly as the device wires them.** Replay reproduced the live `GapDetected` count at all four rates, which validates the replay *and* the event wiring in one check. That decoupling is what let me splice synthetic drops into *real* stamp sequences and sweep thresholds without re-streaming per hypothesis.
- **FINDING 1 (real, consumer-facing): losing exactly one sample is a coin flip decided by floating-point rounding.** At steady cadence the EMA converges to the period, so one lost sample gives a delta of *exactly* 2× the average — and `DefaultGapThresholdMultiplier = 2.0` with strict `>`. Measured delta/EMA at a 1-sample splice: 10 Hz `1.999999996` (missed), 200 Hz `1.999995129` (missed), 50 Hz `2.000000000` (caught) — and a **second independent capture set at the same rates caught all three.** Two/three-sample losses detect reliably (3.0×/4.0×). `>=` is NOT the fix: it changed nothing at 3 of 4 rates because the real ratios fall just *below* 2.0.
- **The test that hid it is the purest specimen of this failure mode yet found:** `IsGap_DeltaExactlyAtThreshold_ReturnsFalse` asserts `Assert.False(IsGap(period * 2.0))` with the comment "Equal to 2x the average is not strictly greater". After a steady warm-up `period * 2.0` **is** the physical signature of one dropped sample — the test pins the operator, and its positive-case neighbour uses `2.01` so nothing in the suite ever connects the boundary to the thing being detected.
- **FINDING 2: at 1000 Hz on fw 3.7.2 no threshold works at all.** 19.6% of frames (1245/6350; 912/4762 in an earlier run) repeat the previous `msg_time_stamp` — fw#717. Those zero deltas are skipped by the `is not > 0` guard and never enter the EMA, so the average converges to **~1.24 ms instead of 1 ms (24% inflated baseline)**. Every multiplier below 2.0 becomes unusable (840 FPs at 1.5, 368 at 1.8, 56 at 1.9 on a *healthy* capture), and a real single-sample loss produces a 2 ms delta byte-identical to a duplicate-stamp catch-up — **confirmed by re-running the sweep with duplicates removed: detected at no multiplier from 1.10 to 3.00.**
- **The doc's false-positive claim HOLDS** — 0 gaps in 19,041 frames across 3 × 8 s of 1000 Hz, 0 at every other rate, no negative `SecondsBetweenMessages` anywhere. The docs assert the FP direction and are silent on the FN direction, which is the one that bites.
- **Near-miss worth keeping: a stray `-113` appeared beside `SYSTem:StopStreamData` in teardown and I nearly logged it as a firmware finding.** Four controls (idle, idle-repeated, start-then-stop, known-good header) all came back clean — it was queue residue from an earlier probe. The standing "re-send the raw primitive before calling it a device bug" discipline paid for itself again; extend it to "drain the queue before attributing an error to the command next to it".
- Filed as chip `task_411a8d72` (move the default off 2.0 into the measured-safe 1.10-1.99 window, rewrite the boundary test to name the requirement, document the 1000 Hz limit). Bench evidence posted on **#339** (`#issuecomment-5203294009`), the closed issue that landed the port.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**, so no repo suite and no PR (correct at the cap). Device healthy at exit: `*IDN?` byte-identical to baseline, error queue `0,"No error"`, streaming stopped; benign RAM residue = analog enable mask left at `7`. Detached worktree `/tmp/wtgap` and harness `/tmp/gapbench` removed.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** 20 queued chips now; newest and best-evidenced first: `task_411a8d72` (gap-detector threshold), `task_0ac96481` (code-keyed SCPI error lookup), `task_2363da94` (TimestampProcessor 1.19 doc claim), `task_d682cb77` (serial base mismatch), `task_10ba9fca` (CsvExporter high-rate loss). #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `DaqifiDeviceFactory` + `DaqifiDeviceRegistry` (40/40) → a real serial duplicate-contract gap

- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` — 443 (1), 444 (1), 445 (2, both outdated) — all `isResolved: true`. Every in-place-edited `Code Review by Qodo` body reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0); finding titles grepped out to be sure the counts hid nothing: #443 `<s>Retry options not validated</s> ✓ Resolved`, #444 `<s>Missing transparency settle delay</s> ✓ Resolved`, #445 `<s>Missing pre-send cancel check</s> ✗ Dismissed` + `<s>Recovery armed too early</s> ✓ Resolved`.
- **Method note worth keeping:** `jq '...|last'` over PR comments picks Qodo's short "review was updated up to the latest commit" note, NOT the review — it silently returned empty for #443/#445 and looked like a clean summary. Select by `select(.body|test("Code Review by Qodo"))` instead. An empty grep is not evidence of zero findings.
- Priority 2: nothing. All three `MERGEABLE` / `BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` SUCCESS on the exact heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) an ancestor of all three, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged. Priority 4 skipped — **at the 3-PR cap.**
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — `DaqifiDeviceFactory` + `DaqifiDeviceRegistry`.** Picked because the registry ships an internal `DeviceConnector` seam whose own doc says it exists "so the duplicate-policy paths can be tested without hardware" — i.e. the entire duplicate surface is mock-only by construction. Detached worktree at `origin/main`, console harness `ProjectReference`'d at its Core csproj. **40/40 checks passed**, then a 4-control second round.
- **FINDING (real, consumer-facing): a same-transport serial duplicate throws instead of reporting `DuplicateRejected`.** `registry.ConnectAsync(portOnlyInfo)` twice for one unit → `SerialPortConnectException: Serial port '...' is in use.` (inner `UnauthorizedAccessException`). A port-only `DeviceInfo` yields an **empty** pre-connect `DeviceIdentity`, so `FindDuplicateLocked` can't match; the registry connects, and macOS refuses the second open before the `AfterConnect` phase can run. Two doc claims are overstated on this path — "the loser is detected after connecting and disposed" (there is no loser connection) and the `DuplicateRejected`-carries-the-registration contract (a serial caller must also catch the transport exception).
- **The control is what made it a finding rather than a guess:** two *direct* factory connects with no registry involved throw the byte-identical exception → the refusal is the OS/transport, not a registry defect. Same standing discipline as the raw-primitive re-send. Damage checked rather than assumed: live set stayed `Count=1` and the original session was still connected and usable afterwards.
- **Cheap fix, and the design constraint matters:** the registry already holds it — stored `DiscoveryInfo.PortName` and the incoming `IDeviceInfo.PortName` were verified *identical strings* in the failing case. But this must be a **liveness** check ("is this exact port open right now"), **NOT** a new `DeviceIdentity` discriminator — port names are reassigned across replug on this rig, so PortName is not a stable fingerprint. Filed as chip `task_308e7d43`.
- **The cross-transport design is NOT broken and I checked before writing it up.** `ConnectAsync_SerialOnlyKnownAfterConnect_IsCaughtByThePostConnectCheck` models WiFi-registered + serial-connecting, which is genuinely reachable (TCP and serial sessions coexist). The seam is what lets that test hold two live devices for one unit; the real serial transport never can. Scope the finding to same-transport serial only.
- **Everything else green, and the OS-level claims are the ones worth having:** the port is truly freed on every teardown path — `DisposeAsync`, `registry.Remove(key)`, `registry.Dispose()`, and the **failure** path (a cancelled mid-dial connect per #341 threw, and the next connect succeeded with matching metadata — so `ConnectWithTransportAsync`'s catch-block cleanup releases the handle, not just the reference). `Register(disconnectedDevice)` threw `ArgumentException` and the **same instance** then reconnected and re-initialized — "a caller mistake never costs them their device" holds literally. `PruneDisconnected()` left the live device alone (0 pruned); re-`Register` idempotent with no second `DeviceAdded`; discovery-sourced duplicate rejected at `BeforeConnect` with **no transport opened**.
- **`SerialDeviceFinder` DOES probe a serial number pre-connect** (matches post-connect metadata exactly) — so the registry doc's "the serial number of a serial-port device is often only known once it has answered its first status message" applies to a hand-built port-only `DeviceInfo`, not to discovery-sourced info. Post-connect metadata for a serial device also carries a MAC even though serial discovery reports `MacAddress=(null)`.
- Secondary (low severity, in the comment not the chip): `InitializeDevice=false` yields `Channels.Count=0` + empty serial → `Register` mints key `device-1` with `identity=(unidentified)` and `Find(realSerial)` misses, so **duplicate detection is silently disabled** for uninitialized devices. And the `baudRate` parameter is not load-bearing — 115200 returns metadata identical to the 9600 default (CDC-ACM), so the overload implies control that does not exist.
- `LocationKey` confirmed `null` on macOS as documented — the third discriminator is simply unavailable off Windows.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**, so no repo suite and no PR (correct at the cap). Evidence posted on **#380** (`#issuecomment-5203485469`), the issue the registry landed under (PR #381).
- Bench hygiene: connect/disconnect cycles and queries only. No reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware / WiFi reconfiguration / `LAN:*` writes. Used `SerialDeviceFinder` rather than the all-transports finder so **zero UDP broadcast or WiFi traffic** was generated (connect-churn hazard). Device healthy at exit: serial + `fw=3.7.2` byte-identical to the pre-run baseline. Port confirmed free; detached worktree `/tmp/wtreg` and harness `/tmp/regbench` removed; main checkout left on `main` with the expected pre-existing `D`/`??` `SESSION_LOG.md` reconciliation from the #438 untrack.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** 21 queued chips. #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining:** `Device/Network` (read-only paths only — LAN writes are churn-risky), `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (destructive flash path, deliberately skipped), `Device/DeviceErrorThrottle` + `DeviceErrorEventArgs` (may only be reachable synthetically), and `Logging` outside `Export`.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Device/Network` decode (19/19) → six public SCPI producers no consumer can read

- **Shepherd pass first, nothing actionable.** `git fetch`; 3 open loop PRs (#443 `0d903a7`, #444 `06bcf8a`, #445 `1967c2e`) — **AT THE CAP**, so no new ticket. Checked **both** Qodo sources on all three: summary comment bodies all read Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) with every earlier finding struck through, and the GraphQL `reviewThreads` list returns **0 unresolved** on each. CI `build` SUCCESS, all `MERGEABLE`, `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) still an ancestor of every head, and each already carries a ready-for-review note pinned to its exact current head. No regression, no drift, nothing to re-note.
- **Bench target chosen because its tests can't reach the thing that matters:** `Device/Network`'s `NetworkAddressHelper` is *public* production code (feeds `DeviceMetadata.IpAddress`/`MacAddress` and `WiFiDeviceFinder`), but every one of its ~30 unit tests builds the `ByteString` by hand. Byte order, field length and field presence are firmware facts, and no hand-built array can falsify them. Detached worktree at `4b8eed2`, console harness against local Core, USB only.
- **The decode is correct, and now it is actually evidenced. 19/19, twice, byte-identical.** Real wire bytes off the Nq1: `IpAddr=C0-A8-01-1E`, `NetMask=FF-FF-FF-00`, `Gateway=C0-A8-01-01`, `MacAddr=F8-F0-05-A4-95-AC` (6 B), `PrimaryDns`/`SecondaryDns`/`IpAddrV6`/`Eui64` all **absent (0 B)**. `string.Join(".", bytes)` is right: firmware sends **network order, MSB first**, so the decode re-parses to the identical 4 bytes. MAC OUI `F8-F0-05` is Microchip — the WINC, as expected. `Metadata.MacAddress` == helper output. The strict 4-/6-byte length gate is what keeps the two absent DNS fields from decoding into junk, so that gate is load-bearing on this firmware, not defensive dead code.
- **THE FINDING (real, consumer-facing): six public `ScpiMessageProducer` members that a consumer can send but cannot read.** `GetLanAddress`, `GetLanMask`, `GetLanGateway`, `GetLanConfiguredAddress`, `GetLanConfiguredMask`, `GetLanConfiguredGateway` have **zero callers in Core** — grep finds them only in `ScpiMessageProducerTests`, which asserts the command string and nothing else. Through the managed device all six returned **empty**, reproducibly.
- **The control is what turned that from a timing guess into a fact.** Raw serial, same port, same session: every one of them answers — `LAN:ADDRess? → 192.168.1.30`, `LAN:MASK? → 255.255.255.0`, `LAN:GATEway? → 192.168.1.1`, `LAN:CONFigure:ADDRess? → 192.168.1.1`, with positive controls (`*IDN?`, `SYSTem:SERialNUMber?`) and a negative control (`SYSTem:NOTAREALCOMMAND? → **ERROR: -113`) bracketing them. **The device is not silent; Core drops the reply.** Structural, not a race: `TextMessage` is constructed **nowhere** in Core production code, so no SCPI text reply ever reaches `MessageReceived`, and the only reader — `ExecuteTextCommandAsync` (what `GetLanChipInfoAsync` uses) — is `protected` on `DaqifiDevice`, which the factories never return a consumer subclass of. Not reachable by timing, patience, or a longer wait.
- **Why the protobuf snapshot does NOT cover the gap.** `LAN:ADDRess?` = `192.168.1.30` (active) but `LAN:CONFigure:ADDRess?` = `192.168.1.1` (staged) — genuinely different values, exactly as `GetLanConfiguredAddress`'s own doc-comment promises ("may differ from the active address reported by `GetLanAddress`"). The status message carries only the **active** address, so the **staged/pre-apply** configuration is obtainable by no supported route at all. That matters for the persist-first `SET→SAVE→APPLY` flow (`LAN:SAVE persists staged config`): a caller can stage a configuration and has no way to read back what it staged.
- **Fix shape for a later fire (chip filed):** a public read that reuses the `ExecuteTextCommandAsync` seam `GetLanChipInfoAsync` already proves out — not a new transport path. Alternative, if the producers are meant to be desktop-only: document them as send-only and say the protobuf/`Metadata` route is the supported one. Either is small; the current state (public API, silently unreadable) is the one option that should not stand.
- Tests: no repo suite run — **no source changed this fire**, and no PR (correct at the cap). Bench: 19/19 green on two independent runs plus the raw-serial control series.
- Bench hygiene: reads only. No LAN write, no `APPLY`/`SAVE`, no `SYSTem:POWer:STATe` change, no reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware. **Zero UDP broadcast or WiFi traffic** — serial only, so no connect-churn exposure. Device healthy and byte-identical at exit (`sn` unchanged, `fw=3.7.2`, same MAC and IP across all runs); port confirmed free. Worktree `/tmp/wtnet` and harness `/tmp/netbench` removed; main checkout left on `main` with the expected pre-existing `D`/`??` `SESSION_LOG.md` reconciliation from the #438 untrack.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** 22 queued chips.
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining:** `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (destructive flash path, deliberately skipped), `Device/DeviceErrorThrottle` + `DeviceErrorEventArgs` (internal; `MessageConsumer`/`Reconnect` sources need a physical unplug, so synthetic-only for an unattended fire), `Device/Network`'s **write** path (`UpdateNetworkConfigurationAsync`, churn-risky — do not), and `Logging` outside `Export`.

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of the feature-gating surface → the 1000 Hz board-table ceiling is falsified 4x on hardware

- **Shepherd pass first, nothing actionable.** `git fetch`; 3 open loop PRs (#443 `0d903a7`, #444 `06bcf8a`, #445 `1967c2e`) — **AT THE CAP**, so no new ticket (priority 4 correctly suppressed; 5 open issues remain: #344, #333, #271, #269, #183). Checked **both** Qodo sources on all three: GraphQL `reviewThreads` **0 unresolved** on each, and each in-place-edited `Code Review by Qodo` body reads Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0) with every finding title struck through — #443 `<s>Retry options not validated</s> ✓ Resolved`, #444 `<s>Missing transparency settle delay</s> ✓ Resolved`, #445 `<s>Missing pre-send cancel check</s> ✗ Dismissed` + `<s>Recovery armed too early</s> ✓ Resolved`. CI `build` SUCCESS on all three exact heads, all `MERGEABLE`/`BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), ready-notes already posted. No regression, no drift.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — the feature-gating surface: `DeviceTypeDetector` + `DeviceCapabilities` + `DeviceFeatureRequirements`/`DaqifiDevice.Supports`.** Picked because it had **zero** mentions in this whole journal and every one of its unit tests feeds synthetic part numbers and version strings — the entire surface is claims *about* firmware, tested only against invented firmware. Detached worktree at `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`), console harness assembly-named `Daqifi.Core.Tests` (gets `InternalsVisibleTo`, so the requirement table itself was readable). Two rounds, 27 checks.
- **THE FINDING (real, consumer-facing): `DeviceCapabilities.FromDeviceType` hardcodes `MaxSamplingRate = 1000` for Nyquist1/2/3, and the real Nq1 streams straight through it.** One analog channel, ~2 s per run: **1000 Hz → 1583 samples, 2000 Hz → 3169, 5000 Hz → 7924** — clean linear scaling with no ceiling anywhere. The ~79% requested-vs-actual ratio is the *separate* known fw#716 clock mismatch and is present at 1000 Hz too, so it is not a cap. The device's own capability document (schema 2) reports `maximumSampleRateHz = **22000**` (with `conservativeEnvelopeHz=500`, `currentMaximumRateHz=6260`). So the constant is **22x low vs the device's self-description and 4x low vs measured throughput**.
- **Why it bites, proven live rather than argued:** `StreamingFrequency` *and* `TrackStreamingStart` both validate against `Metadata.Capabilities.MaxSamplingRate`, which is the board table's 1000 until `ReadCapabilityDocumentAsync` overlays the document. Connect `InitializeDevice=false` → `dev.StreamingFrequency = 2000` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException: must be between 1 and 1000 Hz` **on the same physical device that streamed at 2000 Hz one connection earlier.** Also reachable whenever the document read returns null (fw < v3.5.0, schema outside 1..2, unanswered query) — there 1000 is permanent, not a bootstrap value. Quieter second-order effect: `TrackStreamingStart` *silently ignores* a raw `Send(StartStreaming(2000))` in that window instead of throwing, so Core stops believing in a session the device is actually running.
- **Constraint on the fix, measured not assumed: there is no cheap SCPI route to the ceiling.** `CONFigure:ADC:RATE?` → `**ERROR: -113, "Undefined header"`. The JSON document is the only route the firmware offers, so "read it earlier" is not an option — either raise the constant or stop treating the pre-document value as an authoritative rejection threshold. Filed as chip `task_32ac5642`.
- **A FALSE FINDING I KILLED, and it was one line from going out.** Round 1's raw-serial control phase returned empty for *every* probe, and its DAC check "PASSed" only because an empty string doesn't contain `0,"No error"` — I nearly wrote up "firmware rejects the DAC write" off a phase that proved nothing. What caught it: the **positive** control (`*IDN?`) had come back empty too. Cause was mine — `SerialPort.DtrEnable` defaults to **false**, while Core's own `SerialStreamTransport` defaults it to **true**. With DTR asserted every probe answered. **New standing rule: a bench phase whose positive control fails is not evidence, however plausible its other results look.** Also: raw harnesses must mirror Core's DTR default or they read silence and blame the device (cf. the "transient SCPI silence" note — check DTR before suspecting the board).
- **Everything else on the surface holds, and is now evidenced.** Real part number is exactly `Nq1`, 3 bytes `4E-71-31`, **no padding** — so the non-`Trim` dictionary lookup is safe on this firmware, but only by luck (one trailing space ⇒ `Unknown`). Board-table booleans all true and all corroborated: `HasUsb` (on USB CDC now), `HasSdCard` (real 7,800,356,864 B reply), `HasWiFi`+`HasWincWifiModule` (status MAC `F8-F0-05-…`, Microchip OUI = WINC1500), `SupportsStreaming` (157 samples @100 Hz). Channel counts are correctly **not** board-table-sourced — table leaves AI/DIO at 0, status message fills 16/16, matching the 32 real channels. All four `Supports()` gates agree with firmware at 3.7.2/Nyquist1; `AnalogOutput` is board-refused by Core **and** firmware independently refuses raw `SOURce:VOLTage:LEVel 0,0.0` with `**ERROR: -200`. `EnsureSupported` reports `Board=Nyquist1, Actual=3.7.2, RequiredVersion=(none)` — correctly declining to imply an upgrade helps. The ADR-0001 Unknown-board window behaves exactly as documented: fails **open** on board/hardware (`Supports(AnalogOutput)=true` on Unknown), **closed** on version (`Supports(CapabilityDocument)=false` on an empty version). Document overlay survived a streaming session intact (pn/board/22000 all unchanged), so the guarded rebuild in `UpdateFromProtobuf` works.
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**, so no repo suite and no PR (correct at the cap). Evidence posted on **#336** (`#issuecomment-5203962843`), the issue whose fix introduced the `StreamingFrequency` validation (PR #349); cross-referenced #390/#404 for the document reader.
- Bench hygiene: queries, streaming, and one **staged** DAC write — `CONFigure:DAC:UPDATE` never sent, so nothing reached a pin. No reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware / `LAN:*` write. Serial only ⇒ **zero UDP broadcast or WiFi traffic**, no connect-churn exposure. Device healthy at exit: `*IDN?` byte-identical to baseline, error queue drained to `0,"No error"`, streaming stopped. Benign RAM residue = analog enable mask left at 1. Detached worktree `/tmp/wtfeat` and harness `/tmp/featbench` removed; port confirmed free; main checkout left on `main` with the expected pre-existing `D`/`??` `SESSION_LOG.md` reconciliation from the #438 untrack.
- **Harness note for the next fire:** the .NET `SerialPort` finalizer can hang process exit on macOS *after* all output is written — round 1 looked like a wedged bench but had actually completed every phase. Call `Environment.Exit` at the end, and write bench output to a file rather than through a `| tail` pipe (the pipe buffers everything until EOF and makes a finished run look hung).
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** 23 queued chips. #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining:** `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (destructive flash path, deliberately skipped), `Device/DeviceErrorThrottle` + `DeviceErrorEventArgs` (internal; real sources need a physical unplug, so synthetic-only for an unattended fire), `Device/Network`'s **write** path (`UpdateNetworkConfigurationAsync`, churn-risky — do not), and `Device/Capabilities`' `CapabilityRateModel` evaluation (the `rateValidation='error'` / `currentMaximumRateHz` machinery — a fresh angle now that the 22000/6260/500 figures are captured).

Picked because every unit test for it hand-builds the document, so the constants and the formula *shape* had never met a device that enforces them. Detached worktree at `origin/main`, harness assembly named `Daqifi.Core.Tests`. **Four rounds**: R1 through Core (5/5 checks green), R2 raw serial, R3 through Core's public API, R4 the composition experiment.
- **The doc claims that HOLD, now evidenced.** `rate_validation:"error"` is exact — raw serial, 1 muxed channel (cap 7746): 1000 Hz → 31,720 B/4.00 s and a clean queue; **10000 Hz → 36 bytes + `-222,"Data out of range"`**; 30000 Hz same; repeat control → 31,720 B again, byte-identical. **Hard rejection, no silent clamping, no wedge.** Also confirmed: `current_max_rate_hz` = **0** with nothing enabled; `MaximumSampleRateHz` (22000) populates `MaxSamplingRate`; `ConservativeEnvelopeHz` (500) is genuinely drop-free — 8 s × 16 analog channels → 50,736 samples, **0** gaps, **0** decode failures.
- **FINDING 1 — the published formula uses the wrong channel count.** It makes the ceiling a function of **total** analog count; the device makes it a function of the **MUXED** count and charges essentially nothing for a dedicated-converter channel. Two *direct* measurements, no fitting: 3 muxed → **6260 Hz**, and 3 muxed **+ 2 simultaneous** → **6260 Hz** (two extra channels, **0 Hz** of change, while Core's model drops 12222 → 10000); 1 simultaneous → **8788**, 2 simultaneous → **8788** (identical). Holding total fixed at 5 the device spans **5253 → 8634 Hz (1.64x)** by composition alone while `TryComputeMaxRateHz` returns **10000 for all three**. Model/device ratio **1.16x–2.03x** across the sweep. One-parameter fit `65332/(7.43 + muxed)` reproduces every muxed-only point to **0.03%** vs the published `110000/(6 + total)`. Note the **sign**: the published `type1_aggregate/simultaneous_count` term *penalizes* simultaneous channels; the device *rewards* them.
- **FINDING 2 — `Type1AggregateMaximumHz` is unreachable on shipping hardware.** With 55000/110000/6 the term binds only when simultaneous count **> 6**; the board has **5** (ids 4,8,10,12,14). Swept all **86** reachable (sim, total) pairs on the real document: **0 binds**, closest 1.10x. So `simultaneousChannelCount` is a no-op whose only live effect is its own `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` — next to a careful div-by-zero comment implying the branch is load-bearing.
- **FINDING 3 (the consumer-facing one) — Core starts streams the device refuses and reports them as live.** `CurrentMaximumRateHz` has **zero consumers in Core production code** (grep: only the parser that fills it + doc comments). `StreamingFrequency` validates against `MaxSamplingRate` (22000, the absolute ISR envelope). With 16 analog enabled (device cap **3518**) Core **accepts 3519 / 5000 / 10000 / 22000** and rejects only 22001 — **every rate in (3518..22000] is accepted by Core and refused by the device.** Streaming at 10000 Hz for 5 s: `IsStreaming`=**true**, samples=**0**, gaps=**0**, `DecodeFailureCount`=**0**, `ErrorOccurred`=**0**. Totally silent; the `-222` sits unread because the protobuf consumer owns the port. **Same symptom #378 closed for the decode-failure cause, from a cause `DecodeFailureCount` structurally cannot see.** And the number handed to the caller is the one that fails: for the 5-simultaneous set the model returns 10000 → **0 samples**; cap+300 → **0**; cap−300 → **132,250**.
- **The controls are what make it a refusal rather than a wedge.** 3018 Hz immediately before and after the 10000 Hz attempt: **191,472 samples both times, ratio 1.000.** And the cap is genuinely per-selection: 5632 Hz streams with 1 channel (17,864 samples) and yields **0** with 16 — same rate, same session.
- **FINDING 4 — the staleness window, quantified.** Read at 1 channel → 7746; `EnableChannels` the other 15 without re-reading → `Metadata...CurrentMaximumRateHz` still **7746** while the true cap is **3518** (2.2x high, dangerous direction). #409 landed field-22 parsing so the enabled *view* is in sync; the cached *cap* is not recomputed, so #409's drift still lives in the rate figure.
- **Honest scoping: no Core arithmetic defect.** `TryComputeMaxRateHz` evaluates the published formula correctly — Findings 1-2 are firmware self-description, 3-4 are Core's. Caveats stated in the writeup: one unit, fw 3.7.2, USB only (the wifi/sd transport cap the remarks blame may be real *there*; it is measurably not the mechanism here), and the 65332/7.43 fit is a fit while the *shape* claim is direct measurement. fw#716's ~79% clock mismatch is orthogonal — every cap quoted is device-reported, not host-timed.
- **Method notes worth keeping.** The raw harness re-used the DTR lesson (`DtrEnable=true`, mirroring `SerialStreamTransport`) and led with a positive control before any probe. New fact: **the IEEE 488.2 error queue survives a CDC close/reopen** — four `-222`s from rounds 2-4 were still poppable from a third, independent port open, so cross-phase queue residue is real (extend the "drain before attributing" rule across process boundaries, not just commands).
- Tests: none run — **no source changed this fire**, so no repo suite and no PR (correct at the cap). Evidence posted on **#390** (`#issuecomment-5204147062`), the issue that landed the rate model via #404; cross-referenced #409/#336/#378/daqifi-desktop#118. Chips filed: `task_1e48142e` (guard `StartStreaming` against the device cap — includes the staleness constraint and an explicit "do not substitute the model"), `task_269c64dc` (correct the `CapabilityRateModel` doc claims).
- Bench hygiene: capability reads, channel enables, and streaming only. **No** reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware / `LAN:*` write; serial-only ⇒ **zero UDP broadcast or WiFi traffic**, no connect-churn exposure. Device healthy at exit: `*IDN?` byte-identical to the round-2 baseline, error queue drained to `0,"No error"` (all four entries were my own over-cap probes), analog enable mask restored to **0**, not streaming. Detached worktree `/tmp/wtrate` and harness `/tmp/ratebench` removed; port confirmed free; main checkout left on `main` with the expected pre-existing `D`/`??` `SESSION_LOG.md` reconciliation from the #438 untrack.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** 25 queued chips. #269's items 1-5 are all addressed — consider proposing it closable; do **not** go back to #344.
- **Unbenched surfaces still remaining:** `Communication/Transport`'s **HID** half (destructive flash path, deliberately skipped), `Device/DeviceErrorThrottle` + `DeviceErrorEventArgs` (internal; real sources need a physical unplug, so synthetic-only for an unattended fire), `Device/Network`'s **write** path (`UpdateNetworkConfigurationAsync`, churn-risky — do not), `Logging` outside `Export`, and the capability document's **non-streaming** blocks (`storage` / `power` / `buffer_ranges_bytes` / `test_patterns`, all captured raw this fire but unexercised).

Priority 3: no regressions. Nothing merged.
- **True-saturation pass: the capability document's non-streaming half** — the surface the last fire explicitly left as "captured raw but unexercised". Harness: a throwaway console app against a detached `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) worktree, USB CDC to the bench Nq1 (fw 3.7.2, hw 2.0.0). Method: pull the live document through the real `InitializeAsync` path, then re-parse `RawJson` independently with `System.Text.Json` and assert **every** parsed property against raw ground truth, rather than against a fixture. **26 checks: 25 pass, 1 fail.**
- **Green, with independent corroboration where one was available.** `identity` — `vendor`/`model`/`variant`/`serial`/`firmware_rev`/`hardware_rev` all match raw exactly, and fw/hw also match the protobuf-sourced `Metadata` values. `storage.sd_supported`=true, corroborated by a live read-only `SYSTem:STORage:SD:SPACe?` that answered with a real ~7.8 GB card. `power.battery_present`/`external_power_supported`=true/true, consistent with the status protobuf (battery percent reported, `PwrStatus=1`). `transports.usb/wifi/ethernet.supported`=true/true/**false**, `usb` corroborated by the session itself being USB CDC — note **false is a real answer here**, not a gap, so the "null means not-stated" contract is genuinely exercised in both directions. `schema_version`=2 / `schema_uri` match. `MergeInto` overlays SD/USB/WiFi and the channel counts (0/0/0 → **16 AI / 0 AO / 16 DIO**, MaxRate 1000 → 22000) and leaves `HasWincWifiModule` board-derived, exactly as its remarks claim. Two consecutive `ReadCapabilityDocumentAsync` calls returned **byte-identical** JSON.
- **THE ONE FAILURE — already known as a chip, now hardware-re-confirmed and, more importantly, TRACKED.** `Identity.Serial` (16-digit uppercase **base-16**) vs `Metadata.SerialNumber` (19-digit **base-10** of protobuf `DeviceSn`, a `ulong`) — proved numerically to be the same 64-bit value in one session. A prior fire found this and filed chip `task_d682cb77`, but **no GitHub issue existed**, so it was living only as a UI affordance. Filed **#446** with the full evidence, the `DeviceIdentity` blast radius (`Key = "sn:" + ToLowerInvariant()` + `OrdinalIgnoreCase` equality can never bridge a base split, and its own doc comment says "Compared case-insensitively"), the suspected-but-**unverified** `HidDeviceFinder` angle (HID serial *descriptor*, not `DeviceSn.ToString()` — would be a live #380 duplicate-detection hole; not benched because reaching HID means bootloader mode = destructive), and three candidate fixes with the ambiguity called out (a 16-digit all-numeric string parses as both hex and decimal). Replaced the old chip with `task_931f8071`, which points at #446; dismissed `task_d682cb77` as superseded. **No serial value written here or in the issue.**
- **Catalogued what the device states and Core does not surface** (recorded in #446 so it is not re-discovered): `identity.usb` → `vid`/`pid`/`class` (a second transport-level discriminator); `storage` → `filesystems`, `max_file_size_bytes`; `power` → `sources`, `otg_output_supported`; `transports.wifi` → `bands`, `modes`, `security`, **`tcp_command_port`, `udp_announce_port`** (device-stated rather than assumed); a fourth `transports.serial_debug` entry with no Core property; top-level `triggers`. Also: `identity.model`="Nyquist"/`variant`="NQ1" vs protobuf `PartNumber`="Nq1" — `DeviceTypeDetector` keys on the latter, so the document's model string is **not** a drop-in for board detection.

Error queue drained **empty** (zero entries — the whole pass provoked no SCPI error). Device answered a full second session identically after the first, which is its own proof of health.
