## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Daqifi.Mcp` end-to-end → two real defects, filed as #447 and #448

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog. Nothing merged this fire (`origin/main` still `4b8eed2`).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked, and re-checked at end of fire.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` → **0 unresolved** on all three (443: 1 thread, 444: 1, 445: 2, every one `isResolved: true`). Every in-place-edited `Code Review by Qodo` body reads `Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0)`. Priority 2: nothing — all three `MERGEABLE`/`BLOCKED` (code-owner gate only), `build` **SUCCESS** on the exact unchanged heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), `origin/main` an ancestor of all three, ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions.
- **The last two fires declared unbenched surfaces "genuinely exhausted" — they were wrong, and the miss is worth remembering: every prior sweep scoped itself to `src/Daqifi.Core`. `src/Daqifi.Mcp` (the agent-facing MCP server, 1020 lines across 5 files) had NEVER been exercised against hardware** — it appears in this log only as unit-test counts ("+23 Mcp on net9") and once as a *hypothetical* shape in the #183 discovery analysis. **When a future fire thinks it is out of surfaces, check the other projects in the solution before believing it.**
- **Method.** Built `Daqifi.Mcp` from a detached `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) worktree — deliberately NOT the globally-installed `daqifi.mcp` **0.28.0** dotnet tool, which is a stale published build and would have tested the wrong code. Drove the real stdio JSON-RPC surface with a throwaway Python MCP client (initialize → `notifications/initialized` → `tools/list` → `tools/call`), so this is the shipping tool contract rather than a direct call into `DaqifiAgent`. Handshake clean: protocol `2025-06-18`, **15 tools** listed. Serial-only discovery (`wifi: false`) throughout ⇒ **zero UDP broadcast**, no connect-churn exposure.
- **FINDING 1 → #448. `discover_devices` clamps the timeout to a floor that can never succeed.** `DaqifiAgent.DiscoverAsync` does `Math.Clamp(timeoutMs, 250, 30_000)`; the serial identify handshake measures **~830 ms**. Rested (3 s) 4-rep sweep: **250/400/500/600/750/780/800/820 ms all found 0/4**; **840 → 3/4**; **860/900/1000/1250/2000 → 4/4**. Successes return **early** — 16 of them spanned 0.803–0.855 s whether the budget was 900 ms or 2000 ms — while failures return exactly at their timeout. So the floor is **3.3x below** the requirement, the tool description advertises "clamped to 250..30000" as if 250 were usable, and the failure is an **empty list with no error**, indistinguishable from "nothing plugged in". Secondary, and the part that kills the obvious workaround: after one short miss, **8 back-to-back calls at 1000 ms and 8 at 2000 ms all returned 0 devices in ~1 ms each** — the timeout argument is ignored entirely inside the ~490 ms port-claim window measured in the #183 fire, so "retry with a longer timeout" does not rescue it; 24 consecutive failures fit inside ~300 ms of wall clock. A 3 s rest restored 2/2.
- **FINDING 2 → #447. The `set_sample_rate` device-cap guard is set-time only, so call ordering defeats it.** First the good news, because it reframes an earlier finding: **the guard is correct and is the only thing in the repo stopping an over-cap stream.** Enforced caps matched the raw-serial measurements from the #390 fire *to the Hz* — **7746** (1 analog ch), **3518** (16 ch), **0** (none) — boundaries exact (cap ACCEPT, cap+1 REJECT), and `RefreshCapabilityDocumentAsync` is **bidirectional** (1→16→1 gave 7746 → 3518 → **7746**), which genuinely closes the cap-staleness window flagged as Finding 4 last fire. With 16 channels it correctly rejects 5000/10000/22000 Hz — every one of which bare `IStreamingDevice.StreamingFrequency` accepts, since that setter validates against `MaxSamplingRate` (22000). **But** `ConfigureAnalog/DigitalChannelsAsync` refreshes the cap and never re-validates the live rate: `configure([0])` → `set_sample_rate(7746)` ACCEPT → `configure([0..15])` leaves `StreamingFrequency` at **7746 against a 3518 cap — 4228 Hz over** — and `ConfigureResult` **reports `sampleRateHz: 7746` back to the agent as if coherent**. Re-requesting that same value is REJECTED, which proves the live value is one the guard would never let you set. `StartLoggingAsync` then hands `streaming.StreamingFrequency` straight to the firmware. Same condition #410 fixed (reject instead of clamp), but #410 only covered "the rate changed", not "the cap changed". Stale value even survives emptying the channel set. Secondary: with 0 channels the cap is 0 and **every** rate including 1 Hz is rejected with "exceeds the maximum 0 Hz" — defensible per the code comment, but the message points the agent away from the actual remedy (enable a channel first).
- **A quiet corroboration worth keeping:** the SCPI error queue drained **empty on the first pop** at end of fire — the whole bench provoked **zero** device-side errors. In the raw-serial #390 fire the same over-cap rates produced `-222`s. That is independent evidence the MCP guard really did stop the traffic client-side rather than merely reporting a rejection.
- Tests: none run — **no repo source changed this fire**, so no repo suite and no PR (correct at the cap). Harness lived entirely in `/private/tmp`. Two issues filed with full evidence: **#447** (rate guard) and **#448** (discovery floor). Chip `task_738fa07d` was hardware-confirmed but under-specified; **replaced** by `task_4c52ecc9` (points at #448, carries the ~830 ms measurement) and the old one dismissed.
- Bench hygiene: discovery, connect, channel enable/disable, and client-side rate probes only. **No** streaming, **no** SD logging (`start_sd_logging` deliberately not run — it writes to the card; the "firmware refuses an over-cap rate" half of #447 is cited from the earlier raw-serial measurement, not re-run), **no** reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware / `LAN:*` write. Device healthy at exit: `*IDN?` answers, analog enable mask back to **0**, error queue `0,"No error"`, not streaming. Detached worktree removed + `git worktree prune`, harness deleted, port confirmed free.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Open issues now **8**. When a PR slot frees, the three well-scoped ones are **#448** (smallest: a constant + a description + a test), **#447**, and **#446** — all fully evidenced, none requiring hardware to implement. Still do **not** go back to #344; #269's items 1-5 are all addressed, consider proposing it closable.
- **Unbenched surfaces after this fire:** `Daqifi.Mcp`'s remaining tools — the **DIO/PWM** group (`set_digital_direction` / `set_digital_output` / `set_pwm_output` / `disable_pwm`, all bench-safe on the loopback rig and now the best next target) and the **SD-logging** pair (writes to the card — treat as out of bounds for an unattended fire). Beyond that, what remains is destructive by nature (WiFi/WINC flash, HID/bootloader enumeration, SD write/format/download) or churn-risky (`LAN:*` writes).

## 2026-08-06 — Fire: shepherd-only at the cap; true-saturation bench of `Daqifi.Mcp`'s DIO/PWM group → two real defects (#449, #450) + a firmware-clock corroboration

- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --prune`. Open loop PRs: **#443, #444, #445** — exactly the 3-PR cap, so **priority 4 suppressed by the cap**, not by an empty backlog. Nothing merged this fire (`origin/main` still `4b8eed2`).
- Priority 1: **NONE, both sources checked, and re-checked at end of fire.** GraphQL `reviewThreads` → **0 unresolved** on all three. Every in-place-edited Qodo summary body reads `Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0)`. Priority 2: nothing — all three `MERGEABLE` (`BLOCKED` = the code-owner gate only), `build` **SUCCESS** on the exact unchanged heads (`0d903a7`, `06bcf8a`, `1967c2e`), ready-notes already posted. Priority 3: no regressions.
- **True-saturation pass: the target the last fire named — `Daqifi.Mcp`'s DIO/PWM tool group.** Method: real stdio JSON-RPC against a `Daqifi.Mcp` built from a detached `origin/main` (`4b8eed2`) worktree (never the stale globally-installed 0.28.0 tool), serial-only discovery throughout ⇒ zero UDP broadcast. **The MCP surface has no way to read a digital input** (15 tools, none stream), so the observer was a separate Core harness that connects after the MCP client disconnects and reads the digital port off a short stream — DIO state was empirically proven to survive the handoff by the inverted negative control. Loopback rig: DIO4→DIO2, DIO5→DIO9; DIO10 (5 V tie) never driven. Phase A contract checks **27/30**; Phase B hardware rounds **6/6 conclusive**.
- **FINDING 1 → #449. Digital writes on a PWM-active channel report success while the firmware ignores them.** `set_pwm_output(5,50%,20Hz)` then `set_digital_output(5,false)` returns `{direction:"Output", outputValue:false}` and `list_channels` reports `direction:Output, outputValue:false, pwmEnabled:true` — while the pin is **byte-identically unchanged** from the no-write control (52.2% vs 52.3% duty, 38 transitions both). Root cause is **Core, not MCP**: `ChannelControlOperations.SetDioValue`/`SetDioDirection` have no `IsPwmEnabled` guard and mirror the level unconditionally; `grep IsPwmEnabled` over Core returns only the interface, the property, and the one assignment in `SetPwmEnabled` — **it is never read as a guard anywhere.** The knowledge already existed: the `set_pwm_output` description says verbatim "While PWM runs, digital direction/state commands for the channel are ignored by the hardware", and `SetPwmEnabled` already blocks the mirror-image case. Filed with the symmetric fix (reject, matching the existing PWM-capability guard) preferred over auto-disable.
- **FINDING 2 → #450. `PwmResult` reports never-commanded defaults as device state.** `disable_pwm(1)` — a channel that is not even PWM-capable — returns `dutyCyclePercent: 50` (`DigitalChannel`'s ctor default). A fresh server process returns `frequencyHz: 1000` (the `DefaultPwmFrequencyHz` seed) on a device whose timer was actually still at 20 Hz. The DTO doc's "**0 when none was set**" is **unreachable** because the seed is 1000. Secondary, isolated with a raw-serial probe against a drained queue: `disable_pwm` on a non-capable channel sends `PWM:CHannel:ENable 1,0`, which the firmware answers `-200 "Execution error"` (capable channel: clean) — that was the **only** device-side error the whole pass provoked, and the tool returns success and leaves it in the queue.
- **FINDING 3 → comment on firmware #716 (CLOSED, so evidence not a reopen). The PWM timer is off by the same 79.4% factor as the streaming ISR** — never measured when #716 was written, and it moves the diagnosis from "streaming timer" to the shared peripheral clock. Host-clock measurement via the loopback (transitions ÷ 2 ÷ host window, so independent of the sample rate being wrong): 20→~15.8 Hz (0.792), 50→39.63 Hz (0.793), 100→79.47 Hz (0.795); same sessions' sampling path 1000→794.6 Hz, 500→398.2 Hz. **Multiplicative, not an offset**, same 0.7937 = 33.33/42 on two independent peripherals. Flagged in the comment as a cheap second observable for verifying the fix — one jumper, and immune to the `TimestampFreq` self-consistency that makes the sampling path look right to the device.
- **Greens worth keeping so they are not re-derived.** `set_digital_output` genuinely moves the pins — matched pair **plus inverted negative control** (drive 4H/5L → bits 2,4 = 100%, bits 5,9 = 0%; drive 4L/5H → exactly inverted). Auto-switch Input→Output on `set_digital_output` works. The documented PWM recovery path (`disable_pwm` → `set_digital_output`) works end-to-end. `disable_pwm` really stops the output and releases the pin. **All** argument validation is client-side and correct with useful messages: non-capable PWM channel, duty 0/101, freq 5/50001, unknown/negative channel, bad direction string. Undocumented-but-working direction aliases `in`/`out` and case-insensitivity. PWM duty is accurate (50%→50.2% at ~20 samples/period; **the 25%→20.0% reading is sampling quantization at 10 samples/period, not a duty error — do not re-file it**).
- Tests: none run — **no repo source changed this fire**, so no repo suite and no PR (correct at the cap). Harnesses lived entirely in `/private/tmp` and are deleted.
- Bench hygiene: DIO writes, PWM on loopback-safe channels, short streams, and read-only SCPI queries only. **No** reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / SD write / firmware / `LAN:*` write; serial-only ⇒ no connect-churn exposure. Board restored to its **exact** pre-run baseline word `0x400` (the 5 V tie on DIO10), all 16 DIO back to Input, PWM off, analog+digital enable sets empty, error queue `0,"No error"`, `*IDN?` answering. Detached worktree removed + `git worktree prune`, port confirmed free, main checkout left on `main` with only the expected pre-existing `D`/`??` `SESSION_LOG.md` reconciliation from the #438 untrack.
- End-of-fire state: still **3 open loop PRs (#443, #444, #445)** — AT THE CAP, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, awaiting the user's review. **Next fire stays shepherd-only until one merges or closes.** Open issues now **10**. When a slot frees, best-scoped first: **#448** (a constant + a description + a test), **#450**, **#449**, **#447**, **#446** — all fully evidenced, none needing hardware to implement. Still do **not** go back to #344; #269's items 1-5 are all addressed, consider proposing it closable.
- **Unbenched surfaces after this fire:** the last fire's warning held — check the whole solution, not just `src/Daqifi.Core`. What is left really is destructive or churn-risky: `Daqifi.Mcp`'s **SD-logging pair** (writes to the card), WiFi/WINC flash, HID/bootloader enumeration, SD write/format/download, `LAN:*` writes. Prefer **re-validating an already-benched surface against a changed assumption** over hunting for a new one.

- **Started at the cap.** `git fetch`; 3 open loop PRs (#443 `0d903a7`, #444 `06bcf8a`, #445 `1967c2e`), all Qodo-clean on **both** sources (GraphQL `reviewThreads` 0 unresolved each; every `Code Review by Qodo` body Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0), findings struck through), CI `build` SUCCESS on all three exact heads, ready-notes posted. - **Killed a stale journal item:** the old "unbenched: `Logging` outside `Export`" entry is **void** — `git ls-tree` shows `Logging/` contains *only* `Export/` (5 files), which was benched in the CsvExporter fire. Don't re-list it.
- **TRUE-SATURATION BENCH — the discovery timeout floor (#448), chosen as a re-validation that de-risks the smallest open ticket** rather than hunting a new surface (per the last fire's standing advice). Harness replicated `DaqifiAgent.DiscoverAsync`'s exact shape (fresh `SerialDeviceFinder` per call + the `DiscoverAsync(TimeSpan)` overload) against a detached `origin/main` worktree. ~70 calls, serial-only ⇒ zero UDP/WiFi. Evidence posted on **#448** (`#issuecomment-5205051331`).
- **Numbers worth keeping.** Requirement is tighter than the earlier "~830 ms": warm/rested **829–842** (n=12), port-cold after a **30 s** idle **824–835** (n=4, so re-enumeration costs *nothing* — no cold-port penalty to budget for), process-cold **858–875** (n=8 separate process starts, ~30 ms of JIT). **One 1096 ms outlier** on the very first call of the first process, **did not reproduce in 10 further process-first calls** — unexplained, and it is the number that kills a 1000 ms floor. Boundary is sharp: **820 → 0/3, 840 → 3/3** with only 14 ms headroom, so the true requirement is in **(820, 840]** and any floor near the median is a latent flake.
- **The load-bearing insight: raising the floor is FREE.** Successes return early and the budget does not affect them — worst-case success was **826–847 ms at every budget from 850 through 5000**. So there is no latency argument for a tight floor. Recommended clamping to **[3000, 30000]**, derived from `SerialDeviceFinder.DefaultPortProbeHardTimeoutMs` (3000) — "never budget less than the time Core itself will spend on one port" — with 1500 as the lowest defensible alternative and 850–1000 explicitly called out as *avoid*.
- **Retry-poisoning quantified, and it does NOT need its own fix.** A timed-out call abandons the process-wide `SerialDeviceFinder.PortClaims` entry; later calls short-circuit and return 0 in ~1 ms. Recovery measured **816 / 870 / 870 ms** across 3 trials = one residual handshake (the abandoned probe completes normally, its continuation releases). **Not** the 30 s `QuarantineRetryTtlMs` — that path is for genuinely wedged ports and never engages on a healthy device. Acceptance criterion established: back-to-back with **zero** rest, **8/8 at 1000 / 1250 / 1500 / 2000**, vs the control **0/8 at 250** (whole burst in 256 ms). Once nothing times out, no claim is abandoned and the poisoning cannot start ⇒ **the floor fix alone is sufficient.**
- **MID-FIRE STATE CHANGE — the user merged #445 then #444, and `origin/main` went from `4b8eed2` to `7965b26`. Main is RED.** Caught only because the end-of-fire re-check re-derived state instead of trusting the start-of-fire snapshot — **keep doing that.** #443's build had flipped to FAILURE on its new head `67f65d9`, which is just a merge of main into the branch.
- **Diagnosed before assuming whose fault it was**, which is what made it correct: ran `FirmwareUpdateServiceTests` against a clean checkout of `origin/main` with **no changes applied** → **5 failures on main itself**. So #443 is an innocent inheritor, not a regression. #444 and #445 were each green on their own head and merged back to back; **neither CI run ever saw the other**, and main's CI is **schedule-only** (runs weekly ~14:12, last real one 2026-08-03) so nothing caught it post-merge. **That gap is structural — expect this class again on any back-to-back merge.**
- **Two distinct causes, not one — and only careful reading separated them.** (1) **Four** tests are stale: #444 made `SYSTem:POWer:STATe 1` the first prep command, so every exact-sequence assertion written before it is missing that entry. Confirmed production-correct rather than assumed, because #444's own `WhenPowerOnBeforeLanUpdateModeDisabled_...` test legitimately omits it by setting `PowerOnWifiModuleBeforeLanUpdateMode = false`. (2) **`WhenCanceledDuringTransparentModeExitSettle_LeavesLanRestoreUnsent` is a genuine interaction**: cancelling inside #444's post-exit settle now falls into #445's failure path, which runs `TryLeaveLanUpdateModeAfterFailureAsync` on its own token and emits a **second** `SetTransparentMode 0` plus `LAN:APPLY`. My first single-cause theory was wrong and the per-test assertion diffs are what corrected it — **get all the diffs before theorising.**
- **Judged the interaction rather than rubber-stamping it.** The behavior is *correct*; the test aged. Its actual guarantee — a cancel must not persist a network configuration — still holds exactly (`LAN:ENAbled`/`LAN:SAVE` never sent); #445's `LAN:APPLY` is the bridge-mode kick, deliberately not the restore triple. Fix records the merged reality in the exact-sequence assertion **and** adds two `DoesNotContain` assertions stating the guarantee independently of the recovery sequence, so the test keeps meaning if that sequence changes. **Left the duplicate `SetTransparentMode 0` alone on purpose** (documented no-op off transparent mode; suppressing it is a behavior change on the WiFi-flash path that cannot be bench-validated non-destructively) and flagged it in the PR for the user's call.
- **PR #451** opened (base main, test-only, 28 insertions / 2 deletions, one file). Full suite green **net9 + net10: 2703 passed / 2 skipped each**, plus 23 Mcp, **0 warnings**. Qodo `/agentic_review` → **Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0), 0 unresolved threads**; CI `build` SUCCESS on `b711eca`; `MERGEABLE`; `origin/main` an ancestor. Ready-note posted. **Not merged.** Also commented on #443 explaining its red build is inherited and will go green on a rerun once #451 lands — **deliberately did NOT rerun #443 yet**, since main is still red and it would just fail again.
- Bench hygiene: discovery probes only (open port + identify handshake). **No** streaming / channel writes / reboot / format / delete / `SD:GET` / firmware / `LAN:*` write; serial-only ⇒ no connect-churn exposure. Device healthy at exit — final rested discovery found the board in 845 ms. Both detached worktrees removed + `git worktree prune`; harness deleted; port free. (The other `.claude/worktrees/*` entries are pre-existing and not mine — leave them.)
- End-of-fire state: **2 open loop PRs — #443 (blocked on main being red) and #451 (the fix)** — so **1 slot free, under the cap**. #444 and #445 merged this fire. **Next fire: re-check whether #451 merged; if it did, `gh run rerun --failed` on #443 first, then priority 4.** Open issues **10**; best-scoped first: **#448** (now fully de-risked — the constant is pinned by this fire's measurements, so it is a constant + a description + a test), **#450**, **#449**, **#447**, **#446**. Still do **not** go back to #344; #269's items 1-5 are all addressed, consider proposing it closable.

Keep preferring re-validation of a benched surface against a changed assumption, and prefer one that **de-risks an open ticket** the way this one did.

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: queue empty → priority 4, new ticket #497 (Send/disconnect race) → PR #503

- **Live state re-derived, not trusted from notes.** `gh pr list --state open --author @me` returned `[]` — the user merged/closed the previous three (#443/#444/#445 era). So 0/3 against the concurrency cap, priorities 1–3 all vacuous, priority 4 is the default. Walked the open backlog (#480–#502 + older), preferred bugs: #497, #496, #495, #494, #493, #492 all unclaimed (no PR, no pushed branch). Picked **#497** — smallest, crispest, fully unit-testable, non-destructive to bench. #496 (streaming hang) and #495 (ConfigureAwait deadlock) are High but carry real design surface (reconnect-crossing semantics; #495's success criteria depend on the #484 `.editorconfig` analyzer that doesn't exist yet) — left for a fire that can give them a whole unit.
- **The bug, confirmed by reading not assuming.** `DaqifiDevice.SendNow` double-read the mutable `_messageProducer` (null-check then deref) while `StopMessagePumps` nulls it from the disconnect thread under no shared lock → `NullReferenceException` out of a public API. Losing the race the other way (field set, producer stopped) → bare `InvalidOperationException("...Call Start() first.")`, the untyped failure #395 existed to kill.
- **Scope check that saved a wrong edit.** `StopMessagePumps` double-reads the same fields, but it runs under `_lifecycleGate` (`DisconnectCore` → `LifecycleGate.Run`), so teardown is serialized against itself and needs no snapshot. Left untouched — the fix stayed confined to `SendNow`.
- **Also learned while scoping:** for the *stream*-constructed `DaqifiDevice`, `CompleteConnect` rebuilds the producer only inside `if (_transport != null)`, so after one Disconnect a stream-backed device permanently falls through to the direct-write path. That's why the stress test uses a fake `IStreamTransport`, not a `MemoryStream` — a MemoryStream device would have exercised the producer path for exactly one cycle and looked green for the wrong reason.
- **Fix:** snapshot the field once (the pattern `TextExchangeEngine.SuspendInboundConsumer` already documents for the consumer field); new `internal static SendViaProducer` translates stopped/disposed producer → `DeviceNotConnectedException(IsShuttingDown: true)` with the original as `InnerException`. `ObjectDisposedException` caught **before** `InvalidOperationException` (it derives from it — broader-first would mislabel disposed as "not running"); a producer's own `DeviceNotConnectedException` rethrown unchanged; non-lifecycle failures (`IOException`) keep their type.
- **Verified the test actually catches the bug** rather than trusting a green run: backed the file up, temporarily reverted `SendNow` to the pre-fix double-read, ran the stress test 5×. It failed **5/5** with the raw `InvalidOperationException` inside ~25 reconnect cycles — so this is a *common* real-world failure, not an exotic interleaving. Restored the fix and re-verified. The NRE half is a nanoseconds-wide window and did not reproduce; the test asserts the negative (no untyped failure escapes) and says so in its own docs rather than pretending to schedule the race.
- **Tests:** 9 new in `DaqifiDeviceSendDisconnectRaceTests.cs` — translation pinned through every branch against a throwing double *and* against a real `MessageProducer<string>` (stopped + disposed), so a change to what the producer throws fails here; plus the reconnect stress loop and a happy-path guard. Full suite green **net9.0** (2902 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2902), 0 failures.
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`:** example CLI built against local core; two full connect → configure → stream → disconnect cycles, exit 0, **396 samples each** — matching this unit's known 79.4% clock ratio (500 × 0.794 ≈ 397). Happy path unregressed. (Aside: `--duration 0 --limit 0` hangs the CLI rather than doing a status-only run; had to kill it. Use a bounded duration. Also `--limit N` silently defeats `--min-samples M` when N < M.)
- **PR #503** opened (base main, "closes #497", marked not-for-merge), `/agentic_review` requested. Nothing merged. Now 1/3 against the cap — the next fire should shepherd #503 if Qodo has landed, otherwise start the next ticket (#496, #495, #494, #493 or #492).

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: Qodo review loop on PR #503 (Send/disconnect race) → clean in 2 rounds

- **Scope: shepherd #503 only.** Started at head `495e80e` with `/agentic_review` already requested. Took **2 Qodo rounds**; ended Qodo-clean and CI-green at head `32ea856`. Nothing merged.
- **Checked both Qodo surfaces every round, as required.** The `Code Review by Qodo` issue comment *and* the GraphQL `reviewThreads` field filtered to `qodo-code-review` + `isResolved:false`. Round 1's finding lived in **both** places, but the thread is what carries the reply/resolve affordance. Confirmed freshness by grepping the reviewed-commit SHA that Qodo embeds in the comment (`<!-- .../commit/<sha> -->` plus its code permalinks) rather than trusting the in-place-edited summary — round 2's body referenced `32ea856` **3×** and `495e80e` **0×**, so it was provably a re-review of the new head, not a stale render.
- **Round 1 — exactly one finding (Medium, `🐞 Bug` + `➹ Performance`): "Stress test retains all exceptions."** `Send_RacingRepeatedDisconnectAndReconnect_OnlySurfacesTypedFailures` appended every sender-thread exception to a shared `List<Exception>` under a lock, analyzed only at the end.
- **Triaged as PARTLY valid, and said so instead of accepting the whole claim.** The retention half was real: a 4 s loop against a losing race holds thousands of exception instances only one of which can fail the test. The **contention half was wrong** — the sender thread was the sole writer and the main thread only read after `Thread.Join`, so every acquisition was uncontended; the lock became *unnecessary*, it was never *costing* anything. Fixed the real part, corrected the wrong part on the thread, then resolved it.
- **Fix (`32ea856`, test-only, 28+/19-):** keep the first untyped failure via `Interlocked.CompareExchange`, count typed ones for diagnostics; the sender raises the `stop` flag the instant something untyped escapes and the **driver loop now watches that flag too**, so a failing run reports immediately instead of cycling a device nobody is sending to for the rest of the budget. Dropped the dedicated `NullReferenceException` assertion as redundant — an NRE is untyped, so it lands in the same check, which names the type it caught.
- **Re-proved the restructured test still catches the bug rather than assuming the refactor was behavior-preserving.** Temporarily reverted `SendNow` to call `producer.Send` directly, ran the stress test **5×: failed 5/5** with the raw `InvalidOperationException` — and now in **~45 ms** per run instead of burning the full 4 s budget, which is the early-stop working. Restored the production file with `git checkout --` (no `git stash` — shared stack across ~30 worktrees) and confirmed `git status` showed only the test file dirty.
- **Round 2 — CLEAN.** Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX issues (0) / Cross-repo conflicts (0) / Skill insights (0), round-1 finding rendered struck through as `✓ Resolved`, **0 unresolved threads** (1 thread total, resolved).
- **Also rewrote the PR #503 description for a human reader** (mid-fire instruction): two lead sections — *What was wrong* (what a library user actually experiences: an NRE or a bare `InvalidOperationException` out of a public API instead of the `DeviceNotConnectedException` they're told to catch, on ordinary disconnects **and** every auto-reconnect) and *How it was fixed*, the latter carrying an explicit "things you may want to push back on" list (exception-type-based translation; the load-bearing `ObjectDisposedException`-before-`InvalidOperationException` ordering; `internal` seam; why it deliberately isn't a lock). Verification/test-counts/bench demoted below. No file-by-file diff restatement.
- **No bench run this fire, and that was the correct call**, not an omission: `git diff 495e80e..32ea856 -- src/Daqifi.Core/` is **empty** — production code was untouched, so the original PR's `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101` validation still applies verbatim. Board never opened; zero destructive ops.
- Tests: full suite green **net9.0** (2902 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2902), 0 failures, 0 warnings. CI `build` **pass** on the exact head `32ea856`; `MERGEABLE` / `CLEAN`. Ready-note posted.
- **Worktree hygiene:** used the existing `eager-lederberg-814d4b` worktree, checked out `fix/send-disconnect-race-497` in it, and restored it to `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` at end of fire. Note the trap that cost a couple of minutes: `cd /Users/tylerkron/projects/daqifi/daqifi-core` in a Bash call lands in the **main** checkout (on `main`), not the worktree — always use the absolute worktree path.

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #503 merged → queue empty → priority 4, new ticket #494 (StatusChanged isolation) → PR #504, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → `[]`; #503 merged (branch deleted on fetch), #497 closed. 0/5 against the cap, priorities 1–3 vacuous, so priority 4. Walked the bug backlog (#496, #495, #494, #493, #492 — all still unclaimed, no PR, no pushed branch) and picked **#494**: High priority, crisp fix shape, unit-testable, non-destructive to bench. Deliberately skipped again for now: #496 (needs reconnect-crossing semantics designed), #495 (its second success criterion depends on the #484 analyzer that doesn't exist yet), #493 (raw-capture locking is a much bigger blast radius and validating it wants live SD work).
- **The bug, read not assumed.** A drop runs `transport.HandleConnectionLost` → `OnTransportStatusChanged` → `Status = Lost` → `StatusChanged` → `BeginReconnectIfEnabled`, all on one thread, with the transport's handle dispose still pending after the raise. `StatusChanged` was the only event on that path raised unguarded, so a throwing subscriber (WPF cross-thread `InvalidOperationException` — the event documents itself as firing on a background thread) skipped the reconnect start entirely AND unwound back into the transport before `port?.Dispose()`. Because the field was already nulled by `Interlocked.Exchange`, `Disconnect()`/`Dispose()` then skipped it too → OS handle claimed for the life of the process.
- **Fix:** `Status` setter routes through a new private `RaiseStatusChanged`, which catches and reports via the existing `RaiseDeviceError` under a new `DeviceErrorSource.StatusNotification = 4` (additive enum member; no exhaustive switch anywhere in Core or Mcp, and the docs table got the new row). Both transports dispose the handle in a `finally`. Isolation applies to **every** transition, not just `Lost` — a throwing subscriber used to make `Connect()` itself throw after the device was already connected.
- **Deliberately NOT done:** per-subscriber isolation. The first handler to throw still ends that notification for the rest of the invocation list, matching `RaiseReconnectEvent`/`RaiseClassifiedEvent`. Called out in the PR's push-back list and in `DEVICE_INTERFACES.md` rather than silently chosen.
- **Verified the tests catch the bug**: backed the three production files up to scratchpad, un-fixed them (no `git stash` — shared stack), re-ran → **10/10 failed**; restored from backup and confirmed `git diff` matched the intended fix.
- **Qodo round 1 — one Medium finding ("Silent swallowed status errors"), triaged VALID after checking rather than reflexively defending.** First instinct was to reply "nothing was lost, it was already swallowed by the watchdog catch" — but tracing all three call paths showed the writer path (`MessageProducer`'s loop-level `catch`) *did* log it, so the new `catch` really did remove the one visible surface. And the repo already has the pattern for exactly this: `DeviceFinderBase.RaiseIsolated` / `DaqifiStreamingDevice.SafeTrace` do a best-effort `Trace.WriteLine` wrapped in its own try/catch for isolated raises in classes with no `ILogger`. Added a local `SafeTrace` twin to each transport (`482390d`) plus a test that captures it through a **synchronized** `TextWriterTraceListener` and asserts with `Contains`, so parallel test traffic can neither corrupt nor flake it. Note for future fires: this is NOT the settled "logging-in-catch rejected (#98)" case — that was about `ILogger` calls; a logger-less class tracing is established here.
- **Qodo round 2 — CLEAN.** Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX issues (0) / Cross-repo conflicts (0) / Skill insights (0); 1 thread total, resolved. Freshness proved by SHA grep on the in-place-edited comment: **5** references to `482390d`, **0** to `21780f5`.
- **Tests:** 11 new (5 transport-side incl. TCP over a real loopback socket where the peer's FIN proves the handle was released, 5 device-side, 1 in `DeviceReconnectTests` for the reconnect start). Full suite green **net9.0** (2913 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2913), 0 failures, 0 warnings. CI `build` **pass** on head `482390d`; `MERGEABLE` / `CLEAN`.
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`:** four connect → `--show-status` → 3 s @ 500 Hz stream → disconnect cycles, two before and two after the review fix (production code changed, so it was re-run). exit 0, 1186–1187 samples (≈79% clock ratio, as known for this unit), `sn` and fw unchanged, clean `Disconnected` through the new raise path. Serial only; no LAN/WiFi traffic, no SD, no reboot, nothing destructive.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `eager-lederberg-814d4b` on `fix/statuschanged-isolation-494`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` at end of fire. Scratchpad backups removed.
- End-of-fire state: **1 open loop PR (#504)**, Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, **not merged** — 1/5 against the cap, 4 slots free. Next fire: re-check #504, then take the next unclaimed bug (#496, #495, #493 or #492).

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: priority 1 — PR #504 was NOT actually clean; two more Qodo rounds (3 and 4) to close it out

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **only #504**. The previous fire logged it "Qodo-clean in 2 rounds" and posted the ready note at 16:19:32 — but a Qodo inline thread landed at **16:19:46, fourteen seconds later**, unresolved and not outdated. The summary comment still read as before. **This is exactly the trap the loop instructions warn about**: the ready note was posted on a round that had not finished, and the summary alone would never have shown it. Priority 1, not priority 4.
- **Round 3 finding A — "Unsafe trace message build" (Medium): VALID, fixed in `8524922`.** Both transports built the trace line as `SafeTrace($"...{ex}")`, so the interpolation ran *outside* `SafeTrace`'s try. Since `ex` came out of a consumer's `StatusChanged` subscriber, it is consumer code too — an exception whose `ToString()` throws escaped the very `catch` that exists to contain the subscriber. Confirmed by reproduction, not assumed: reverted the two production files to `482390d` and the two new tests **failed 2/2** with the escape at `DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler.AppendFormatted` inside `HandleConnectionLost`. `SafeTrace` now takes the `Exception` and composes the whole line inside its guard. Handle release was never at risk (the `finally` covers it) — the damage was to the watchdog / reader thread, which is what the isolation is for.
- **Round 3 finding B — "FIN-only TCP assertion" (Medium): VALID, fixed in `720da1b`.** Both TCP tests proved release by having the peer read EOF; a correct close can surface as a reset on some stacks. Now a shared `AssertPeerObservedTheSocketClose` accepts EOF **or** `ConnectionReset`. **Deliberately still rejects `SocketError.TimedOut`** — a leaked socket sends the peer neither a FIN nor an RST, so broadening to "any SocketException" would have made the test pass on the bug it exists to pin.
- **Round 3 finding C — "Global Trace listener mutation": DECLINED as settled.** Qodo's own relevance note says `● Weak` / "explicitly rejected previously" and cites **#428**. Reasoning posted rather than silently ignored: listener added/removed in a `try`/`finally`, writer is `TextWriter.Synchronized`, assertions are `Contains`, and a `TextWriterTraceListener` only ever appends so it cannot disturb a parallel test. Carried forward un-struck in the summary (Qodo can't tell "declined" from "unaddressed") but **never raised as a thread**, so nothing to resolve. Do not re-litigate on a future fire.
- **Round 4 — CLEAN on head `720da1b`.** Freshness proved three ways: comment updated 16:40:06 (4 min after the `/agentic_review`), bot's "review updated up to latest commit" at 16:40:10, and **20** SHA refs to `720da1b` vs 1 each to the older two. **0 unresolved review threads**; A, B and C's two predecessors all struck through `✓ Resolved`.
- **Tests:** 4 added this fire (2 unrenderable-exception, serial + TCP; plus the reset-tolerant helper reused by both TCP tests) → 13 new on the PR total. Full suite green **net9.0** (2915 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2915), 0 failures, 0 warnings, on both pushes. CI `build` pass on `720da1b`; `MERGEABLE` / `CLEAN`.
- **Bench (non-destructive) re-run because production code changed in `8524922`:** two connect → `--show-status` → 3 s @ 500 Hz → disconnect cycles on fw 3.7.2 `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`. exit 0 both, **1186 / 1187 samples** (matching the earlier baseline's ~79% clock ratio exactly), `sn=9090539562006014104` and fw unchanged, clean `Disconnected`. `720da1b` was test-only, so no second bench needed. Serial only; no LAN/WiFi, no SD, no reboot.
- **PR description updated** to keep it honest for the user: test count 11 → 13, and the TCP wording now says "the peer observing the close" rather than claiming a FIN specifically.
- **Lesson for future fires:** after posting `/agentic_review`, a summary that still reads clean is NOT evidence the round finished — Qodo posts a "Check back in a few minutes, agents are on it" placeholder and edits the summary in place minutes later, and the first edit after a push can be mere bookkeeping (it struck the resolved finding 15 s after my comment, then delivered the real pass 5 min later). Always confirm with the bot's "review updated up to latest commit" comment AND a SHA-ref count, and always check `reviewThreads` separately.

- End-of-fire state: **1 open loop PR (#504)**, genuinely Qodo-clean this time, CI green, ready-noted, **not merged** — 1/5 against the cap, 4 slots free. Next fire: re-check #504, then take the next unclaimed bug (#496, #495, #493 or #492).

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #504 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #492 (deferred-send backlog cap) → PR #505, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **only #504**, head `720da1b`, all 3 review threads resolved, CI `build` SUCCESS, ready note at 16:43:59 with the last Qodo comment at 16:40:10 — i.e. the previous fire's fourth round really did settle, nothing landed after it. Priorities 1–3 vacuous → priority 4, 1/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice.** Walked the remaining bugs (#496, #495, #493, #492). Took **#492** (Medium, but the only one of the four with no prior blocker): crisp, entirely unit-testable, no destructive bench need. The other three stay deferred for the reasons already logged — #496 needs reconnect-crossing semantics designed, #495's second success criterion depends on the #484 analyzer that doesn't exist, #493's raw-capture locking wants live SD work and a much bigger blast radius. **Read #493's code before deferring it again this time**: `ExecuteRawCaptureAsync` really is missing the whole `ExecuteAsync` lock protocol (no `WaitForOperationLockAsync`, no `HoldsOperationLock` nesting, no `MarkOperationInFlight`), so it is tractable — roughly "mirror `ExecuteAsync`'s preamble" — and a future fire should take it rather than skip it reflexively.
- **The bug.** `_deferredSends` is an unbounded `Queue<Action>`; `MaxDeferredSendsPerFlush = 64` bounds only how many replay per pass, not how many accumulate. A 10 Hz sender through a 30-minute SD download parks ~18,000 closures and then replays every one at the device in a burst.
- **Fix (`764be40`).** `TryDeferSend` now enforces `MaxDeferredSends` (public `DefaultMaxDeferredSends = 1024`, `internal virtual` seam for tests, clamped to ≥1) with **drop-oldest**, counts into a new public `DroppedDeferredSendCount` (mirrors `DroppedLiveSampleCount`), and logs **one** warning per overflowing backlog via a `_deferralOverflowReported` flag cleared at all three sites that null the backlog. The cap is read once *outside* `_deferralGate` so the log can't report a different number than was enforced, and the log itself is emitted after the gate is released — `Send()` must not block.
- **Deliberately NOT done** (all called out in the PR's push-back list): per-command coalescing (needs a message identity the outbound layer doesn't model), a configurable cap (no `DeviceConnectionOptions` knob), and raising `SendFailed` on a drop (would put consumer code under the deferral gate).
- **Verified the tests catch the bug**: backed `DaqifiDevice.cs` up to scratchpad, removed the cap enforcement, re-ran → **4 of the 6 new tests failed**, and the 2 that are *guards* (backlog under the cap drops nothing; an idle device drops nothing) correctly still passed. Restored from backup.

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #504/#505 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #493 (raw-capture operation lock) → PR #506, Qodo-clean in 3 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#504** (head `720da1b`) and **#505** (head `3e6d4d3`). Both: CI `build` SUCCESS, all review threads resolved, ready-noted, and — checked, not assumed — **nothing landed after either ready note** (#504's last Qodo comment 16:40:10 vs note 16:43:59; #505's last Qodo comment 17:14:17 and summary `updated_at` 17:14:12 vs note 17:20:38). Priorities 1–3 vacuous → priority 4, 2/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice: #493**, the pick the previous fire recommended. Remaining bugs were #496, #495, #493; the other two stay deferred for the reasons already logged (#496 needs reconnect-crossing semantics designed, #495's second criterion depends on the #484 analyzer that does not exist). #493 turned out to be **fully unit-testable and non-destructive on the bench** — the earlier "wants live SD work" worry was wrong, because the lock protocol is observable through a scripted transport without ever issuing `SD:GET`.
- **The bug.** `TextExchangeEngine.ExecuteRawCaptureAsync` suspended the protobuf consumer and handed out the transport stream while excluding **nothing**. A concurrent text query took the exchange lock *uncontended* (the capture never held it) and started a second reader on the same stream; a bare `Send()` was not even deferred, because `_operationInFlight` was false, so its bytes went out mid-transfer and the reply landed inside the downloaded `.bin`. The only gate held was `_sdDownloadGate`, which serializes downloads against each other and nothing else.
- **Fix (`4ba16d2`).** Raw capture now runs the same lock protocol `ExecuteAsync` does: nested re-entry via `HoldsOperationLock`, `EnterOperationLockOwnership()` (which is what starts deferring other flows' sends), all validation moved **inside** the lock (`IsShuttingDown` / `IsConnected` / transport null / `transport.IsConnected` — the #186 TOCTOU, wider here because captures are long), an outbound drain (#342) so a command queued microseconds before the capture cannot be written into it, and release on every exit path.
- **Deliberately NOT done, and called out in the PR's push-back list:** nothing was done about an abandoned transfer holding the lock. Documented honestly instead — it is cancelled on the way out so the usual case unwinds, and a read that never returns keeps the *stream* too, so a query blocked on that lock was blocked on a stream nothing could have used anyway.
- **Verified the tests catch the bug**: backed the engine up to scratchpad, reverted it to `origin/main`, re-ran → **6 of 10 failed**, and the 4 that are *guards* (nested re-entry, release on throw, release after validation failure, `Send` still returns immediately) correctly still passed. Restored from backup — no `git stash` (shared stack across ~30 worktrees).
- **Qodo round 1 — two findings.** (A) *Unguarded nested consumer swap* (**High**): **VALID, taken** (`394a1da`). The capture was the one swap not covered by the `IsInsideTextExchange` AsyncLocal, and the lock makes it worse — a nested call from the same flow now runs nested *by design*, so the inner swap's `finally` would restart the protobuf consumer while the outer capture still owned the stream. Reused the existing flag (already documented as "a consumer swap is in progress on this flow (nesting is a bug)") rather than adding Qodo's suggested new `IsInsideConsumerSwap`; broadened its docs in three places. (B) *Unbounded deferred-send backlog* (Medium): **real but already fixed on PR #505 / issue #492** — declined here with reasoning, plus the honest admission that this PR widens the window even though it does not create it, and an explicit **merge-order note (#505 before #506)** in both the thread and the PR body.
- **Round 1 fix re-proved**: reverted the engine to `4ba16d2` (lock present, guard absent) → the 2 new nesting tests **failed 2/2**; both pass with the guard, and each also asserts the flag is cleared afterwards.
- **Qodo round 2 — one Medium, *Misleading re-entrancy message*: VALID, taken** (`5dcd8e2`). The message technically covered both cases but would print a sentence about text exchanges to someone debugging a nested *capture*. Reworded to name the state ("already inside a consumer swap — another raw capture, or a text exchange") rather than guess the cause. Kept the substring `not re-entrant` so the pre-existing `DaqifiDeviceTextCommandLockTests` assertion still holds.
- **Qodo round 3 — CLEAN on head `5dcd8e2`.** 0 unresolved threads; both taken findings struck `✓ Resolved`. Freshness proved two ways: the bot's "review updated up to the latest commit `5dcd8e2`" and **20** SHA refs to `5dcd8e2` vs 1 each to the two older commits. **Settle re-check at +11 min re-read both surfaces — the summary comment was byte-identical (`diff -q`) and threads still 0.** Note for future fires: the declined #492 finding stays **un-struck** in the summary forever (Qodo cannot distinguish "declined" from "unaddressed"), but its thread is resolved — same shape as #504's Trace-listener decline. Do not re-litigate.

Full suite green **net9.0** (2914 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2914), 0 failures, 0 warnings, on all three pushes.

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #506 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #496 (live enumeration never ends) → PR #507, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#506** only (head `5dcd8e2`, CI `build` SUCCESS, 3 threads all resolved, ready-noted 18:15:33Z with nothing landing after it — last Qodo comment 18:04:54Z). #504/#505 had merged. Priorities 1–3 vacuous → priority 4, 1/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice: #496**, which the previous fire had deferred as "needs reconnect-crossing semantics designed". The design turned out to be the *simple* answer, not the hard one: the ticket's own success criterion ("enumeration ends on unplug") rules resume-across-reconnect out, so ending deterministically at the drop is both required and simpler. #495 stays deferred (its second criterion depends on the #484 analyzer that does not exist).
- **The bug.** `LiveSampleStream`'s bounded buffer was completed only by the enumerator's own `finally`. Nothing else ever called `TryComplete()`, so an `await foreach` parked when the cable was pulled waited forever — no samples, no exception, no loop exit — still subscribed to every `IChannel.SampleReceived`. `await using var device` could not rescue it: `DisposeAsync` runs only *after* the loop exits.
- **The contract chosen** (one crisp rule, documented in the XML docs and `docs/DEVICE_INTERFACES.md` as a table): an enumeration is bound to the connected session it started in. Buffered samples drain first, then — teardown you asked for (`Disconnect`/`DisconnectAsync`/`Dispose`) ends the loop **normally**; a drop nobody asked for (`Lost`/`Failed`/anything not `Connected`) throws **`DeviceNotConnectedException`**; starting on a non-connected device throws on the first `MoveNextAsync`; cancellation is unchanged. Faulting on a drop is deliberate — a silent end would make an acquisition cut short by an unplug indistinguishable from a complete one — and *not* faulting on a deliberate disconnect is equally deliberate, or every clean shutdown would end in an exception.
- **Auto-reconnect deliberately does NOT resume an enumeration.** Ending at the drop makes the outcome identical whether or not reconnect is enabled, and `_channelPopulator.Populate` only *sometimes* reuses channel objects, so a resumed enumeration could silently yield nothing forever. Called out in the PR's push-back list.
- **Implementation (`2c063ee`).** `LiveSampleStream` now registers each enumeration (lock-guarded list) before subscribing, ends them from two device-driven entry points, and rethrows after the drain. Two **internal** `virtual` hooks on `DaqifiDevice` carry the signals — `OnConnectionStatusChanged` (from the `Status` setter) and `ReleaseDerivedResources` (from `ReleaseResources`, which is the one teardown a status transition cannot cover: disposing a never-connected device transitions nowhere). Internal, so **zero public API growth**; `internal virtual` already has precedent here (`SdCardDownloadTimeout`). `AllowSynchronousContinuations = false` spelled out explicitly with a comment — ending an enumeration now runs on the watchdog's thread, and the consumer's `await foreach` body must not run there.
- **Verified the tests catch the bug**: backed the three production files up to scratchpad, reverted them + `LiveSampleStreamTests` to `origin/main`, re-ran → the first test **failed at 5.3 s and the run then wedged permanently** (a parked async iterator's `DisposeAsync` never returns), which is the reported failure mode exactly. Killed the test host, restored from backup — no `git stash` (shared stack across ~30 worktrees).
- **Qodo round 1 — one Medium, *Uncaught hook aborts transition*: VALID, taken** (`b3040a0`). The new hook was called bare from the `Status` setter, so a throwing override would escape the transition and skip `RaiseStatusChanged` — the exact #494 failure, on the same drop path where it also skips the reconnect start. Now routed through a `RaiseConnectionStatusChanged` wrapper mirroring `RaiseStatusChanged` (reports `DeviceErrorSource.StatusNotification`, transition completes). Gave the **release** hook the same treatment unprompted: it runs from `Dispose`, and a throwing `Dispose` hides the handles it did release. Two new tests pin both; `DeviceErrorSource.StatusNotification`'s docs now say Core's own reaction is isolated there too.
- **Qodo round 2 — CLEAN on head `b3040a0`.** 0 unresolved threads, round-1 finding struck `✓ Resolved`, "review updated up to the latest commit `b3040a0`" plus 6 SHA refs to `b3040a0` vs 1 to `2c063ee`. **Settle re-check at +4 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), threads still 0.**
- **Tests:** 21 new — 11 in `DaqifiStreamingDeviceLiveStreamTerminationTests` (drop faults, buffered-samples-first, sync/async disconnect, dispose, not-connected start, disposed start, new enumeration on the restored session, cancellation still wins, plus the two hook-isolation tests) and 10 added to `LiveSampleStreamTests` (unsubscribe on the drop path, deliberate vs dropped, drop-then-teardown keeps reporting the drop, release with no transition, latched post-release throw, concurrent enumerations, no-op after the loop ended, arriving at `Connected` leaves a healthy loop alone). Full suite green **net9.0** (2942 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2942), 0 failures, 0 warnings, on both pushes. Also had to make `FakeHost.IsConnected` settable (was `throw new NotSupportedException()`), justified in a doc comment: reading connection state is now in the collaborator's remit.
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`:** the example CLI has no `StreamSamplesAsync` command, so a throwaway harness at `/private/tmp/livebench` (referencing the local core csproj) exercised the new contract on hardware. Run before *and* after the round-1 production fix: 1190/1189 live samples at 500 Hz in 3 s (this unit's known ~79% clock ratio), `DisconnectAsync` while the `await foreach` was parked ended the loop in 516 ms / 9 ms with **no exception**, then a disconnected start threw `DeviceNotConnectedException(IsShuttingDown=false)` and a disposed start `IsShuttingDown=true`. CLI health run after: `sn=9090539562006014104`, fw unchanged, 1188 samples, clean `Disconnected`. No reboot/format/delete/`SD:GET`/firmware/LAN writes; serial only ⇒ zero WiFi connect-churn exposure. The **unplug** path itself is covered by the scripted-transport tests, not by physically pulling the cable (user away).
- **CLI note for future fires:** `--duration 0` streams forever — it is not "no streaming". Use an explicit small duration for a health check, and never pipe the CLI through `grep` when you may need to kill it (the pipe buffers and you lose all output).
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `eager-lederberg-814d4b` on `fix/live-sample-stream-completion-496`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` at end of fire. Scratchpad backups and `/private/tmp/livebench` removed. Note: `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` was an old `main` snapshot (`3aa294d`) and is now at `origin/main` — local-only branch, harmless.

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #506/#507 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #495 (connect-path ConfigureAwait) → PR #508, Qodo-clean in 3 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#506** (head `5dcd8e2`) and **#507** (head `b3040a0`). Both CI `build` SUCCESS, 0 unresolved threads, ready-noted, and — checked, not assumed — nothing landed after either note (#506 last Qodo 18:04:54Z vs note 18:15:33Z; #507 last Qodo 19:08:44Z vs note 19:14:37Z). Priorities 1–3 vacuous → priority 4, 2/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice: #495**, the last item on the `bug` label. Prior fires deferred it because its second success criterion ("analyzer rule active so a naked await fails the build") depends on the `.editorconfig` from #484, which does not exist. That turned out to be the wrong reason to skip it: **`src/Daqifi.Core/.editorconfig` with one rule (`dotnet_diagnostic.CA2007.severity = warning`) is all it needs**, `TreatWarningsAsErrors` is already true in the csproj so a warning IS a build error, and #484 can extend the same file later. Do not defer #495-shaped tickets on "the config ticket hasn't landed" again.
- **The bug.** `DaqifiDeviceFactory.ConnectTcp/ConnectSerial` and `device.Connect()` block on their own async work; nothing underneath used `ConfigureAwait(false)`, so a WPF/WinForms caller on the UI thread froze permanently — the continuation posted back to a context already blocked inside `GetResult()`. Serial hit it as soon as retry engaged (first `Open()` fails after a re-plug → `await Task.Delay(backoff)` never resumes).
- **Scope decision: library-wide sweep, not just the connect path** (`0a9600f`). Enumerating "which awaits are reachable from a blocking facade" goes stale on the next refactor, and it is the wrong rule for a library anyway. **Method that made 90 edits safe:** turn CA2007 on first, let the compiler enumerate the sites, script the mechanical insertion, then build until zero. Numbers: 317 await lines in Core, 223 already had `ConfigureAwait`, **90 CA2007 hits across 14 files**. `DaqifiDevice.cs`, `DaqifiStreamingDevice.cs` and the Pic32 files had **zero** — their grep hits were all XML doc prose.
- **Two script traps worth remembering.** (1) The expression-end scanner treated `!` as a binary operator, so `await _udpClient!.SendAsync(...)` became `await _udpClient.ConfigureAwait(false)!.SendAsync(...)` — compiled fine, completely wrong; 5 sites in `UdpTransport.cs`, fixed by hand. Always eyeball the whole generated diff. (2) An **incremental** `dotnet build` emits no warnings on a cache hit, so the first two attempts to harvest CA2007 locations silently returned 0 rows — use `--no-incremental`.
- **`await using` needs restructuring, not a suffix.** 11 sites (5 SD parsers, 4 in `GitHubFirmwareDownloadService`, 1 in `SdCardOperations`). `await using var x = expr.ConfigureAwait(false)` retypes `x` to `ConfiguredAsyncDisposable`, so the variable is declared first and a `await using (x.ConfigureAwait(false)) { ... }` block wraps the body. Disposal order is preserved, including "disposes before the enclosing `catch` runs" (which `SdCardOperations`' temp-file `File.Delete` depends on). `await foreach` is the easy case — `.ConfigureAwait(false)` on the `IAsyncEnumerable` works as a suffix.
- **Verified the tests catch the bug**: backed the 14 changed files up to scratchpad, `git checkout origin/main --` them, parked the `.editorconfig`, re-ran → **4 of the 5 real tests failed** with the reported symptom (TCP connect/disconnect, serial connect-with-retry, the retry executor's backoff delay, `ConnectTcp`). The UDP test passes either way — its awaits complete synchronously — so it is a **guard, not a regression catcher**, and the PR says so. Restored from backup; no `git stash` (shared stack across ~30 worktrees).
- **Test harness worth reusing** (`SynchronizationContextDeadlockTests`): a `BlockedSynchronizationContext` that queues `Post` and never pumps, driven on a dedicated background thread with `Thread.Join(timeout)` as the verdict, plus a `DrainUntil(thread)` that runs the queued continuations from the test thread on failure so a genuine deadlock does not leak a blocked thread into the rest of the run. Includes a **self-check that asserts a naked `await` still deadlocks** — without it the other six could pass vacuously. Note: `xUnit1031` rejects `Task.Result`/`.Wait()` in a test body, so the loopback tests take the connection from the **listen backlog** with no explicit `AcceptTcpClientAsync`.
- **Qodo round 1 — one Medium, *Undocumented event thread-affinity*: TAKEN as a doc fix** (`3dc1c18`). Corrected the premise in the reply: the events were never on the caller's thread anyway (drops come from the watchdog/reader thread, reconnects from the supervisor), so this PR removes the last exception rather than creating a hazard. Added `<remarks>` to `IStreamTransport`, `IUdpTransport` **and** `IDevice.StatusChanged` — the last one unprompted, because documenting only the transports would leave the event most consumers actually subscribe to silent on the same question. No behaviour change: marshalling notifications back to a captured context is the exact coupling that caused #495.
- **Qodo round 2 — one Medium, *Threadpool claim inaccurate*: VALID against my own round-1 text, taken** (`fce8613`). `ConfigureAwait(false)` declines the captured context; it does **not** force a thread hop, so an already-completed dial raises `StatusChanged` inline on the calling thread. All three remarks now say "no particular thread is guaranteed", name both outcomes for a connect, and spell out that `ConfigureAwait(false)` only declines the context.
- **The "bookkeeping edit" trap fired again and was caught.** 19 s after the round-2 `/agentic_review`, the summary comment updated to Bugs (0) **and already carried 4 SHA refs to the new head** — it would have read as a clean round. The real pass landed **5 minutes later** with Bugs (1). Neither "0 unresolved threads" nor a fresh SHA ref is sufficient on its own; wait for the bot's separate *"review updated up to the latest commit"* comment.
- **Qodo round 3 — CLEAN on head `fce8613`.** Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0); 0 unresolved threads; both findings struck `✓ Resolved`. Freshness: bot's latest-commit note + **20** SHA refs to `fce8613` vs 1 each to the older two. **Settle re-check at +5 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), threads still 0, nothing posted in between.** CI `build` SUCCESS; MERGEABLE / CLEAN.
- **Tests:** 7 new in `SynchronizationContextDeadlockTests` (2 harness self-checks, TCP connect+disconnect, serial connect-with-retry, `ConnectRetryExecutor` backoff, UDP open/close, `DaqifiDeviceFactory.ConnectTcp`). Full suite green **net9.0** (2928 Core + 43 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2928), 0 failures, 0 warnings, on all three pushes.
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`:** connect → `--show-status` → 3 s @ 500 Hz → disconnect (**1188 samples**, this unit's known ~79% clock ratio), `--discover-serial` (found Nq1, `sn=9090539562006014104`), `--sd-list` (45 files) and `--sd-storage` (7.80 GB free/540,672 used) as separate invocations — the last two cover the SD text-exchange path the change touches. No `SD:GET`, no delete/format, no reboot, no firmware, no LAN writes; serial only ⇒ zero WiFi connect-churn exposure. **Both Qodo fixes were XML-doc-only** (`git diff 0a9600f..fce8613 -- src/Daqifi.Core/` has no non-comment line), so no re-bench — stated explicitly in the ready note rather than left silent.

## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #506/#507/#508 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #486 (serial probe teardown) → PR #509, Qodo-clean in 4 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#506** (`5dcd8e2`), **#507** (`b3040a0`), **#508** (`fce8613`). All CI `build` SUCCESS, 0 unresolved threads, ready-noted; #508's only post-note comment (20:07:44Z) is Qodo *agreeing* the re-check is clean, not a finding. #506's summary still reads Bugs (1) — the declined #492 finding that stays un-struck forever; do not re-litigate. Priorities 1-3 vacuous → priority 4, 3/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice: #486** (High priority, `perf`). The `bug` label is empty, so this walked the perf backlog; #486 was picked over #482/#483 because it is bench-*measurable* on the board, non-destructive (discovery only), and self-contained.
- **The issue's diagnosis was wrong, and measuring first is what caught it.** #486 blamed a 1s `ReadTimeout` making `StopSafely(500)` fail. Instrumented replica said otherwise: setting the read timeout to 150ms changed the 502ms teardown *not at all*. The real cause: the "status arrived" `TaskCompletionSource` had **inline continuations** and is completed from inside the consumer's `MessageReceived` dispatch, i.e. ON the reader thread — so the poll-loop exit, the device-info build and the whole `finally` resumed there, and the `finally`'s first act is `StopSafely`, which **joins that very thread**. A self-join can never succeed: it burned the full 500ms every time and returned with the reader still live over a port about to be closed. Proved by printing `Thread.CurrentThread.Name` at teardown → `MessageConsumer-DaqifiOutMessage`.
- **Both halves were needed.** With the TCS fixed but the read timeout left at 1s, the replica measured `consumerStop=503` + `consumerDispose=508` ≈ **1000ms** — *worse* than the 500ms it replaced, because the join is now honest and the reader is parked in a 1s read.
- **Phase breakdown worth keeping** (replica, real Nq1): open 4ms / wake 202ms / consumer start ~1ms / **response 2ms** / teardown. The device answers in ~2ms, so after this fix the probe is dominated by the 200ms `DeviceWakeUpDelayMs` and the 55ms DTR-settle+close. Both deliberately untouched — device-behaviour knobs, not teardown.
- **Deliberately did NOT do the issue's fix #2** (return the device early, tear down in the background). It would be faster, but callers *connect to the port they just discovered*, and on macOS a second open of a `/dev/cu.*` still held by the probe fails. Preserving "the port is closed when `DiscoverAsync` returns" was validated: discover-then-immediately-open succeeded 10/10 in 1-4ms.
- **Refactor that made it testable:** `RequestDeviceStatusAsync(Stream, CancellationToken)` extracted from `TryGetDeviceInfoAsync` (which keeps the port lifecycle). A scripted `Stream` can now drive the exchange with no hardware. Also removed a real cross-thread local (`statusMessage`) by having the TCS carry the message.
- **Qodo round 1 — *Exception-driven polling overhead*: VALID, taken** (`99531c8`). The blanket 50ms read timeout made a silent port wake 20x/s, each ending in a thrown `TimeoutException`. **Measured before deciding** (real consumer over an idle bench port, 2s window, idle CPU baseline subtracted): 1000ms → ~2 timeouts, **5/13ms** reader CPU; 50ms → ~40 timeouts, **178/184ms**. Rather than compromise at 100ms (which put `DiscoverAsync` back over 400ms), the timeout is now shortened **only after the device identifies**, from inside the status dispatch — on the reader thread *between two reads*, the one moment it can change with no read in flight under the old value. Silent path byte-for-byte unchanged; finding closed, not traded.
- **Qodo round 2 — *ReadTimeout not restored*: VALID, taken** (`407f83a`). The method documents the stream as caller-owned and left open, then leaked the 50ms. My own `CanBeRunTwiceOverTheSameStream` was already exercising the leak. Restored in the `finally` **strictly after** the consumer join (restoring earlier gives the benefit straight back); both directions best-effort; writes only when the value actually differs, so a silent probe leaves the stream untouched.
- **Qodo round 3 — *Timeout assertion too weak*: VALID, taken** (`c3cc25c`). `CanBeRunTwiceOverTheSameStream` asserted `ReadsIssuedWithTimeout.Take(1)`, which only covers the *first* exchange — it could not catch the regression it exists for. Now indexes the second exchange's opening read explicitly; with the restore deleted it fails too.
- **CI went red on `407f83a` — my own flaky assertion, not a regression.** `Cancelled_GivesUpEarlyAndStillTearsDown` asserted no retry had gone out yet, racing the 300ms `RetryIntervalMs` timer against wherever the scheduler put the cancellation; net10.0 on the runner lost it. Replaced with `Assert.InRange(count, 1, 2)` — the `MaxRetries` bound, which always holds. **Lesson: never assert an exact send count against a retry cadence.**
- **Qodo round 4 — CLEAN on head `c3cc25c`.** Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0); 0 unresolved threads; round-2 finding struck `✓ Resolved`. Freshness: bot's latest-commit note + **22** SHA refs to `c3cc25c` vs 1 each to the three older commits. **Settle re-check at +5 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), threads still 0, nothing posted in between.** CI SUCCESS; MERGEABLE / CLEAN.
- **Tests:** 11 new in `SerialProbeTeardownTests`, three of them proven regression catchers by reverting the fix (2/2 runs each): `DoesNotCompleteOnTheReaderThread` and `WhenItReturns_ReaderThreadHasExited` (revert the TCS flag), `AfterIdentifying_ShortensTheReadTimeoutForTeardown` (delete the shorten call), `AfterIdentifying_RestoresTheReadTimeoutItFound` + `CanBeRunTwiceOverTheSameStream` (delete the restore call). **Deliberately no wall-clock assertions** — they assert the reader thread has exited *at the moment the exchange completes* (sampled via an `ExecuteSynchronously` continuation, because an assertion made after `await` passes either way) and that the completion did not run on a reader thread. Full suite green net9.0 (2932 Core + 43 Mcp) and net10.0 (2932), 0 failures, 0 warnings, on all four pushes.
- **Also pinned a pre-existing behaviour rather than changing it:** cancellation during the response wait returns **null**, not OCE — `Task.WhenAny` hands back the cancelled delay instead of throwing. Callers are unaffected (`ProbeSafelyAsync` watches the same token). Test comment says so, so a future refactor has to choose deliberately.
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`:** `DiscoverAsync` **834/836/839ms → 397/380/372/373/373/382ms** under matched conditions (2.5s spacing so every run pays the macOS `ioreg` refresh, exactly like the baseline runs). Warm-descriptor-cache runs 312-320ms. Abandoned-claim drain **490ms → 53-58ms**. 16/16 back-to-back zero-delay sweeps found the device; 10/10 discover-then-immediately-open in 1-4ms. Example CLI: `--discover-serial`, `--show-status`, 3s @ 500 Hz (1186/1187 samples, this unit's ~79% clock ratio), `--sd-list` (45 files) — `sn=9090539562006014104`, firmware unchanged, clean `Disconnected`. Serial only; no reboot/format/delete/`SD:GET`/firmware/LAN writes. Re-run after the round-2 *production* fix; round-3 push was test-only (`git diff 407f83a..c3cc25c` = 1 test file), stated on the PR rather than left silent.
- **Bench-harness gotcha worth remembering:** `MacOsUsbPortDescriptorProvider` caches `ioreg` for **2000ms** (`CacheTtlMs`). A measurement loop whose period straddles that TTL produces perfectly *alternating* fast/slow results (~85ms apart) that look like a bimodal bug. Space runs >2.5s for cold-cache numbers, <2s for warm.
- **Criterion 3 (lower the MCP discovery floor) deliberately NOT done.** Re-measured and documented instead: the floor stays 1000ms because it must cover the slowest supported path (several candidate ports; platforms still running an uncached per-port descriptor query — #487), not one macOS host with a warm cache. Lowering it on the fast case is how you land back in #448. The stale "~830 ms" figure was removed from `DaqifiAgent`, its test comment, and the finder's own docs.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `eager-lederberg-814d4b` on `perf/serial-probe-teardown-486`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` at end of fire. Scratchpad backups and `/private/tmp/discbench` removed.
- End-of-fire state: **4 open loop PRs (#506, #507, #508, #509)**, all Qodo-clean, CI green, ready-noted, **not merged** — 4/5 against the cap, 1 slot free. Next fire: re-check all four, then take the next unclaimed ticket from the perf/chore backlog — **#487** is the natural follow-on (this fire's data already shows the descriptor query is the remaining discovery cost on macOS, and it gates lowering the MCP floor); otherwise #489/#490/#491, or the chores #481-#484.

- **The `bug` label is empty, so this walked the perf/chore backlog — and three High/Medium candidates were triaged OUT before landing on #488. Record the reasons; do not re-derive them.**
  - **#485 (High, ~700 ms per SCPI text exchange) — blocked on a public-API decision, needs the user.** Its two headline criteria (`<200 ms` per exchange, `InitializeAsync` −1 s) both depend on the "let callers short-circuit the inactivity tail" bullet, and there is no non-breaking way to thread a terminator predicate through: `DaqifiDevice.ExecuteTextCommandAsync` is `protected virtual` and **~20 test doubles override that exact signature** to return canned lines, so adding a parameter breaks them all (and any external subclass), while adding an *overload* silently bypasses those overrides and runs the real engine against a null transport. Measured cost budget for a device answering in ~25 ms: protobuf-consumer stop ~250 ms avg (the in-flight read's 500 ms timeout is fixed once issued — the #486 trick of shortening it from the reader thread does **not** apply, there is no dispatch to hook on an idle device), `Task.Delay(50)`, ~25 ms reply, ~275 ms tail (250 ms inactivity + 50 ms poll granularity), text-consumer stop ~125 ms. The non-breaking subset (event-driven collection, reader-thread timeout shortening for the text consumer, `Thread.Sleep(100)`×3 → async) is worth ~150-200 ms of the ~725 ms and meets criterion 3 only. **Also worth knowing:** an exchange whose setup sends only non-query commands (the init sequence) waits the FULL `responseTimeoutMs` — 1000 ms on the init path — because `hasReceivedAny` never goes true. That is where criterion 2's second would come from, and it is a call-site tuning decision, not an API one.
  - **#489 (High, SD parsers materialize the whole file) — changes a public runtime contract.** True laziness means `ParseAsync(Stream)` can no longer tolerate the caller disposing the stream on return, and `Samples` stops being re-enumerable for the `Stream` overload (the path overload could reopen per enumeration). `SdCardLogSession` documents "produced lazily as the file is read", so the *docs* already promise it — but shipping it silently breaks any consumer relying on today's eager behaviour. Needs the user's call on the lifetime semantics.
  - **#490 (Medium, per-frame streaming allocations) — the issue's proposed fix is unsafe as specified.** It says to cache the sorted active-analog array and invalidate it with a version counter bumped from `PopulateChannelsFromStatus` / `ChannelControlOperations`. But **`IChannel.IsEnabled` is `{ get; set; }` on the public interface** and `AnalogChannel`'s setter is a plain lock-guarded assignment with no notification — so `channel.IsEnabled = true` from user code would leave the cache stale and the decoder would map analog values to the **wrong channels**, silently. Items 2 (bulk append / span parse) and 3 (wrapper allocations behind the subscriber check) are safe and independent; item 1 needs a channel-change notification designed first.
- **Ticket taken: #488** (Low priority, but the only candidate that was fully closable, bench-measurable and free of API entanglement).
- **The fix reuses Core's `AllTransportsDeviceFinder` rather than a local `Task.WhenAll`** — the primitive already existed (concurrent fan-out + transport-prefixed dedup + per-finder failure isolation), and adopting it fixes a second defect for free: a WiFi probe that throws (host with no usable network) used to come straight out of `discover_devices` and lose the USB device found alongside it. Two deliberate behaviour changes, both stated in the PR: a failing transport no longer sinks the pass, and a device one transport reports twice collapses to one (the same unit on **two** transports still stays two entries, because `ContinuousDeviceFinder.DefaultIdentity` prefixes the connection type).
- **Two seams added, both `internal` (Mcp already has `InternalsVisibleTo`), so nothing public moved:** `CreateTransportFinders(wifi, serial)` and `DiscoverAcrossTransportsAsync(finders, timeout)`. The latter owns/disposes the finders it is given (`AllTransportsDeviceFinder` only disposes ones it created itself) and answers the empty-finder-list case itself — the aggregator's ctor throws on an empty list, and "both transports off" is a legitimate call.
