## 2026-08-12 — Fire: #509 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #500 (MCP SD-card data tools) → PR #511, Qodo-clean in 4 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#509** only (`1705eb8`); #510 was merged by the user at 22:15Z. #509: CI `build` SUCCESS, 3 review threads all resolved, ready-noted at 22:26Z with only Qodo's agreeing reply after it. Priorities 1-3 vacuous → priority 4, **1/5 against the cap**.
- **Ticket choice: #500** (High, `enhancement`). The perf backlog above it was triaged out in earlier fires (#485/#489 need a user API decision, #490 item 1 needs a redesign, #487 is Windows-only). Of the new #498-#502 enhancements, **#498 was skipped on the fire's own rule — it requires promoting `StreamSamplesAsync`/`DroppedLiveSampleCount` onto `IStreamingDevice`, a public-interface change.** #500 needs no interface change at all, is bench-validatable non-destructively, and closes the only complete data loop MCP has.
- **What shipped:** `list_sd_files`, `get_sd_storage`, `download_sd_file` (raw file + parsed CSV, one call), `delete_sd_file`. Plus an internal `SdCardSampleSource` in `Daqifi.Mcp` adapting `SdCardLogSession` → `ISampleSource` for Core's `CsvExporter`, ported from the example app's bench-proven version (`/Users/tylerkron/projects/daqifi/daqifi-core-example-app/SdCardSampleSource.cs`) — including its load-bearing lesson that **a CSV row is a timestamp, not a sample**, so counting samples over-reports by the channel count.
- **Two real bugs found while wiring it, both fixed with proven catchers:**
  - **`SdCardDeviceConfiguration.FromDevice` folded the live `Channels` view** — the exact thing `DaqifiDevice.Channels` documents as unsafe off the consumer thread — and read it *twice*, so the analog and digital counts could disagree. Now one `GetChannelsSnapshot()`. `FromDevice_WhileStatusMessagesRepopulateChannels_DoesNotThrow` fails **2/2** with the snapshot reverted, in ~25 ms. This is the only Core change in the PR.
  - **`Path.ChangeExtension(localPath, ".csv")` names the file being read** when the log is itself a `.csv` (the firmware logs in CSV, and Core's temp file keeps the device-side extension) — the export would truncate its own input. Now `localPath + ".csv"`, which cannot collide with what it is appended to. `CsvSourceFile_IsNotOverwrittenByItsOwnExport` fails when reverted.
- **Deliberate design calls, all stated in the PR:** `download_sd_file` is **not** gated by `--read-only` (it changes nothing on the device; only `delete_sd_file` is gated); a CSV parse failure does **not** fail the tool call (the raw path comes back with `csvError`, because re-downloading to rediscover the same error is the worse outcome) — except a zero-sample log, which deletes the header-only stub and says so; a dropped-analog-column truncation keeps the CSV *and* reports it, since silence is the one unacceptable option.
- **Added a listing pre-flight to `download_sd_file`.** Asking the firmware for a nonexistent file produces no answer at all and Core gives up 20 s later advising a retry. Now checked against Core's cached listing (free when the caller listed first), re-listed once on a miss so a just-recorded file is never wrongly rejected, matched case-insensitively, and the **card's own spelling** is what goes to the firmware. Bench: **20 s stall → 2.1 s refusal naming the files that are there.**
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101` — driven as a real MCP server over stdio JSON-RPC** (python harness spawning the built `daqifi-mcp` and speaking initialize / tools/list / tools/call), not through the agent class. All 19 tools register. **Complete loop through tool calls:** configure analog [0] → rate 50 → `start_sd_logging` → 1.2 s → `stop` → `list_sd_files` (46→47, new `log_20260812_165615.bin` at 430 B) → `download_sd_file` → **430 B in 0.68 s, 43 samples, 43 CSV rows, reported count == the CSV's actual data lines**, timestamps exactly 20 ms apart — which is also the proof the live device's 42 MHz clock reached the parser (the 50 MHz fallback would have stretched them ~19%). Upper-cased name resolved. `get_sd_storage` 7.80 GB / 100% free.
- **Firmware finding worth keeping: the SD-buffer collapse is SIZE-dependent, and an SD recording does NOT re-arm it.** Files at 186 B / 186 B / 274 B served in ~0.68 s; 3.7 KB, 11.7 KB and a freshly recorded 3.7 KB file all returned marker-only empty transfers after 26 s — including on a **fresh reconnect** with no streaming in that session. So the memory note "an SD recording re-arms it without a power cycle" did not hold here: recording re-armed *small* transfers only. Both conditions surface as Core's typed exceptions, which is criterion 2.
- **The bench Nq1 is left in the transient SCPI-silence state and NEEDS A REPLUG before the next fire's bench work.** After the run of 26 s stalled transfers (each abandons the transport mid-switch — Core documents "reconnect before using the device again"), discovery went intermittent and then silent, and `connect_device` times out at the channel-configuration wait. **Confirmed device-side, not a regression:** the example CLI fails identically on the same port (`--discover-serial` found it, `--show-status` timed out at 8 s), and the same MCP binary had connected six times earlier in the session. A shell read and four discovery passes did not clear it; power-cycle/reboot are off-limits for the loop, and the user is away. **Consequence: the `--read-only` bench check did not run** — it is unit-tested only, and that path throws before any device I/O, so nothing was left unverified that touches hardware.
- **Qodo — 4 rounds.** R1: *orphaned CSV on export error* (VALID, `39ebfca`) — the file is created before the export and `DownloadSdFileAsync` converts failures to `csvError` with `CsvPath=null`, so a half-written file was litter nobody could name; whole block now wrapped in an untyped catch that deletes and rethrows, and I removed the `SampleCount==0` branch's own `TryDelete` so `EmptyLog_ReportsZeroSamplesAndLeavesNoCsvBehind` **only** passes because of the new catch (proven by `catch when (false)`). Declined the `.tmp`+`File.Move` suggestion — the destination is already a unique temp path nothing observes until the method returns. R1 also: *unsanitized filename in error* (VALID, and **a regression I introduced**) — my pre-flight runs ahead of Core's SCPI-safety check, so control characters produced a multi-line "no such file"; `RequireFileName` now rejects `char.IsControl` plus `"` and `;`, with tests on both sides (`OrdinaryFileNames_AreNotCaughtByTheCharacterCheck` pins that spaces/dots/dashes still pass). R2: *sync CSV output stream* (VALID, `ceb98eb`) — output opened without `useAsync` while the input already had it. R3: *duplicated buffer constant* (`c4872f7`) — **my comment was the defect, not the constant**: it claimed the CSV write buffer tracked `SdCardParseOptions.BufferSize`. Declined the "derive it from the parse buffer" option (that coupling is the finding pointed the other way) and rewrote the doc to say they are independent. R4: **CLEAN** — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0), 0 unresolved threads (4 total, all resolved), 26 SHA refs to `c4872f7` vs 1 each to the older commits. **Settle re-check at +5.5 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), comment count unchanged, threads still 0.** CI SUCCESS; MERGEABLE / CLEAN.
- **Tests:** 40 new (33 `Daqifi.Mcp.Tests`, 7 `Daqifi.Core.Tests`) → Mcp 55 → 95. The reported CSV row count is checked against the lines Core's **real** exporter writes, not against the rule the counter was written from; the parse-and-export chain runs end to end against a synthetic on-disk log. Full suite green **net9.0** (2964 Core + 95 Mcp) and **net10.0** (2964) after every push, 0 warnings.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `eager-lederberg-814d4b` on `feat/mcp-sd-data-tools-500`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` at end of fire. Scratchpad harnesses (`mcpbench.py`, `mcploop.py`, `mcpfinal.py`, `mcpro.py`, `mcphealth.py`, `mcpsizes.py`) and backups removed.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: #511 verified clean (merged mid-fire) → priority 4, new ticket #490 (per-frame streaming allocations) → PR #512, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#511** only (`c4872f7`): CI SUCCESS, 4 review threads all resolved, ready-noted at 23:49:49Z with nothing after it. Priorities 1-3 vacuous → priority 4, 1/5 against the cap. **The user merged #511 mid-fire** (origin/main moved `9823530` → `23920e0` while I was implementing); caught it when creating the baseline worktree, rebased onto it before pushing, MERGEABLE throughout.
- **The bench Nq1 had recovered on its own — probe it, don't assume.** The orchestrator flagged it as needing a physical replug. A 5-second pyserial probe (`SYSTem:SYSInfoPB?`, 3 s read window) came back with **588 bytes of protobuf**, so the documented transient silence had cleared with no user action. Full bench validation was available and used. **Cost of the probe: seconds. Always probe before believing a stale "device is dead" note.**
- **Ticket choice: #490** (Medium, perf) — the top of the previous fire's own recommended order, and the only remaining candidate that is bench-*measurable*. Taken in **full**, not the "items 2+3 safe subset" earlier fires had scoped it down to, because item 1's blocker turned out to be solvable (below).
- **The issue's own fix for item 1 was unimplementable as written, and that is the interesting part.** It says to invalidate the cached channel array from `PopulateChannelsFromStatus` / `ChannelControlOperations`. But **`IChannel.IsEnabled` has a public setter** and `AnalogChannel`'s was a plain lock-guarded assignment — a caller writing `channel.IsEnabled = true` directly is legal, invisible to every device API, and would leave the decoder mapping frame values onto the previously active channels, filing AI1's reading under AI0, silently and forever. Fix: an **internal `IChannelEnablementNotifier`** implemented by `AnalogChannel`/`DigitalChannel`, raised only on a real value change and **outside the channel's lock**; `DaqifiDevice` subscribes to every channel it owns and bumps an internal `ChannelStateVersion`. That is the piece that makes the whole cache safe.
- **Ordering detail worth keeping: the decoder reads the version BEFORE taking the snapshot.** Read after, a change landing in between would stamp a pre-change snapshot with the post-change version — undetectable by any later frame. Read before, the worst case is one redundant rebuild.
- **What shipped, all three items:** (1) decoder caches the channel snapshot + the sorted active-analog array, rebuilt only on a version change; (2) `IMessageParser<T>` gained a **span overload as a default interface method** (non-breaking — existing parsers keep compiling), `ProtobufMessageParser`/`LineBasedMessageParser` implement it for real, the consumer bulk-appends via `AddRange(ReadOnlySpan<byte>)` and parses from `CollectionsMarshal.AsSpan`; (3) `ProtobufProtocolHandler.Handle(DaqifiOutMessage)` typed entry point kills the per-frame `GenericInboundMessage<object>`, and the `ProtobufMessage` wrapper is built only when `MessageReceived` has a subscriber.
- **The `MessageReceived` subscriber check alone would have been hollow — `DaqifiDevice` always subscribes.** So Core's three subscribers (device, `SerialDeviceFinder`, `TextExchangeEngine`) moved to a new **internal `MessageParsed`** event that carries no raw-buffer snapshot. That is what actually makes the per-read copy disappear in real use; the public event is unchanged for external consumers.
- **Measured, branch vs `origin/main`, same harness (16 analog + 16 digital), reproducible across 3 runs each:** decode **2312 → 1648 bytes/frame** (−664 B ≈ seven objects: the snapshot array, the intermediate list plus its growth steps, and the two wrappers), **1.79 → 1.35 µs/frame**, gen0 55 → 39 per 200k frames; consumer **1385 → 1310 bytes/frame** — and that −75 B is almost exactly the frame size, which is the proof the removed copy *was* the wire throughput. At 1 kHz the decode saving is **~2.4 GB/hour of gen0**, the order the issue estimated. Harness: a scratch console app + `git worktree add --detach` baseline, switched with `-p:DaqifiCoreProjectPath=`.
- **Qodo — 2 rounds, both round-1 findings valid and taken (`3fd4594`).** R1a *MessageParsed blocks MessageReceived*: an internal handler throwing withheld the message from an external subscriber; now isolated + `SafeRaiseError`. Deliberately did **not** wrap `MessageReceived` too — it has always propagated to `ProcessMessageBuffer`'s catch, and wrapping would be observably identical but hide it from an overriding subclass. R1b *version bumps every status*: real, **but the suggested fix would have introduced the exact bug the version exists to prevent** — comparing `(Type, ChannelNumber)` sequences reports "unchanged" for a set whose *instances* were replaced (the populator builds a fresh one whenever it cannot reuse), leaving the cache writing into channels the device no longer has. Implemented as a **reference-identity** membership check instead and said so in the reply. R2: **CLEAN** on `3fd4594` — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0), both findings struck, 0 unresolved threads, 16 SHA refs to the current head vs 1 to the old. **Settle re-check at +10 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), comment count unchanged, threads still 0.** CI SUCCESS; MERGEABLE.
- **Tests: 39 new** (2975 → 3014 Core), across `StreamFrameDecoderTests` (+7 cache tests), new `ChannelStateVersionTests`, `ChannelEnablementNotificationTests`, `StreamMessageConsumerBufferCopyTests`, `UndifferentiatedMessageRaiseTests`, and `ProtobufProtocolHandlerTests`. **Every fix has a proven catcher, verified by reverting it:** deleting the enablement notification fails **10**, the repopulation version bump fails 2, the unsubscribe-on-repopulation fails 2, the reference-membership check fails 1, the lazy buffer snapshot fails 1, the `MessageParsed` isolation fails 1. Full suite green net9.0 (3014 Core + 95 Mcp) and net10.0 (3014), 0 warnings, on both pushes.
- **Revert-to-prove gotcha:** warnings-as-errors makes `catch when (false)` and `if (false)` fail the build (CS8360 / CS0162). Use a runtime-opaque predicate — `catch (Exception ex) when (DateTime.UtcNow.Year < 2000)` — to neuter a catch block for a catcher check.
- **Bench (non-destructive), fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`, run twice — before the PR and again after the round-1 production fixes:** `--discover-serial` found `sn=9090539562006014104` (also exercises the changed `SerialDeviceFinder` subscription); 3 s @ 500 Hz on channels 0-2 → **1186 then 1185 samples**, three analog values per row (this unit's ~79% clock ratio); **channel masks 1 / 3 / 21 → 1 / 2 / 3 analog values per row with distinct per-channel values**, which is the cached channel map being rebuilt correctly per session on real hardware; `--sd-list` 47 files and `--sd-storage` 7.80 GB (the text-exchange path, whose line collection also moved to the new event). Discovery/status/streaming/SD queries only — no reboot, format, delete, `SD:GET`, firmware or LAN writes.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `eager-lederberg-814d4b` on `perf/streaming-decode-allocations-490`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-8348ec` at end of fire. Baseline `git worktree` and the `allocbench` harness removed.
- End-of-fire state: **1 open loop PR (#512)**, Qodo-clean on `3fd4594`, CI green, mergeable, ready-noted, **not merged** — 1/5 against the cap, **4 slots free**. Next fire: re-check #512, then take a new ticket. Remaining backlog: **#491** items 1+2 (item 3 needs a shared reader-thread design), **#502**, **#501/#499** (channel-model changes), **#480/#482/#483/#465/#464**; **#485/#489/#498** still want a user decision (see the 2026-08-12 triage above). Note #490's item-1 groundwork — a device-level `ChannelStateVersion` and per-channel enablement notification — is now available to anything else that wants to cache a derivation of the channel set.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: #512 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #481 (sample-rate cap lives only in MCP) → PR #513, Qodo-clean in 3 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **#512** only (head `3fd4594`, CI `build` SUCCESS, 2 threads resolved, ready-noted 00:56Z, nothing landed after). Priorities 1–3 vacuous → priority 4, 1/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice: #481.** No bug-labelled tickets remain unclaimed (#490 is #512's). Walked the rest: #485 (700 ms SCPI overhead) was scoped and **deferred** — its biggest term is the protobuf consumer's parked 500 ms blocking read, which cannot be interrupted without a redesign of the consumer's read loop, so its "<200 ms" success criterion is not reachable by a contained change; that is a multi-fire redesign, not one fire. #489 (SD parsers materialize the file) is a good next candidate but changes the configuration-scan semantics (bounded look-ahead vs. today's whole-file scan) — worth doing deliberately. #484 renames public constants (breaking, skipped). #481 was High priority, additive, and directly bench-measurable.
- **Design.** New `Daqifi.Core.Device.Capabilities.SampleRateCap`: `Compute` (pure arithmetic), `ComputeForDevice`, `Enforce`, `EnforceOn`. `IStreamingDevice` gains `MaximumStreamingFrequencyHz` and `EnforceStreamingFrequencyCap()` as **default interface members** (non-breaking, the pattern the interface already uses for `StartStreamingAsync`), with matching members on `DaqifiStreamingDevice` so both surfaces have them and the logic lives once. `Daqifi.Mcp.SampleRateCapCalculator` deleted (Mcp is `PackAsTool`, not a consumable library); MCP keeps only `ApplyServerRateClamp` for `--max-sample-rate-hz`.
- **Source precedence — the load-bearing decision.** The device's `CurrentMaximumRateHz` stays authoritative; the published rate model (which had **zero** production callers, the ticket's other complaint) is the fallback only. The bench numbers are the argument: the model sits **above** the device's own answer in every measured configuration (15714 vs 7746 at one channel; 5000 vs 3518 at sixteen), so preferring it would over-permit and taking `min()` of the two would contradict `CapabilityRateModel`'s own documented contract. The `StreamingFrequency` setter deliberately still validates against the board ceiling only — capping there would break "set the rate, then enable channels".
- **Tests: 27 new in Core + 4 left in MCP.** Proven to catch regressions by mutation: inverted precedence fails 5, counting disabled channels fails 4, no hardware-max floor fails 2, no write-back in `EnforceOn` fails 2, counting digital channels fails 1, id lookup without the kind check fails 1, negative reported cap treated as real fails 1. Two mutations had to be rewritten because `if (false)` trips CS0162 (warnings-as-errors) — use a value change, not a constant-false condition.
- **Bench (non-destructive), Nq1 fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`.** Custom harness in the scratchpad (the example CLI has no capability/cap flag), referencing the local Core csproj. Core's cap matched the device's `current_max_rate_hz` **exactly** across six selections: none→0, digital-only→0, {0}→7746, {0,1,2}→6260, {4,8,10,12,14}→8634, all 16→3518. The #447 trap reproduced end to end: 7746 Hz live, enable all 16, cap drops to 3518, `EnforceStreamingFrequencyCap()` lowers it and reports 7746, second call reports no change. Channels restored to none enabled.
- **Qodo: 3 rounds.**
  - **Round 1 (head `eb1e19f`) — 1 bug, valid.** "Empty set yields nonzero cap": with the device stating no cap, the model fallback answered 18333 Hz for an empty configuration (`110000/(6+0)` — the model's per-tick overhead term stays finite at zero channels), so every downstream `cap > 0` check would read an empty configuration as live. **Checked the premise on hardware before fixing**: the device answers 0 for nothing-enabled *and* for digital-only, 7746 as soon as one analog channel joins. Fixed in `66eade3` by returning 0 from the fallback when no analog input is enabled — scoped to the fallback path, **not** to `ComputeForDevice` as a whole, because a device with no capability document has stated nothing and returning 0 for it would newly refuse rates MCP accepts today (a behaviour change #481 forbids).
  - **Round 2 (head `66eade3`) — 2 bugs, 1 valid.** Valid: the board-ceiling fallback was documented as "no capability document at all" but also covers a document carrying neither a current cap nor a rate model (an existing test already pinned this) — reworded on both surfaces in `0218b60`. **Not taken:** "digital-only treated as none" — the measured device answer for digital-only *is* 0, this property already returned 0 for it on the authoritative path before the PR, and the finding pulls directly against round 1's, which demanded that same zero. Replied with the measurement table and resolved; the digital-on-the-analog-tick semantics are now stated in the docs rather than implicit.
  - **Round 3 (head `0218b60`) — Bugs (0)** across all six categories, all three findings struck `✓ Resolved`, 0 unresolved threads, 18 SHA references to the current head. Settle re-check at +5 min: summary byte-identical, comment count unchanged, threads still 0. CI `build` SUCCESS. Ready-noted.
- **Worth remembering:** when a Qodo finding turns on what the device actually does, measuring it on the bench is both faster and more conclusive than arguing the docs — it settled round 1 as valid and round 2's second finding as invalid, from the same four-line measurement.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: queue empty (#512/#513 both merged) → priority 4, new ticket #491 items 1+2 (idle device CPU) → PR #514, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; `gh pr list --state open --author @me` → **empty**: the user merged #512 and #513 (`origin/main` at `e0a5d1b`). Priorities 1-3 vacuous, 0/5 against the cap.
- **Ticket choice: #491, items 1 and 2 only — and this fire deliberately did NOT close the issue.** Item 3 ("no thread creation per SCPI exchange") is not an *idle* cost — it only fires when you send a command — and the fix is a rework of `StreamMessageConsumer`'s thread lifecycle, including the `ConsumerThreadNotExitedException` stale-reader grace and the `ReferenceEquals(staleThread, Thread.CurrentThread)` self-join guard, both of which are expressed in terms of a real `Thread` that exits. Pooling a worker there replaces `Thread.Join`/`IsAlive` with a completion handle across the most safety-critical concurrency class in the repo; not one fire's work alongside items 1+2. PR says "Addresses items 1 and 2", and a comment on #491 records the split so the next fire does not re-derive it.
- **Other candidates walked first:** **#489** (SD parsers materialize the file) still changes a public runtime contract (stream lifetime + re-enumerability) and wants the user's call — same reason as the 2026-08-12 triage, unchanged. **#502** (live acquisition statistics) is fully closable and bench-compelling but is a ~500-line port with API-design surface; noted as the strongest *feature* candidate for a later fire. **#483/#482/#484** are chores; #484 renames public constants (breaking).
- **Item 1 — the 100 ms poll was never load-bearing, and that had to be argued before it could be deleted.** The loop's order is `Wait → Reset → drain`, not `Wait → drain → Reset`, and that ordering is what makes the timeout redundant: a `Set()` landing after the `Reset()` is picked up by the drain that immediately follows, and one landing after the drain leaves the sticky event set so the next wait returns at once. Both stop paths clear `_isRunning` *then* `Set()`, and the `while` re-reads the flag after the iteration, so a set consumed by the `Reset()` still exits on the next pass. `_messageAvailable.Wait()` unbounded; idle wakeups go from 10/s/device to **0**.
- **Item 2 — the two presence sources are OR-ed, so reordering them cannot change the answer.** `File.Exists` first for device-node paths means macOS/Linux never run `SerialPort.GetPortNames()` at all while the port is there. When it does run (Windows) it comes from a new `SerialPortNameSnapshot` shared by every polling transport.
- **The snapshot's freshness rule is the load-bearing design decision, and the first draft had it wrong.** A plain time-based cache introduces a hazard the original never had: `StartDropDetection` arms presence polling by asking this probe *at connect time*, so a cached "no" that predates a just-plugged device would silently disable drop detection for the life of that connection (and during polling would tear down a healthy one). Fixed by making staleness one-directional — **a "yes" may be up to 1 s old, a "no" is always taken now**: a cached miss falls through to a fresh enumeration. Cost is bounded and stated in the docs (worst-case unplug detection ~3 s → ~4 s); the perf goal survives because the steady state is all-positive.
- **Tests: 21 new** (3046 → 3068 Core), in `MessageProducerIdleWakeupTests` (8) and `SerialPortNameSnapshotTests` (13). **Every fix has a proven catcher, verified by mutation:** restoring `Wait(100)` fails 2, restoring the enumeration-first ordering fails 1, serving cached negatives fails 3. Full suite green **net9.0** (3068 Core + 86 Mcp) and **net10.0** (3068), 0 warnings, on both pushes.
- **Mutation gotcha, again:** warnings-as-errors makes a bare early `return` before live code fail with **CS0162 Unreachable code detected**. Wrapping it as `if (true) { return ...; }` compiles and neuters the branch — cheaper than the `DateTime.UtcNow.Year < 2000` trick when you just want to short-circuit rather than kill a catch.
- **Bench (non-destructive), Nq1 fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`.** Harness in the scratchpad (`SerialStreamTransport.Connect()` + a started `MessageProducer`, 30 s fully idle, reporting `TotalProcessorTime` and `GC.GetTotalAllocatedBytes`), alternated against a detached `origin/main` worktree via `-p:DaqifiCoreProjectPath=`. **CPU 230.1 / 254.6 / 257.2 ms → 93.0 / 78.6 / 86.1 ms** over 30 s (7.7-8.6 → 2.6-2.9 ms/s), **allocations 68.5 KiB → 0.0 KiB**, `transport.IsConnected` still true at the end of every run (i.e. the cache did not cause a false drop). One `GetPortNames()` measures **0.11 ms** on this host. Re-run after the round-1 fix: 87.0 ms / 0 KiB. CLI health checks each time: `--discover-serial` → `sn=9090539562006014104` fw 3.7.2, 3 s @ 500 Hz on channels 0-2 → **1186/1187** samples (this unit's ~79 % clock ratio), `--sd-storage` 7.80 GB, clean `Disconnected`. No reboot/format/delete/`SD:GET`/firmware/LAN writes; serial only ⇒ zero WiFi connect-churn exposure.
- **Honest bench limitation, stated in the PR too:** on macOS the device-node check answers first, so the *shared snapshot* itself is never exercised on this host. The bench proves item 1 and the reordering; the sharing and the freshness rule are covered by unit tests only.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.**
  - **Round 1 (head `aa9f9c4`) — 1 bug, valid.** `WakeCount` was documented "since `Start()`" but nothing resets it. **Took the other branch of the suggested fix and said why:** `MessageProducer` has no stale-thread guard, so a `Stop()` whose 1 s `Join` times out leaves the old background thread alive, and `Start()` sets `_isRunning` back to true — a thread that thread can observe and keep looping on. Zeroing the counter in `Start()` would hand back a number a second thread is concurrently incrementing. Documented as cumulative-over-the-instance instead (`10a4218`), with `WakeCount_IsCumulativeAcrossRestarts` pinning it. Replied on the inline thread and resolved it.
  - **Round 2 (head `10a4218`) — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0)**, the finding struck `✓ Resolved`, 0 unresolved threads, 4 SHA refs to the current head vs 1 to the old. **Settle re-check at +5 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), comment count unchanged at 5, threads still resolved, head unchanged.** CI `build` pass; MERGEABLE / CLEAN. Ready-noted.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `github-issue-450-14db16` on `perf/idle-device-cpu-491`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-2a95bf` at end of fire. Baseline worktree and the `idlebench` harness removed.
- End-of-fire state: **1 open loop PR (#514)**, Qodo-clean on `10a4218`, CI green, mergeable, ready-noted, **not merged** — 1/5 against the cap, 4 slots free. **#491 stays open for item 3.** Next fire: re-check #514, then take the next ticket — **#502** (live acquisition statistics) is the strongest remaining candidate, then #482/#483; #485/#489/#498 still want a user decision, and #491 item 3 is a `StreamMessageConsumer` thread-lifecycle rework worth its own fire.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: #514 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #487 (per-COM-port WMI queries) → PR #515, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; one open PR (#514), already Qodo-clean on `10a4218`, CI green, ready-noted, 0 unresolved threads — priorities 1-3 vacuous, 1/5 against the cap. Straight to priority 4.
- **Ticket choice: #487.** A perf *bug*, small, and the fix has a template in-repo (the macOS provider's TTL-cached `ioreg` map). Walked past **#502** (live acquisition statistics — still the strongest feature candidate, ~500 lines with API-design surface) because a bug outranks a feature and #487 is one fire's work.
- **The change.** `WindowsPnpPortMap` runs one `SELECT DeviceID, Caption FROM Win32_PnPEntity WHERE PNPClass = 'Ports'` per pass, builds port → device-instance-id from the captions, and caches it 2 s. Both Windows providers read it. `PnpDeviceIdParser` takes the VID/PID regex out of `WindowsUsbPortDescriptorProvider` so the selection logic is testable off Windows — same pattern as `HidDevicePathParser`/`LocationParentWalker`, and the same reason.
- **The two callers needed different staleness rules, and that is the load-bearing decision.** For the descriptor provider a miss just means "probe the port", so it takes the cached answer — refreshing on miss there would reinstate a per-port query on any machine with a COM port that has no `PNPClass='Ports'` entity, i.e. exactly the bug. For the location provider a miss is a real answer (no location key) *and* it runs for a port that just probed as a DAQiFi device, so it passes `refreshOnMiss: true`. Inverse of #514's `SerialPortNameSnapshot`, where the negative was the dangerous direction for every caller.
- **Equivalence with the old `LIKE '%(COMn)%'` predicate was argued, not assumed.** Matching `\((COM\d+)\)` including the parens is what keeps `(COM9)` from matching `(COM90)`, exactly as the LIKE literal did; a port keeps a *list* of ids because the LIKE matched a caption substring, and the descriptor provider took the first id carrying a VID/PID while the location provider took the first id full stop.
- **Tests: 27 new** (21 in `WindowsPnpPortMapTests`, 6 parser cases appended to `UsbPortDescriptorTests`), all cross-platform because the WMI call sits behind an injected query. **Seven mutations, all caught:** no caching fails 6, ignoring `refreshOnMiss` fails 2, always refreshing on miss fails 1, publishing the map before the query returns fails 1, inspecting only the first device id fails 1, case-sensitive port keys fail 1, overwrite-instead-of-append fails 1. Full suite green **net9.0** (3071 Core + 86 Mcp) and **net10.0** (3071), 0 warnings, on both pushes.
- **Stated the limitation instead of papering over it:** the WMI path itself is exercised nowhere — no Windows machine here, CI is `ubuntu-latest` — the same caveat `docs/DEVICE_INTERFACES.md` already carries for `WindowsUsbLocationProvider`.
- **Bench (non-destructive), Nq1 fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`** — no-regression check only, since macOS never constructs these providers: `--discover-serial` → `sn=9090539562006014104` fw 3.7.2, 3 s @ 500 Hz on channels 0-2 → **1186** samples (the unit's ~79 % clock ratio), `--sd-storage` 7.80 GB, clean disconnect. Re-run after the round-1 fix changed production code: same numbers. No reboot/format/delete/`SD:GET`/firmware/LAN writes.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.**
  - **Round 1 (head `8779e5a`) — 1 bug, valid.** `GetDeviceIds` handed back the `List<string>` stored in the shared map, so a caller could cast it back and edit what every concurrent lookup sees. Took the fix but not Qodo's suggested `ToArray()` — an array is still element-assignable through the same cast; `AsReadOnly()` wraps the list and the only reference to the list goes out of scope with the build. `WhatALookupReturns_CannotBeMutatedByItsCaller` pins it (proven catcher: publishing the raw lists fails it; note a `ReadOnlyCollection` *subclass* is not a valid mutation — it still throws).
  - **Round 2 (head `5937454`) — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0)**, finding struck resolved, 0 unresolved threads, 8 SHA refs to the head vs 1 to the old. **Settle re-check ~10 min later: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), threads still 0, head unchanged.**
- **CI went red on the new head — and it was NOT mine, and NOT a one-rerun flake.** `RawCapture_UnderASendHammer_ReadsThePayloadWithNothingInterleaved` failed twice (net10.0, then net9.0 — different frameworks, same commit), passed on the third run untouched. **Root cause found rather than shrugged at:** the deferred-send backlog is capped at 1024 and overflows **drop-oldest**, and the test ends by waiting for `HAMMER-0` — the oldest, hence the first evicted. It holds only while the capture window is short enough to park < 1024 messages (~130 ms locally, 6-12 s on a loaded runner). Filed as **#516** with three concrete fixes; a good next-fire ticket. **Lesson: two consecutive "flake" failures deserve the mechanism, not a third rerun — the mechanism is what tells you it is not yours.**
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `github-issue-450-14db16` on `perf/windows-wmi-batch-487`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-2a95bf` at end of fire. Bench lock acquired/released twice, both times cleanly.
- End-of-fire state: **2 open loop PRs (#514, #515)**, both Qodo-clean, CI green, mergeable, ready-noted, **not merged** — 2/5 against the cap, 3 slots free. Next fire: re-check both, then take the next ticket — **#516** (the flake just filed, small and self-contained) or **#502** (live acquisition statistics); #491 still open for item 3; #485/#489/#498 still want a user decision.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: #514/#515 verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #516 (RawCapture hammer flake) → PR #517, Qodo-clean in 1 round

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --prune`; two open PRs (#514 on `10a4218`, #515 on `5937454`), both already Qodo-clean (0 unresolved `qodo-code-review` threads, the one finding on each struck resolved), CI `build` SUCCESS, MERGEABLE/CLEAN, both already ready-noted. Priorities 1-3 vacuous; 2/5 against the cap. Straight to priority 4.
- **Ticket choice: #516** — the flake filed by the previous fire. A test defect, self-contained, and the mechanism was already diagnosed, so the fire's value was in *proving* the diagnosis rather than guessing at it. (No other candidate was walked: #516 is a bug and the log already nominated it.)
- **The diagnosis was reproduced before anything was changed, and that is the load-bearing step.** Restored the `origin/main` version of the test and simulated a loaded runner the way the runner actually behaves — by serving the capture payload **one byte per `Read`** instead of 64, which stretches the window from ~130 ms to ~10 s. The old test failed with the *exact* CI message, `'HAMMER-0' never reached the wire.` Mechanism confirmed: the hammer parks > `DefaultMaxDeferredSends` (1024), the backlog overflows **drop-oldest**, and `HAMMER-0` is by definition the first eviction.
- **The fix bounds the hammer, and derives the bound from the cap rather than picking a number.** `HammerSendBudget = DaqifiDevice.DefaultMaxDeferredSends / 8` (=128) — the *relationship* is what has to hold, so hard-coding 128 would just re-arm the same drift if the cap ever moves. 128 also spans several `MaxDeferredSendsPerFlush` (64) replay batches, so the multi-flush handoff still gets exercised.
- **Bounding the hammer alone would have weakened the test, so the coupling was inverted.** The capture now holds the stream until the hammer finishes (`while (received.Length < payload.Length || !hammerFinished.Task.IsCompleted)`), which makes "every hammer send was issued while the capture owned the device" true **by construction** instead of by hoping the window outlasts the sender. The old test's `Assert.True(hammered > 0)` counted *total* sends — satisfied even by sends made after the capture ended — so it never actually pinned that. New assertion is `sentIntoTheOpenWindow == HammerSendBudget`. `hammerFinished` is signalled from a `finally` so a throwing hammer surfaces as its own failure rather than hanging the capture for the 15 s `DeadlockBudget`.
- **Added `Assert.Equal(0, device.DroppedDeferredSendCount)` ahead of the `HAMMER-0` wait** — a failure-message fix as much as an assertion: if the budget and the cap ever drift apart the test now says "112 dropped" instead of the unrelated-looking "HAMMER-0 never reached the wire" that cost the previous fire a CI investigation.
- **Three mutations, all proven catchers:** (1) old test + 1-byte reads → fails with the CI message, **new test + same 1-byte reads passes 3/3** (this is the direct proof of success criterion 1); (2) `MaxDeferredSends => 16` on the test device → new dropped-count assertion fires, `Expected: 0 / Actual: 112`; (3) `TryDeferSend` short-circuited to `return false` → still fails, now catching **all 128** sends during the capture rather than a timing-dependent subset (success criterion 2).
- **Mutation gotcha, third time:** `if (true) { return false; }` in `TryDeferSend` hit **CS0162 Unreachable code detected** under warnings-as-errors — the compiler constant-folds `if(true)`. `if (DateTime.UtcNow.Year > 2000) { return false; }` is opaque enough to compile. Prefer the date trick over `if (true)` when the short-circuit sits above live code.
- **No production code changed** — the diff is one test file, +42/-11.
- **Tests:** full suite green **net9.0** (3045 Core + 86 Mcp) and **net10.0** (3045), 0 warnings. Target test run 6/6 clean before the mutations, and again after.
- **Bench (non-destructive), Nq1 fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`** — health check only, since a test-only diff cannot affect the board: `--discover-serial` → `sn=9090539562006014104` fw 3.7.2, 3 s @ 500 Hz on channels 0-2 → **1186** samples (the unit's ~79 % clock ratio, matching the last two fires exactly), `--sd-storage` 7.80 GB, clean disconnect. No reboot/format/delete/`SD:GET`/firmware/LAN writes; serial only. Bench lock acquired and released once, cleanly.

Priorities 1-3 vacuous; 3/5 against the cap. Straight to priority 4. (**#517 was merged by the user mid-fire**, 17:16Z, closing #516.)
- **Ticket choice: #502**, the candidate the last four fires nominated. **#489 was NOT re-litigated** — it still changes a public runtime contract (stream lifetime + re-enumerability) and wants the user's call, exactly as triaged on 2026-08-12; #485 stays deferred; #484 is breaking.
- **The design is additive to the point of being inert: three new files, no existing production file touched.** The aggregator observes the per-channel `SampleReceived` events the decode pipeline already raises — the same seam `LiveSampleStream` uses — so "no measurable decode-path cost when detached" is true by construction rather than by measurement, and the claim in the PR is the diff itself.
- **Two rates per channel, deliberately.** `MeasuredSampleRateHz` (host wall clock) and `DeviceClockSampleRateHz` (the device's own timestamps). Either one alone detects lost frames; only their disagreement detects a device clock that is not keeping real time. That is what made the bench run a demonstration rather than a smoke test.
- **Three desktop `SummaryLogger` defects fixed in the port** (per [[project_desktop_thin_wrapper]]'s "fixing latent bugs"): value extremes were never seeded from the first sample (`buffer.MinValue = buffer.MinValue`, so a channel at 4.5 V reported a minimum of 0), means divided by a configured window size rather than the samples actually seen, and the window swapped automatically every N samples instead of on demand. Core's version seeds, divides by the real count, and runs until `Reset()`.
- **Bench (non-destructive), Nq1 fw 3.7.2 on `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`** — the feature reproduces the two known firmware defects from its own numbers. At 1 kHz / 3 s: device clock **1000.03 Hz**, host measured **794.4 Hz** (the 79.4 % ratio of fw#716); latency climbing to **617 ms**, which is exactly 3 s × (1 − 794/1000) = 618 ms of accumulated drift — internally consistent, not a coincidence. Alternating 0 ms / 2 ms intervals are fw#717's duplicate timestamps surfacing as jitter. 500 Hz run and a mid-stream `Reset()` behaved identically. Bench lock acquired/released **four** times (once per code change), all cleanly. Serial only; no reboot/format/delete/`SD:GET`/firmware/LAN writes.
- **Qodo: 4 rounds, and rounds 1-3 each found a real defect — all three the same underlying blind spot.** `TimestampProcessor` deliberately reconstructs an *earlier* time when it decides a rollover was a false positive (out-of-order frame), so `DataSample.Timestamp` is not monotonic. This is worth remembering: **anything that differences consecutive `DataSample.Timestamp` values has to handle a backwards step.**
  - **Round 1 (head `6918327`) — 1 Bug, valid.** Negative intervals could land in the jitter bounds, and a backwards step near the end of a window could drop `DeviceClockSampleRateHz` to zero. Fixed in `02664af`: span measured between the earliest and latest timestamps *seen* rather than first-and-last *recorded* (identical for a monotonic stream, which is why no existing test moved), backwards steps excluded from the bounds the way `TimestampGapDetector` already treats non-positive deltas, and a new per-channel `OutOfOrderSampleCount` reports rather than hides it. **Zero-length intervals are deliberately kept** — fw#717's duplicate timestamps are a true statement about the device clock.
  - **Round 2 (head `02664af`) — 1 Bug, valid, and the more damaging half.** Excluding the backwards step was not enough: the *next* sample was still measured against the rewound timestamp, so the stream resuming showed up as a gap the size of the whole rewind (a multi-second "worst gap" that never happened — worse than a negative minimum because it looks plausible). Fixed in `747cb6f` by measuring intervals against the channel's high-water mark; that also *removed* a field rather than adding one.
  - **Round 3 (head `747cb6f`) — 1 Bug (maintainability), valid.** Moving the interval reference also moved what "out of order" means (behind the high-water mark, not behind the previous sample), and the XML docs still described the old rule. Docs-only fix in `0f5afd5`; `git diff` over the two files contains no executable line.
  - **Round 4 (head `0f5afd5`) — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0)**, all 3 inline threads resolved, SHA reference matches head. **Settle re-check at +4.3 min: summary byte-identical (`diff -q`), comment count unchanged at 9, threads still 0.** CI `build` SUCCESS; MERGEABLE / CLEAN. Ready-noted.
- **Seven mutations, all proven catchers:** zero-seeded extremes (6 tests fail), rate denominator `n` instead of `n-1` (3), missed unsubscribe on channel repopulation (1), removed post-dispose guard (1), negative interval folded into the bounds (1), device-clock span from first-and-last (1), interval measured from the last recorded sample (2). **Mutation gotcha:** `state.MinValue = state.MinValue` (the literal desktop bug) does not compile under warnings-as-errors — CS1717 — and removing the `OutOfOrderCount` increment trips CS0649. Assign a constant instead.
- **Allocation is asserted, not asserted-ish:** `Record` after the first sample per channel allocates **exactly 0 bytes** over 10,000 calls (`GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread`). The decode-path test compares an attached aggregator against an **inert subscriber**, not against no subscriber — the per-sample `SampleReceivedEventArgs` is only allocated when something is listening, so "no subscriber" is not an honest baseline for "does attaching cost anything".
- **Tests:** 27 new; full suite green **net9.0** (3070 Core + 86 Mcp) and **net10.0** (3070), 0 warnings, at every push. README gained a capability row and a recipe.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `github-issue-450-14db16` on `feat/acquisition-statistics-502`, restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-2a95bf` at end of fire. Bench harness lived in the scratchpad (a throwaway console app referencing the local Core csproj) — the example CLI has no flag for this API, and adding one belongs to the example-app repo, not here.
- End-of-fire state: **3 open loop PRs (#514, #515, #518)**, all Qodo-clean, CI green, mergeable, ready-noted, **not merged** — 3/5 against the cap, 2 slots free. Next fire: re-check all three, then take the next ticket — the strongest remaining are the chores **#482** (49 hand-rolled connected-guards → one helper) and **#483** (bootloader wire constants declared twice); then **#501/#499** (channel-model changes) and **#465/#464/#480** (test/decomposition). **#491** stays open for item 3; **#485/#489/#498** still want a user decision; **#484** is breaking.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: implemented #489 (SD parsers stream instead of materializing) → PR #520
- Live state re-derived from `gh` after `git fetch --all --prune`. Open loop PRs at start: **#514, #515, #518, #519** — 4 of the 5-PR cap, all four already Qodo-clean (0 unresolved `reviewThreads` on each current head), CI `build` SUCCESS, and each already carrying its ready-for-review note. Priorities 1-3 were therefore **empty**, so this fire went to priority 4.
- Backlog triage: no bug-labelled tickets remain unclaimed. #484 renames public constants (breaking, skipped). #485 was already scoped and deferred by an earlier fire (multi-fire redesign). #480/#344 are large decompositions; #499 (DAC) writes voltages on a bench whose outputs are loopback-wired; #501 is a bigger feature. **#489 was the High-priority, contained, directly measurable one** and was flagged by an earlier fire as the good next candidate.
- **The hard part was not the streaming, it was the contract.** `ParseFileAsync` (both the parsers' and the factory's) opened a `FileStream` inside an `await using` and returned the session **after closing it** — which only worked because everything was materialized first. And the example CLI + MCP both call `ParseWithFormatAsync(stream, …)`, so `Samples` had to stay **re-enumerable**, which ruled out the obvious "single-use enumerator with a look-ahead buffer" design.
- **Design.** New internal `SdCardParseSource` (rewindable source: a path re-opens its own `FileStream` per read; a caller-supplied seekable stream rewinds to its captured origin) + `SdCardTextLineReader` (streams non-blank lines, carrying the byte position). Each parser now reads a **bounded prefix** for the configuration, then hands the session an iterator that re-reads the log lazily. `SdCardParseOptions.ConfigurationScanMessageLimit` (default 512, 0 = whole file) bounds the protobuf config scan — that bound is the semantic change, and it is what keeps time-to-first-sample flat. **Forward-only streams keep the old decode-up-front behaviour** rather than failing.
- Incidental fix found by a new test: the fallback timestamp anchor was `DateTime.UtcNow` read *inside* the iterator, so enumerating the same session twice produced different timestamps. Captured once at parse time now.
- Tests: **+20 new** (`SdCardStreamingParseTests`). The 408 pre-existing SD card tests pass **UNCHANGED** — that is the equivalence check, not an afterthought. FULL suite green **net9 + net10** (3064 passed / 2 skipped each, +86 Mcp), 0 warnings.
- **Measured (30 MB `.bin`, this branch vs a detached `origin/main` worktree, same harness):** peak RSS **988 MB → 64 MB**, first sample **1101 ms → 21 ms**, full parse 1355 ms → 543 ms, identical 715,967 samples. A **300 MB** log parses in 65 MB RSS / 3.2 s (main would need ~9 GB). 10 MB CSV: 108 → 61 MB. 12 MB JSON: 112 → 64 MB. CSV export byte-identical in every case.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, non-destructive).** `SD:GET` on this unit returns **empty transfers** again (firmware #703) — a `--sd-log-start` re-arm attempt did NOT clear it this time, and note that the re-arm I ran had `--channels 3 --rate 200`, i.e. it started a live stream, which is precisely what collapses the SD buffer. **Next fire: re-arm with SD logging and NO stream flags.** Worked around it with `--sd-capture-parse`, which captures real firmware protobuf off the wire to a file and parses it: 25,379 bytes / 1,269 samples at 200 Hz, exported to CSV through the old and the new parser — **byte-identical, 44,458 bytes**. Re-run again after the Qodo fixes: still identical.
- **CLI gotchas:** the connection flag is `--serial <port>`, not `--port` (`--port` is the TCP port and errors with "Invalid integer for --port"). `timeout(1)` does not exist on this Mac.
- Test-data gotcha worth remembering: SD CSV/JSON timestamps are **uint32**; a generated file with a large tick step silently overflows past ~4.29e9 and the parser then rejects ~90% of the rows. Both parsers agreed exactly, which is how it was caught.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.** Round 1 = **3 findings, all valid**: (1) the new stream-lifetime requirement was undocumented on every public `Stream` overload; (2) two overlapping enumerations of a stream-backed session would race on the shared read position and silently interleave — now refused with an `InvalidOperationException` naming the way out, path-backed sessions unaffected; (3) CSV/JSON progress mixed a real `Stream.Length` total with a char-derived `BytesRead` and omitted the preamble, so it could never reach 100% — `BytesRead` is now the stream's real byte position. Round 2 = Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0), 0 unresolved threads, and a **settle re-check at +4 min** found the review comment byte-identical.
- Result: **PR #520** (base main, closes #489, "not merging — for review"), CI `build` SUCCESS, `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN`, ready note posted. Now at **5 open loop PRs = the cap**, so the next fire should shepherd only (#514, #515, #518, #519, #520) until the user merges one.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: #515/#522 re-verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #501 (engineering-unit scaling) → PR #523, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --all --prune`. Two open loop PRs — **#515 `5937454`** and **#522 `5516ba5`** — both **0 unresolved `qodo-code-review` threads** on their current heads, `build` SUCCESS, `mergeStateStatus` CLEAN, both already ready-noted. Priorities 1-3 **vacuous**; **2/5 against the cap, 3 slots free** → priority 4.
- **Backlog triage, and the candidate that was deliberately rejected.** Remaining eligible after #464 (claimed by #522) and #487 (claimed by #515): #465, #485, #491 item 3, #499, #501. **#485 was inspected first and passed over on purpose** — it is the only High-priority item left, but its four costs all live in `TextExchangeEngine`, whose own doc comment calls it "the most order-sensitive code in the device", and the two biggest (the protobuf consumer's `StopSafely` waiting out an in-flight blocking read, and the text consumer's stop) cannot be fixed without reworking `StreamMessageConsumer`'s thread lifecycle — the same rework #491 item 3 needs, which the user explicitly split out as "its own change". A partial slice would move ~50 ms of the ~700 ms and still miss the issue's own success criterion. **Left for a fire that can take the read-loop redesign whole; noted rather than half-done.** #465 was passed over as a second consecutive test-only PR when a real feature was available.
- **PICKED #501, Tier 1 only** (linear gain/offset + unit, no new dependency). Tier 2 (expression scaling) is a separate decision in the issue's own framing and was not touched.
- **The whole design question was how to add this WITHOUT an interface change**, since the loop skips breaking-API tickets. Three moves, all additive:
  - `IScaledChannel` is a **new capability interface** (`if (channel is IScaledChannel scaled)`) rather than members on `IChannel`/`IAnalogChannel` — those are public and implementable outside the library. Matches the existing `ILanChipInfoProvider` / `IPic32BootloaderDiagnostics` pattern.
  - `IDataSample`'s three new members (`Scaling`, `ScaledValue`, `Unit`) are **default interface implementations**, so an implementation written before #501 still compiles and reports "no scaling". `DataSampleTests.AnImplementationPredatingScaling_StillSatisfiesTheInterface` builds exactly such a type — **that test stops compiling if anyone ever makes those members abstract**, which is the real guard.
  - `Value` keeps its old meaning (the device's volts); the converted number is the new `ScaledValue`, which equals `Value` when nothing is configured. No existing consumer's numbers move — `DaqifiStreamingDeviceDecodeTests`' `GetScaledValue(1000) == ActiveSample.Value` assertions were left untouched and stayed green, which is the evidence.
- **The scaling travels ON the sample, not looked up from the channel** — that is what stops a reconfiguration from retroactively reinterpreting readings already taken, and it is why nothing is mutated in place (the desktop bug this replaces). Cost is one shared reference per sample, no per-sample allocation.
- **Non-obvious bits worth remembering:**
  - `AnalogChannel.Scaling` is the **one piece of channel state read without `_lock`** (`Volatile.Read`/`Write`). Justified and documented in-place: `ChannelScaling` is immutable and shares no invariant with the calibration fields, and the USB float decode path otherwise takes **no lock at all** per sample — putting one back would be a real regression on the hot path.
  - The decoder reads `Scaling` **per sample, not per cache rebuild**. Scaling changes do **not** bump `ChannelStateVersion` (which is what the cached channel array keys off), so caching it would leave a mid-stream reconfiguration invisible until something else happened to invalidate the cache. Pinned by `ScalingChangedMidStream_TakesEffectOnTheNextFrame_WithoutAChannelStateChange`, which asserts the version is unchanged.
  - `CapabilityChannelUnits` matches on **kind AND id** — the document numbers analog inputs and digital pins from 0 independently (the trap `SampleRateCap` already documents), so a `digital-io` entry with id 0 must not hand its unit to AI0. Test written for exactly that.
  - "Document states no unit" must leave `Scaling` **null**, not set a scaling with a null unit — a non-null scaling is what blocks the next refresh, so the latter would permanently claim the channel.
- **Tests: +54** (3135 → **3189**). New `ChannelScalingTests` (16) and `CapabilityChannelUnitsTests` (8); additions to `DataSampleTests`, `AnalogChannelTests`, `StreamFrameDecoderTests` (incl. a new `RawCountFrame` helper — the existing helpers only built pre-scaled-float frames, so the WiFi calibration branch had no frame builder) and `DaqifiDeviceCapabilityDocumentTests`. FULL suite green **net9.0 + net10.0** (3189 passed / 2 skipped each, +86 Mcp), 0 warnings.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive — streaming + SCPI only).** The example CLI does not touch scaling at all, so a CLI run would have exercised none of this; wrote a throwaway harness (`scratchpad/ScaleBench`, `ProjectReference` straight at the local Core csproj) with 13 assertions. All 16 analog channels took `Unit="V"` from the device's **own** capability document on connect, as an identity scaling; digital channels correctly left bare. **First run was nearly worthless and was redone** — AI0 sits at a flat 0.0000 V on this rig, so "ScaledValue == Value*gain+offset" held for a single constant. Added a 16-channel probe pass that picks the liveliest input (**AI1**, ~0.0085-0.0110 V; AI6/AI9 also non-zero, everything else dead flat) and re-ran: 1,097 samples @200 Hz, **max deviation 0.00E+00** against `Value*20+1`, volts still readable alongside (→ 1.1709-1.2197 PSI). Mid-stream gain change took effect on the next frame with **no channel-set change**; the 780 samples already taken still held the original scaling object; the neighbouring channel stayed on plain volts; a capability re-read did **not** clobber the configured conversion. Re-run on the final head after the doc fix: all 13 still pass.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.** Round 1 on `00f5280` — **1 valid finding**, and a fair one: the `ChannelScaling` remarks claimed `Apply` "never returns a non-finite value", which my own test contradicts (`Apply(NaN)` returns NaN). The real contract is that it never *introduces* a non-finite value and does not launder one either; remarks, `<returns>` and the test's comment now say both halves. Fixed in `034f095`, replied + resolved. Round 2 on `034f095` — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0), earlier finding struck through, 0 unresolved threads, SHA reference matching head. **Settle re-check at +4 min: review comment byte-identical (`diff -q`), still 0 unresolved, `build` SUCCESS, MERGEABLE/CLEAN.** Ready-noted.

Renamed to `feat/engineering-unit-scaling-501` and the stale remote branch deleted — check the branch name matches the ticket actually implemented before the first push.

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: #515/#522/#523 re-verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #498 (MCP live-data tools) → PR #524, Qodo-clean in 2 rounds

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --all --prune`. Three open loop PRs — **#515 `5937454`**, **#522 `5516ba5`**, **#523 `034f095`** — all **0 unresolved `qodo-code-review` threads**, `build` SUCCESS, MERGEABLE/CLEAN, all already ready-noted. Priorities 1-3 **vacuous**; **3/5 against the cap** → priority 4. (#518/#519/#520/#521 merged since the last fire; nothing left of #502's PR but its still-open issue.)
- **PICKED #498** (High, the MCP server's 15 tools could configure everything and measure nothing). Passed over: #499 (DAC — validating it means *driving* analog outputs into a rig with live loopbacks), #465 (test-only, and its scope is partly this PR's), #485 + #491 item 3 (still the `StreamMessageConsumer` read-loop redesign, still wants taking whole), #484 breaking, #480/#344 large refactors.
- **The issue asked for a breaking change and did not get one.** #498's How says "promote `StreamSamplesAsync`/`DroppedLiveSampleCount` to `IStreamingDevice`" — that interface is public and implementable outside the library, so abstract members on it break every external implementer. Shipped **`ILiveSampleSource`**, a capability interface, instead: `if (device is ILiveSampleSource live)`. Same benefit (a typed consumer reads live data without naming `DaqifiStreamingDevice`), additive, and it is the shape `ISdCardOperations` already has. Called out in the PR body so the deviation is the reviewer's to reject.
- **Two tools.** `read_channel_values` (latest value per enabled channel, returns as soon as every channel has reported) and `capture_samples` (rows: one per sample tick, one column per channel, `AI0`/`DIO3`, bounded by duration AND row budget). Both start the device's stream only if nothing is streaming and stop it again; both refused while `IsLoggingToSdCard` — a card recording routes data to the card, so a capture would wait out its window and return nothing.
- **Every state decision moved INSIDE `RunExclusiveAsync`** (stream ownership, the read-only refusal, the enabled-channel snapshot, the rate). The obvious shape — decide outside, then take the lock — lets another tool call land in between and makes each answer stale: the capture would stop a stream it did not own, or align its columns to a channel set that changed while it waited. Costs a slightly later error message; worth it.
- **`onSubscribed` ordering is load-bearing.** Core's live stream subscribes in the *synchronous prefix* of its iterator body, so `DrainAsync` kicks `MoveNextAsync()` and only then calls `StartStreaming` — otherwise the first frames are decoded with nothing listening. If starting throws, the in-flight read is cancelled and awaited before the enumerator is disposed: disposing an async iterator mid-`MoveNextAsync` throws `NotSupportedException` and would replace the real failure. Both pinned by tests.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive — streaming + SCPI only): 24/24**, via a throwaway harness (`scratchpad/LiveBench`) with a `ProjectReference` straight at the local `Daqifi.Mcp` csproj, driving `DaqifiAgent` end to end (discover → connect → configure → read/capture). **The issue's criterion — 4 channels @ 1 kHz × 10 s — returned 7861-7865 rows / ~31.4k samples, 0 dropped, and 0 rows missing** from the sequence the device's own clock accounts for (frame period 42000 ticks; gaps counted by walking consecutive device timestamps, which is how ">=99% of expected rows" can be checked without trusting the requested rate).
- **Two real defects the bench found, both fixed before the PR opened:**
  - **The floors were unusable.** A device that is not streaming yet sends **nothing for the first 85-110 ms** after the start command (six consecutive runs). `MinReadTimeoutMs` was 100 → a 100 ms budget reported a *healthy* device as silent, exactly #448's failure mode. Now **500 ms** (read) and **250 ms** (capture), both documented with the measurement.
  - **`MeasuredRateHz` was charging the device for that wait.** Now timed from the first sample to the last (`LiveCaptureOutcome.DataElapsed`), which moved the 10 s figure from 786 → 793.7 Hz and would have been a ~10% error on a 1 s capture. Also added **`DeviceClockRateHz`** (from the row timestamps) alongside it, following #502/#518's two-rate precedent: measured 793.6 Hz against a device clock insisting on 1000 Hz is fw #716 in the tool's own output.
- **Hardware fact worth keeping: the device's first frame after a stream starts can carry the digital port without the analog readings.** In a mixed capture that is one leading row with `null` analog columns (1 partial row in 1504). Reported as nulls rather than dropped; DTO docs say so.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.** Round 1 on `455c950` — **1 valid finding** (Medium): `rowLimitReached` was `rows.Count >= rowBudget` read *after* `Complete()`, and the final flush of the still-open row can reach the budget on a capture that ended on time — a caller would go back for a continuation that does not exist. Fixed in `f233681` by latching `RowBudgetFilled` inside `CloseRow(duringCapture:)` so the answer no longer depends on read order (rather than by reordering the reads and leaving a comment next to the trap); replied + resolved. Round 2 on `f233681` — Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Skill insights (0), finding struck through as Resolved, 0 unresolved threads, "updated up to the latest commit f233681", `build` SUCCESS.
- **Tests: +38** (Core 3135 → 3137, Mcp 86 → 124). Full suite green **net9.0 + net10.0**, 0 warnings. Bench re-run after the Qodo fix: still 24/24.
- **Flake seen twice, not identified:** the full-solution `dotnet test` (both TFMs in parallel) failed 1 test in `Daqifi.Core.Tests/net10.0` on two of ~six runs, and passed on every isolated net10 run and on the runs captured with a verbose logger — so the name never surfaced. Not in the new tests (the two added Core tests are deterministic). Watch for it in CI; CI on this branch is green.

Next fire: re-check all four, then **#465** (MCP contract tests — note #524 already added 38 of them for the live tools) or **#464 slice 2** (needs #522's doubles, so either wait for it to merge or branch from it), then **#499** (DAC — decide whether driving an analog output on the bench rig is acceptable before taking it). **#485 + #491 item 3 remain the honest big one.**

## 2026-08-13 — Fire: all four PRs re-verified clean → priority 4, new ticket #465 (MCP tool contract tests) → PR #525, Qodo-clean in 1 round

- **Live state re-derived.** `git fetch --all --prune`. Four open loop PRs — **#515 `5937454`**, **#522 `5516ba5`**, **#523 `034f095`**, **#524 `f233681`** — all **0 unresolved `qodo-code-review` threads**, `build` SUCCESS, MERGEABLE/CLEAN, all already ready-noted. Priorities 1-3 **vacuous**; **4/5 against the cap, 1 slot free** → priority 4. Re-verified all four again at end of fire: still clean.
- **#485 was inspected seriously and passed over for the fourth time — but this time the analysis was written down.** Posted as a triage comment on the issue itself so the next attempt starts ahead. Two findings worth keeping here:
  - **There is a fifth cost the issue does not list, and it is the biggest on the connect path.** The init exchange sends four commands that normally produce **no reply**, so it never reaches phase 2 and burns the full `responseTimeoutMs` — **1000 ms** — waiting for a first response that never comes. With its own three `Thread.Sleep(100)` that one exchange is ~1.7 s, not the ~700 ms the issue models. Criterion 2 (">=1 s off InitializeAsync") is mostly this single wait, and it is addressable by the issue's own terminator bullet without touching the consumer.
  - **Costs 1 and 4 are unfixable without the consumer read-loop rework**, confirmed by reading the code: both are a reader parked in a blocking `Stream.Read`, and `ReadTimeout` is read at call time, so lowering it mid-exchange does not shorten an in-flight read. The shape that removes them is a **single reader for the connection with an internal text mode** (flip parsing at a buffer boundary, the way `ClearBuffer` already marshals onto the reader thread) — which also kills cost 2 and closes #491 item 3. The blocker is that it is not a parser substitution (`StreamMessageConsumer<DaqifiOutMessage>` vs `<string>`) and it **invalidates the meaning of existing regression tests** (`DaqifiDeviceConsumerRestartTests` asserts the consumer is left stopped when a reader never exits — a state that cannot arise once it is never stopped). Re-deciding assertions that encode #383/#342/#396 is part of the work, not cleanup after it. Suggested split recorded on the issue.
- **PICKED #465** (MCP contract tests). Its acceptance criteria mentioning #447/#448 are already satisfied — both are closed — so what remained was the real scope: the tool layer had **no coverage of anything past the connected-device lookup**.
- **The whole problem was reachability, and the fix is a device double.** `FakeStreamingDevice` is a **real `DaqifiStreamingDevice`** connected without a transport: channels come from a status message, `Send` is overridden to capture SCPI, and `ReadCapabilityDocumentAsync` is overridden to re-issue a document whose `CurrentMaximumRateHz` is computed from the channels enabled *at the moment it is asked* — which is what real firmware does, and what makes the #447 re-validation testable. Deliberately not a hand-written `IStreamingDevice`: the bitmask arithmetic, the PWM-capable mask (0x00F9 → {0,3,4,5,6,7}) and `SampleRateCap` are Core's own and are exactly what a fake would get to re-specify and therefore never catch.
- **SD is re-implemented, not overridden.** Core's SD members are non-virtual forwards to an internal collaborator, so the double **re-implements `ISdCardOperations` explicitly** (C# lets a derived class re-map an interface; unlisted members fall through to the inherited public ones). That is what unlocked testing `Rewrite` — the busy-card and empty-transfer translations — and the `start_sd_logging` over-cap backstop.
- **One production change: `internal void DaqifiAgent.RegisterConnectedDevice(string, DaqifiDevice)`.** `_registry` is private and `ConnectAsync` requires a `_discovered` entry, so there was no other way in. Matches the file's existing `internal` + `InternalsVisibleTo` convention (`MinDiscoveryTimeoutMs`, `ApplyServerRateClamp`, `ExportCsvAsync`, …).
- **Mutation-tested rather than assumed.** Three deliberate mutations of `DaqifiAgent` (drop `EnforceSampleRateCap`; drive the DIO value before switching direction; drop `RequirePwmDisabled` from `SetDigitalDirectionAsync`) were applied and the suite re-run. The first two failed immediately; **the third did not**, which exposed a genuinely weak assertion: Core's own `EnsurePwmNotEnabled` message has since grown a mention of `disable_pwm`, so `Assert.Contains("disable_pwm")` passed with or without the MCP guard. Added `Assert.DoesNotContain("SetPwmEnabled", …)` — the property that actually distinguishes the two — and all three mutants then failed. Production file restored from a scratchpad copy (never `git checkout`, which would have reverted the real change).
- **Tests: +68** (Mcp 86 → **154**). Three new files: `DeviceDouble.cs` (harness), `ToolContractTests.cs` (introspection, analog/digital configuration, digital pins, PWM, sample rate, read-only), `SdToolContractTests.cs` (SD tools with a card attached + the `DaqifiTools` Guard/GuardAsync translation, which had no tests at all). FULL suite green **net9.0 + net10.0** (Core 3135 passed / 2 skipped each), 0 warnings.
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, USB, non-destructive — no streaming, no card writes, no pins driven, no PWM armed): 28/28.** Throwaway harness (`scratchpad/McpBench`, `ProjectReference` at the local `Daqifi.Mcp` csproj) drove the same tool calls against the board **to check the double is telling the truth** — which is the real risk of a fake-based suite. Confirmed on hardware: PWM-capable set really is {0,3,4,5,6,7}; widening 2 analog channels to 8 really does drop the ceiling **6924 → 4525 Hz** and lower the live rate with `sampleRateAdjustedFromHz` reporting 6924; narrowing leaves a fitting rate alone; disabling everything leaves the rate alone and `set_sample_rate` answers "No channels are enabled" rather than "exceeds the maximum"; `disable_pwm` is accepted on non-capable channel 1 and reports null duty/frequency; the card's listing (49 files) contains **a file with a size but no creation date** (`iso_A_1.bin`, 85 bytes) — the exact null case one of the new tests pins.

A bookkeeping comment already says so on the issue. **A local unpushed-looking branch (`feat/acquisition-statistics-502`, 4 commits) is the merged work, not abandoned work** — the loop's branches stay local after the remote is deleted on merge, so "a local branch exists with no open PR" means *merged*, not *unclaimed*.
- **HARNESS TRAP THAT COST REAL WORK, worth remembering: `cd /Users/tylerkron/projects/daqifi/daqifi-core` in a Bash call goes to the MAIN CHECKOUT, not this fire's worktree.** Bash resets cwd between calls, so every `cd`-to-repo-root command silently operated on the main checkout — which sits at a stale `7bc6161` — and the Edit/Write absolute paths followed it. The whole implementation was written into the main checkout and tested against a 7-months-stale base (2589 tests instead of 3285). Recovery: `git diff` the touched files to a patch, copy the new files out, `git checkout --` the main checkout back to clean, then redo in the worktree. `git apply --3way` onto the real base conflicted in 3 files (main had moved: `_channelPopulator.Populate`, `_host.EnsureConnected()`, `CapabilityChannelUnits.Apply`), so the edits were re-applied by hand. **Always `cd` to the worktree path, never to the repo root.**
- **Ticket choice: #499** (High, `enhancement`), the top remaining item once #502 was ruled out. Walked past #464 (test-only slices, smaller), #491 item 3 (`StreamMessageConsumer` thread-lifecycle rework — still its own fire), #485/#489 (want a user decision), #484 (breaking public renames), #344/#480 (large decompositions), #183/#271 (logged as ineligible).
- **The decisive finding, from reading the firmware rather than the issue: the protobuf path for DAC channels does not exist.** `analog_out_port_num` / `analog_out_res` / `analog_out_port_range` are declared in `DaqifiOutMessage.pb.h` but **never assigned** — the only references in `NanoPB_Encoder.c` are commented out. So `DeviceMetadata.cs:278`'s `if (message.AnalogOutPortNum > 0)` can never fire on real firmware. The **capability document is the only description** (`EmitAoutChannelJson`, SCPIInterface.c:4562 — `id`, `resolution_bits`, `ranges:[{min,max}]`), which is what fixed the design: populate from the document at `ReadCapabilityDocumentAsync`, and carry the channels across every `PopulateChannelsFromStatus` because a status frame says nothing about them.
- **Design notes worth keeping.** `AnalogOutputChannel` deliberately does **not** implement `IAnalogChannel` — every acquisition path selects with `is IAnalogChannel` or `Type == ChannelType.Analog` (ADC bitmask, stream decoder, sample-rate model) and a DAC belongs in none. `ChannelType.AnalogOutput` is a new member rather than `Analog` + `Direction.Output` for the same reason. Range validation applies **only** to channels the device actually described, so firmware that reports no DACs still writes by number exactly as before. Enabling an output for acquisition throws (it reaches neither mask, so it would have been a silent no-op). Three latent desktop `SummaryLogger`-style bugs were not in scope here, but the analogous care was taken: value extremes seeded from the first sample, means over samples actually seen.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.**
  - **Round 1 (head `cfe1485`) — 1 High bug, valid.** "Stale DAC channels persist": the carry-forward ran unconditionally, but `DeviceMetadata.UpdateFromProtobuf` **drops the capability document when the same instance reconnects to a different known board** (`DeviceMetadata.cs:207`, guarded to known→known transitions). The DAC channels that document described would have lingered as the new board's outputs and range-checked writes against the old board's limits. Fixed in `f0bf3a0` by gating the carry-forward on `Metadata.CapabilityDocument is not null` — correct because metadata is updated from the *same* status message before `PopulateChannelsFromStatus` runs (`DaqifiDevice.cs:3886` then `:3889`), so the swap is already visible.
  - **Round 2 (head `f0bf3a0`) — clean.** Bugs (0) / Rule violations (0) / Requirement gaps (0) / UX (0) / Cross-repo (0) / Skill insights (0), round-1 finding struck through as Resolved, 0 unresolved threads, review confirmed "updated up to the latest commit f0bf3a0". Re-checked after a 4.5-minute settle: unchanged.
- **Tests: 53 new** (`AnalogOutputChannelTests` 30, `DaqifiStreamingDeviceAnalogOutputTests` 21, 2 producer). FULL suite green **net9 (3285 Core + 192 Mcp) and net10 (3285)**, 0 warnings. **Six mutations confirm the tests bite**: dropped carry-forward (1 failure), removed range check (3), removed enable guard (1), latch leaving the staged value pending (4), unconditional collection rebuild (1), and reverting the round-1 fix (1).
- **BENCH (real Nq1, fw 3.7.2, `/dev/cu.usbmodem1101`, serial, non-destructive — connect / initialize / query / disconnect, no writes).** Ran before the PR and again after the round-1 fix, identical both times. **The bench unit is an NQ1 and the DAC is NQ3-only** (`DAC_EnsureHardwareInitialized` bails on `BoardVariant != 3`), so this validates the negative and error paths, not the happy path: the capability document lists **0 analog-output entries**, none are modelled, the existing **32 channels (16 analog-in + 16 digital) are untouched** through initialization and 1.5 s of live status frames, re-sync is idempotent, and `GetAnalogOutputAsync(0)` against real firmware returns `**ERROR: -200, "Execution error"` which Core surfaces as a clear `InvalidOperationException` rather than a parse failure. **The positive round-trip through `SOURce:VOLTage:LEVel?` is test-only and needs NQ3 hardware** — called out explicitly in the PR body so the user can judge it.
- **Worktree hygiene:** worked in `github-issue-450-14db16` on `feat/analog-output-channels-499` (branched from `origin/main` `dc6e9f9`), restored to `claude/daqifi-loop-2a95bf` at end of fire. Scratchpad harness `aobench` and the patch/backup files removed. The main checkout was left exactly as found (only its pre-existing `SESSION_LOG.md` modification).

Everything in that list was deliberately NOT rewritten one level down. What was actually unreached:
  - **`PrepareHexImage`'s empty-image guard** — nothing stopped an erase for an image with nothing to program.
  - **The `DescribeBootloaderSearch` diagnostic** — its four clauses, and critically `ResetTargetingState`, which only matters across two runs and is therefore near-unreachable through the facade.
  - **`RequestVersionAsync`'s `OrdinalIgnoreCase` "Error" check** — the stock fake only ever answers the literal `"Error"`, so casing was untestable until a `VersionDecoder` hook was added.
  - **Verify's two opposite verdicts** — a CRC *mismatch* must fail fast (not retried), a *malformed frame* must be retried as transport noise. The facade asserts neither the no-retry nor the `IOException`-wrapping-`InvalidDataException` shape.
  - **The empty-CRC-regions liveness fallback** — the facade's happy path runs through it but never asserts the substituted version exchange.
  - **`RecoverBootloaderHealthWithSoftResetAsync`'s two awkward corners** — the reset write itself failing (original failure must survive, via `ExceptionDispatchInfo`) and there being no handle to reset at all.
  - **Cleanup's other half** — the facade covers NotEligible for a *pre*-flash failure; a `JumpingToApp` failure is the *post*-verify clause and must not re-erase a good flash.
- **Zero production changes.** `git diff <branch-point> -- src/Daqifi.Core/ src/Daqifi.Mcp/` is empty. Pure test slice.

Also moved five HID/bootloader doubles + `CapturingProgress<T>` out of `FirmwareUpdateServiceTests` into the shared `FirmwareUpdateTestDoubles.cs` slice 1 created — **the facade file lost 284 lines and gained 0, and its 113 `[Fact]`/`[Theory]` count is unchanged** (verified against `origin/main`). Three defaulted-off hooks added to the doubles: `ConnectFailures`, `DisconnectFailure`, `VersionDecoder`.
- **Mutation-tested, 13 mutants, all caught.** Dropped empty-image guard (3 failures); case-folded target-path match; case-sensitive version `"Error"`; CRC mismatch made retryable; connect no longer releases a held handle; empty-CRC verify returns silently; poll count survives `ResetTargetingState`; stale enumeration error never cleared; `JumpingToApp` made cleanup-eligible; soft reset written with no handle; recovery surfacing the reset's write failure instead of the original; stop-streaming skipped; and (post-fix) the no-target match always missing.
  - **Gotcha: `if (false) { ... }` as a mutation can fail the build** (unreachable-code warning under warnings-as-errors) and the run silently produces no output. Use a contradiction like `if (!x && x)` instead.
- **Qodo: 2 rounds.**
  - **Round 1 (head `517e06f`) — 2 Medium findings, both with a real kernel, both acted on.** (1) *`FakeHidTransport` left `IsConnected` true after an injected connect failure* — latent (the session disconnects any held handle before every attempt, so production could not reach it) but the double is shared and `IsConnected` is exactly what the soft-reset recovery reads; fixed via a shared `ClearConnectionState()` and pinned by a new assertion.
